# Forest Run codebase implementation map

Status: in progress. Snapshot began 2026-10-03. This companion to [REPO_MEMORY_MAP](REPO_MEMORY_MAP.md) records implementation-level ownership and call/data flow. The current executable/source inventory contains 118,561 lines across production Kotlin, JVM/Robolectric tests, instrumentation, scripts, training-control code and workflows (computed from the current file inventory on 2026-10-03). The counts include the current untracked regression-test sources for MathUtils, RuntimeAssetValidator, RecoveryEvidenceDialogCoordinator, and ForestGardenHistory but exclude documentation and user-owned `ISSUE_NOTES.md`. This is a large repository review and must be continued by coverage area; an inventory or symbol list is not a claim that every line has already been reviewed.

Execution-status note: file-by-file summaries below were written during the source-reading pass and some retain their original “not run” wording. The current execution ledger below supersedes those historical phrases. Passing tests show only the exercised cases; this map does not claim exhaustive behavioral coverage.

Fresh continuation evidence (2026-10-03): `python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts -p 'test_*.py'` passed 835 tests. With Android Studio JBR Java 21, `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest lintDebug lintRelease` passed; JVM results were 1,285 tests with zero failures/errors/skips and lint reported 0 errors / 85 warnings for each variant. On the API 36.1 `Medium_Phone` AVD, the ordinary connected suite passed using the repository's required `notAnnotation=androidx.test.filters.LargeTest` argument: 19 tests, zero failures/errors/skips, including the release runtime-asset guard. A first plain connected-task invocation accidentally included the physical-only `@LargeTest` profiling harness; its first sustained profile failed while the emulator disconnected, so that run is not evidence of a product defect or physical performance. The correctly filtered ordinary suite passed. `git diff --check` passed. The emulator was shut down after the run.

Follow-up finding (2026-10-03): the lint `SetTextI18n` warning at the Forest Journal return action was a real localization/accessibility maintenance gap. The visible label and accessibility description now come from `strings.xml`, with a new connected regression test asserting both. The targeted journal instrumentation suite passed 3/3 on API 36.1; the full JVM suite, debug/release Kotlin compilation, Android test APK assembly, and both lint variants passed after the change. Lint is now 0 errors / 84 warnings per variant. A subsequent complete 20-test ordinary connected rerun was interrupted after 6 tests when kernel logs confirmed the host OOM killer terminated QEMU; the test report had one lifecycle case marked failed because the emulator disappeared. This is an incomplete environmental run, not evidence that the lifecycle assertion failed. The emulator was shut down to release memory.

Persistence repair finding (2026-10-03): `SaveIntegrityManager` repaired the legacy `best_distance` float but missed the newer `best_run_distance` float read by `SaveManager.loadBestDistance`. A wrong SharedPreferences value type survived startup repair and reproduced a `ClassCastException`. The repair now sanitizes that key using the existing finite/nonnegative float rule. The regression test first failed against the old code and then passed after the fix; the full JVM suite now passes 1,286 tests with zero failures/errors/skips, and debug/release lint remains at 0 errors / 84 warnings. `git diff --check` passed.

Typed-preference cross-check (2026-10-03): all literal and constant-key typed reads in `SaveManager` now map to `SaveIntegrityManager` repairs; dynamic `relationship_stage_*` keys are covered by its prefix scan. `GardenPurchaseManager`, ghost promotion, and outcome-maintenance adapters read the same repaired save namespace. The separate run-outcome recovery journal converts malformed typed values to a `Corrupt` result, and `FeedbackSettings` reads raw values defensively before repairing. No second missing repair key was identified in this pass.

Input pointer-order coverage (2026-10-03): inspection found `InputHandlerTest` covered taps, holds, swipe thresholds, cancellation, invalid coordinates, and seeded single-pointer traces, but not Android multi-pointer event ordering. Added synthetic MotionEvent tests for secondary-pointer movement/up while the primary gesture remains active, and primary-pointer release while another pointer remains. Both pass; the full JVM suite now passes 1,288 tests with zero failures/errors/skips, both lint variants remain at 0 errors / 84 warnings, and `git diff --check` passes. No production input defect was found in these sequences.

Game-thread continuation (2026-10-03): reviewed the complete frame loop, bounded stop/join behavior, exception reporting, latency completion only after a real Surface post, and related deterministic tests. The three focused lifecycle suites passed together. A subsequent full-suite attempt under the shell's Java 17 failed before affected Robolectric test bodies because Android SDK 36 requires Java 21; XML failures explicitly report the Java mismatch. Rerunning with `/opt/android-studio/jbr` (Java 21) passed all 1,288 JVM tests with zero failures/errors/skips. This confirms a toolchain requirement, not a product failure. `git diff --check` passed. No new production defect was found in this pass.

Supply-chain schema typing continuation (2026-10-03): Python considers `True == 1`; the evidence validator previously allowed a boolean as the CycloneDX BOM version, and its declared `entryCount` comparison had the same type ambiguity. Both fields now require exact `int` values, with regression tests. The focused validator suite passes 12 tests and the full script suite passes 837 tests. A source scan found schema-version fields in neighboring validators already use exact integer-type checks. `git diff --check` passes.

Resolved dependency report parsing (2026-10-03): `build_resolved_dependency_sbom.py` previously extracted Maven coordinates from a Gradle line ending in `FAILED` and could publish a failed resolution as though it were resolved. It now rejects any report line containing Gradle's standalone `FAILED` marker, including lines with a selected version after `->`; both forms have regression coverage. The report reader also now opens the checked regular file without following symlinks and compares device/inode/mode/size/timestamps before, during, and after reading; an adversarial path-swap test proves it rejects a symlink replacement between inspection and open. The full script suite passes 839 tests. An end-to-end run generated both dependency reports from Gradle (`releaseRuntimeClasspath` and `debugAndroidTestRuntimeClasspath`), built a 60-component SBOM and 12-entry declared inventory, and passed `validate_supply_chain_evidence.py` using the workflow's repository-relative report paths. The validator correctly rejected an earlier diagnostic attempt with absolute report paths. `git diff --check` passes.

Declared dependency source reading (2026-10-03): `build_declared_dependency_inventory.py` used `lstat()` followed by `read_bytes()`, allowing a source file swapped to a symlink between the two operations to be followed; it also allowed a symlinked parent directory to redirect reads outside the requested root. The reader now rejects symlinked path components and uses a no-follow descriptor with identity and metadata stability checks. Tests cover both static parent-directory aliasing and a deterministic path swap. The declared inventory still builds from the current repository (12 entries), all 841 script tests pass, and `git diff --check` passes.

Declared dependency parsing (2026-10-03): a regression showed that raw regex scans counted commented-out Gradle module and plugin declarations, causing phantom inventory entries or rejection for an apparent extra plugin. Generation now strips line and nested block comments while respecting quoted strings, then checks that declaration matches start in Kotlin code rather than inside regular or raw string literals. The regression test first failed on the phantom plugin and now passes with both dependency and plugin examples; the current repository inventory remains 12 entries and the full script suite passes 842 tests. `git diff --check` passes.

Store metadata validation continuation (2026-10-03): added failing-before-fix regression cases for boolean `lines` being accepted as integer `1`, a title path swapped to a symlink during read, a symlinked metadata parent during verification, and finalization creating directories beneath a symlinked parent. The verifier now requires exact integer types for all recorded counts and bytes, reads checked files through no-follow descriptors with stable identity checks, rejects symlinks in every metadata-directory component, and checks ancestors before creating a missing metadata directory. All 14 focused tests and all 846 Python script tests pass; `git diff --check` passes.

Store graphics manifest validation (2026-10-03): exact-value comparisons previously accepted JSON floats such as `1024.0` for integer dimensions or byte counts. These numeric evidence fields now require exact integers. The graphics verifier also rejects symlinked path ancestors and uses bounded no-follow descriptor reads with before/after identity checks; tests exercise float facts, parent aliases, and path replacement at open. All 13 focused graphics tests and all 849 Python script tests pass; `git diff --check` passes.

Store listing parity input boundary (2026-10-03): the listing reader checked its 256 KiB limit only after `Path.read_bytes()` had already read the entire file. It now checks the regular-file size before opening, rejects symlinked path ancestors, reads through a no-follow descriptor within the checked size, and rechecks file identity and timestamps. Added malformed encoding/normalization, pre-open oversize, symlink race, and parent-symlink cases. The focused suite passes 9 tests; the actual checked-in Play listing matches all three metadata files; the full script suite passes 853 tests and `git diff --check` passes.

Curated screenshot PNG grammar (2026-10-03): adversarial, CRC-correct PNGs showed the verifier accepted indexed palettes larger than `2**bit_depth`, lowercase reserved third chunk-type bytes, and `tRNS` on RGBA images. The PNG parser now enforces palette capacity, the uppercase reserved bit, and type/order/length constraints for `tRNS`; focused regressions first reproduced the palette and chunk-bit accepts and now pass. All 16 screenshot-verifier tests and all 856 Python script tests pass. `git diff --check` passes. The uppercase reserved-bit requirement follows the [W3C PNG specification](https://www.w3.org/TR/png-3/).

## Cross-cutting implementation model (from docs and inspected GameView)

### Training-control applicability boundary

`run_all_training.py` is a deliberately repository-specific no-trainable-surface certificate entrypoint, not a generic experiment runner. It imports the audit authority and dataset-cohort not-applicable certificate, enforces output path containment under the repo, clears a prior certificate before current audit, cross-checks source/scope/authority snapshot digests, then atomically emits either a schema-v2 PASS classification (`no_retained_trainable_surface`, not-applicable controls, no execution claim) or a FAIL with unresolved status and exit 2. `training_control/dataset_cohort_not_applicable_v1.py` derives a dataset-cohort certificate only from a complete authority audit and carries scanned-file count plus source/scope/authority digests; it does not fabricate cohorts.

`training_control/forest_no_trainable_authority.py` defines the no-ML applicability scanner. It inventories authored source/config/dependency files while skipping docs, training-control self-code, generated/build/vendor areas and binary assets; source bytes are length-prefixed into a deterministic manifest. Separate scope and authority manifests bind path/type inventory and the exact authority code. It masks Python strings/comments and dependency comments before checking training/framework markers, extracts executable notebook cells, AST-detects dynamic imports of known ML frameworks, records dependency declarations, and separately flags model artifacts, opaque executable archives and repository symlinks (with an explicit Gradle wrapper JAR exception). `require_no_trainable_surface` first requires canonical Android application files and production Kotlin/Android dependency presence, then fails closed on any finding. The scanner reports source-only applicability and expressly makes no training execution claim. Both control files and the root runner were read fully; the runner was not invoked. The scanner was subsequently executed read-only on the current repository; its result is recorded in the execution ledger below.

### Continuous-integration workflows

`.github/workflows/android-validation.yml` has main-push and manual triggers, read-only repository permission, per-ref cancellation, and two exact-event-SHA jobs. Host validation performs source-contract assertions across engine ownership, telemetry, release evidence, accessibility-adjacent gameplay boundaries and tooling; source asset preflight and Python unit suite; checks source immutability; installs Java 21 and Android API 36/build tools; generates and cross-validates dependency inventory/SBOM from release and Android-test runtime classpaths; compiles debug/release/instrumentation, runs unit tests and lint, assembles APKs/AAB, inspects page-size package risk, verifies R8 mapping outputs and uploads reports/artifacts. Connected validation checks out the same SHA, provisions API 36 build tools plus API 35 Google APIs x86_64 emulator/KVM, then delegates to `run_connected_validation.sh` with origin check skipped but candidate SHA pinned; uploaded reports are retained even on failure. The workflow does not run the physical-only large profiling suite on the emulator.

`.github/workflows/training-control-audit.yml` runs on main/manual with read-only permission, exact-SHA checkout, Python 3.11, compiles the local no-training authority, runs the not-applicable regression suite, issues the local certificate and uploads it even after failure. `.github/workflows/estate-training-control-local.yml` uses the same triggers/security/exact-SHA/Python version but generates a separate estate-local certificate path and uploads it with a distinct artifact name; it calls only the repo-local authority rather than introducing a generic external registry. All three workflow files were read completely; no jobs or commands were executed.

## Deterministic scenario traces and release evidence

### Trace capture and replay contracts

`DeterministicScenarioTrace.kt` defines each dispatched input event with scenario, zero-based sequence, scheduled/dispatched float seconds, and action. Construction rejects negative sequence, nonfinite/negative schedule, and dispatch before schedule; lateness is clamped nonnegative. A snapshot is replayable only with an active scenario, no overflow, and matching scenario plus contiguous sequence. `DeterministicScenarioTraceRecorder` is synchronized, bounded (default 256, hard maximum 4096), cleared at `begin`, and records only the currently active scenario at exactly the next sequence. Invalid event construction returns false; capacity overflow latches until next `begin`. Snapshot copies the list. It records successful dispatch evidence only and does not perform gameplay or I/O.

`DebugScenarioInputContract` validates authored action scripts as a state machine: times must be finite, nonnegative, nondecreasing and at most 60 seconds; jump and duck held states cannot overlap, taps cannot occur while either is active, duplicate start or unmatched end actions are violations, and scripts must release both states. It accumulates violations rather than stopping at the first malformed step. `DeterministicScenarioReplayContract.matches` is stricter than structural replayability: expected authored input steps must be nonempty and match event count, scenario, exact index, exact scheduled Float value, and action.

`EncounterScenarioFingerprint` creates versioned SHA-256 fingerprints from canonical UTF-8 strings. Scenario fields are serialized in a fixed order; strings use UTF-8 byte-length prefixes, times are rounded to integer microseconds, and offsets to micro-pixels. The catalogue fingerprint includes every enum entry in declaration order. A separate trace-contract fingerprint binds the scenario fingerprint to its authored debug-input script. These hashes provide stable identity for the authored definitions; they are not signatures or authentication.

### Evidence codec, disk verification, and manifest

`DeterministicScenarioTraceEvidenceCodec.encode` admits only snapshots satisfying the exact replay contract, lowercase-normalizes candidate commit/artifact IDs, validates their 40/64 lowercase-hex forms and nonnegative capture time, computes scenario and trace-contract fingerprints, and writes canonical schema-v2 JSON. Event times are rounded to microseconds; lateness is derived from the two integer values. Payload size is capped at 256 KiB. Decode accepts only the exact current regex schema, then `isCanonical` rechecks envelope hashes, authored fingerprints, event count, byte digest, metadata prefix, event syntax/order, exact scheduled input time and action, dispatch >= schedule, and exact lateness arithmetic. Thus a recomputed payload digest alone cannot bless altered events.

`DeterministicScenarioTraceEvidenceStore` rejects noncanonical evidence and atomically writes its UTF-8 payload using Android `AtomicFile` start/finish/fail semantics. Its stable filename includes the scenario enum name. The independent `...EvidenceVerifier` reads at most 256 KiB (checking declared length and streamed total), requires UTF-8 round-trip identity, decodes canonical payload from bytes, binds scenario/commit/artifact and optionally expected payload digest, and requires the expected scenario-derived filename. It returns a compact verified metadata value rather than trusting the creator's in-memory envelope.

`DeterministicScenarioTraceManifestCodec` constructs schema-v1 candidate-level JSON only from canonical evidence for one matching commit and artifact, with exactly one entry for every scenario having authored debug steps. Entries follow enum order independent of capture order. Canonical decode and `isCanonical` validate exact JSON shape, full ordered scenario set, uniqueness of scenario/file, filename convention, all digest forms, positive action count, nonnegative capture time, current scenario and trace-contract fingerprints, expected script event count, exact canonical payload and digest; size is capped at 128 KiB. `...ManifestStore` writes only canonical bytes atomically. The independent manifest verifier validates bounded UTF-8 bytes, expected commit/artifact/optional manifest digest, rejects extra scenario-trace JSON files, verifies each child file through the evidence verifier, and cross-checks capture time and event count before returning the verified scenario list. This is a fail-closed completeness boundary for release tooling; it does not itself capture traces or establish who built/signed an artifact.

### Narrow gameplay helpers

`MercySystem` owns per-run near-miss count, mercy hearts (capped at 10), and kindness chain. A mercy miss increments all three (heart only below cap); clean pass increments chain by one; spare by two; hit clears chain; reset clears all. Counter increments saturate at `Int.MAX_VALUE` and negative current state is normalized to zero by the helper.

`SweptCoreOverlap` avoids false collision reports from unioned swept bounds: it linearly interpolates each rectangle edge over the same normalized frame interval and intersects the four strict separating-edge inequalities as open time windows. Any malformed/nonfinite/empty rectangle is rejected. A collision is true only if the four inequalities admit a shared nonempty instant interval; it applies to primary physical cores only.

`PacifistRouteTier` is a presentation-bearing enum (`NONE`, `KIND`, `MERCIFUL`, `PEACEFUL`) with the display label and authored rest/garden lines used by the route-tier presentation. `SafeProgressionArithmetic` provides a configurable saturating integer increment (default cap `Int.MAX_VALUE / 16`) and elapsed-time helpers that return false/zero for negative timestamps, negative thresholds, or clock rollback, avoiding overflow-prone subtraction outside validated monotonic inputs.

### Recovery support and maintenance surface

`RecoveryEvidenceMaintenanceCoordinator` requires exactly one handler for each evidence domain (run outcome and ghost promotion), serializes maintenance calls, and reports CLEAN/PENDING/CORRUPT/BLOCKED/IO_FAILURE with fixed machine details. Safe recovery skips corrupt evidence. `discardCorrupt` clears only currently corrupt evidence; `discardUnresolvedPending` refuses CLEAN/CORRUPT/IO_FAILURE, retries recovery first, reports RECOVERED_INSTEAD if that retry finishes, and clears only still-pending/blocked state. Clear success is verified by re-inspection. The Android assembly captures app context and ghost preference namespace once, then supplies a SharedPreferences journal handler and namespace-bound ghost receipt/manifest/artifact handler. Ghost recovery disposition maps to stable state/detail, and manifest evidence is checked against actual ghost frames and current best distance before being called pending or clean. The report's support string contains only domain, state and bounded coded detail rather than summary or frame contents.

`RecoveryEvidencePresentation` maps internal state and allowlisted detail codes to fixed user-facing copy, severity, and available actions. Pending/blocked permits retry and pending discard; corrupt permits corrupt-data discard; I/O failure permits retry; clean permits no action. Unknown detail codes fall back by state, preventing raw data leakage. `RecoveryEvidenceUserController` re-inspects before every operation, rejects actions no longer offered, requires `confirmed=true` for both destructive actions, delegates through injected ports, then returns a newly presented report; runtime exceptions become ACTION_FAILED with the pre-action model. Its factory routes through `ApplicationPersistenceFacade`.

`NamespaceBoundRunOutcomeMaintenanceStateStore` pins one SharedPreferences instance at construction, mirroring relevant SaveManager reads while using synchronous `commit()` for repair writes. It reads mood, return moment, last summary and route counts; enum strings safely fall back, counters are clamped, and route-tier keys are explicitly mapped. `SharedPreferencesRunOutcomeSummarySnapshotStore` writes the normalized completed summary and its route-tier count in one synchronous transaction. NONE has no route counter key. This avoids replaying a legacy writer's implicit counter update as a second recovery side effect.

`GhostIoTelemetry` publishes synchronized snapshots of volatile counters for write starts/completions/failures, latest/max frame counts, and latest/max duration. Counters saturate, invalid negative samples clamp to zero, and this is out-of-band diagnostics only. `FamiliarityWarmthScoring` clamps relationship counts nonnegative, adds one base point by stage and one each at pass>=3, pass>=5, spares>=2, kindness streak>=3, and encounters>=5. Scores >=5 and >=7 map to personal and bonded ordinals; lower maps to the first tier.

### Save schema integrity

`SaveIntegrityManager.repair` resets the active SaveManager namespace to primary, then inspects the primary schema marker. If it is newer than this build's schema v1, the primary namespace remains untouched and repair is directed to the v1 compatibility namespace; the report preserves the newer version and distinguishes write failure from future-version fallback. Otherwise a `RepairSession` snapshots the original preference map and queues edits. Repairs cover nonnegative score/Seeds/distance and counters; bounded garden unlock index; known enums and enum sets; required/default forest mood and costume; timestamps; bounded generic memory/history identifier sets; relationship milestone set; last-run record completeness/types; and only recognized entity/biome-prefixed dynamic keys. Unknown preferences remain unchanged.

Malformed partial last-run bundles are removed as a unit, except `last_run_killer` is optional; complete bundles get per-field normalization. Costume consistency removes an active/featured costume that is not unlocked. A differing schema marker is written as v1. The repair report is CURRENT only when a v1 namespace needed no changes, MIGRATED otherwise, and WRITE_FAILED when the synchronous commit fails. `RepairSession` counts queued edits, uses safe type-casts and clamping/defaults, prunes string sets to allowed members, sorts/caps generic IDs, and avoids a commit when nothing changed. One behavior to revisit only if requested: `repair()` changes SaveManager's active namespace as part of selecting primary/compatibility, so callers observe the selected namespace afterward.

### UI text and geometry leaves

`FiniteCoordinateAdmission.accepts` rejects nonfinite mapped pointer coordinates before hit-testing. `GraphemeClusters.split` iterates Unicode code points and groups combining/spacing/enclosing marks, variation selectors, emoji modifiers/tags, ZWJ-linked sequences, CRLF, and paired regional indicators. It preserves scalar sequences without claiming full platform shaping or glyph support. `MathUtils` is a pure math toolkit: lerp/extrapolating interpolation, clamp/clamp01, normalized/map ranges with zero-span guard, smoothstep, degree trig, dp conversion and three-way sign.

`BitmapHelper.buildPlaceholderStrip` is a debuggable fallback sprite generator: validates minimum frame geometry, positive count, Android width and 16M-pixel allocation cap, then paints pulsing rounded bodies with outline and animated eye into one ARGB strip. `GardenLayoutPlanner` uses finite positive landscape dimensions and positive catalog sizes to calculate fixed proportional bands for Run/catalogue/stats/last-run/wardrobe. Plant cards divide the catalogue with a width-relative gap; wardrobe cards use four columns, ceil-derived row count, panel insets and relative gaps. It rejects cards that cannot fit. `LayoutBox` guards intersection/containment against invalid or nonfinite boxes and uses strict overlap boundaries.

`RestQuoteManager.quoteFor` concatenates the story-system base quote with a deterministic coda. Route tier takes precedence, then a killer-specific mood pool, otherwise biome + mood + universal pools. A stable weighted arithmetic seed from summary counters, mood, biome, killer and route tier chooses `floorMod(seed, poolSize)`, so the same run context produces the same variant without random state. Entity display names derive from enum identifiers by lowercasing and replacing underscores.

`DebugEncounterOverlay` is a Canvas diagnostics panel for selected/active deterministic encounter state, plan summary, biome, remaining steps, suite size, Bloom, mercy and entity count. It clamps its layout width, lays out three tap regions, rejects nonfinite taps, and emits only PREVIOUS/TOGGLE_RUN/NEXT actions; it does not mutate the director. `FeedbackSettingsPanelLayout` sizes three comfort-toggle chips and a separate Forest Journal chip proportionally with clamped dimensions and verifies every rectangle is finite, nonempty and contained. Half-open hit tests reject nonfinite coordinates. `FeedbackSettingsPanel` delegates preference changes through `ApplicationPersistenceFacade`, obtains current values from `FeedbackSettings`, opens `ForestJournalActivity` on the dedicated memory chip, and draws the values as Canvas chips. When context is not an Activity it adds `NEW_TASK` to the intent.

`GameConstants` centralizes scroll speed/ramp, Bloom threshold/duration, mercy window, spawn gap/ramp, biome distance, score scale and base wind multipliers used by simulation/rendering systems. `CollisionRuntimePort` narrows live side effects into methods for run hit, ghost suppression, player rest/stumble, flashes, shakes, sounds, haptics and mercy particles; terminal and nonterminal adapter classes are pure forwarders mapping impact haptic to long pulse, stumble to medium pulse, and mercy acknowledgment to double tap. Ordering stays in the coordinators described above.

`ForestRunLegacyComposer` creates a read-only Journal projection from SaveManager: sanitized mood counters, high score/best distance, and optional last-run record. Distance is finite-positive and truncated/clamped to `Int`; counters clamp nonnegative; quote trims. Route enums get stable display labels. The snapshot data classes enforce nonnegative numeric values and nonblank mood/route labels.

`DebugScenarioScript` owns a volatile prepared action list and next-index with short synchronized prepare/dispatch/clear sections. `prepare` fetches scenario steps, validates the jump/duck state machine, resets state and starts trace capture. `advanceTimed` ignores nonfinite elapsed time and completed scripts, dispatches every due step in order, and for HOLD_JUMP_END computes the authored hold duration from its matching prior start so batched frames preserve jump-release semantics. Successfully returned dispatches are then traced with scheduled and observed dispatch time, and index advances. `clear` snapshots the trace before dropping the active scenario; trace reads return active or completed snapshots. Current scripts author paired jump holds for Cactus/ Cat/ Fox/ Eagle scenarios; others have no input actions. `advance` is an action-only adapter over `advanceTimed`.

`FlavorTextManager` keeps at most 16 singleton floating labels, caps text to 72 characters, clamps lifetime/size, rejects invalid position, and deduplicates nearby repeats against stable original anchors (resetting animation). Update rejects invalid/nonpositive dt, advances time and moves upward with double-intermediate clamping, removes expired labels, and publishes active count. Draw uses the optional pixel font, shadow, shrinking pop-in scale and late alpha fade. Clear empties and publishes zero. Manager state is mutable and intended to be driven in the game runtime's serialized flow.

`DialogueBubbleManager` keeps five world-anchored bubbles and a bounded 128-key cyclic variant index. Spawn normalizes text, wraps to a measured maximum width/three lines and tracks widest width. Update moves/fades bubbles and publishes telemetry. Draw reuses Paint/RectF/Path scratch geometry, clamps bubble horizontal/top placement to screen margins, positions a pointer toward the clamped anchor, draws shadow/fill/border and centered wrapped lines. Wrapping collapses whitespace, splits oversized tokens only at `GraphemeClusters` boundaries, enforces line count and appends a width-fitted ellipsis. `clear` resets bubbles, variant cycling, measurement counter and telemetry. Its static scratch/state likewise expects serialized UI/game-thread access.

### Concrete entity implementations reviewed so far

`Cat` sizes and hit insets from `ReadabilityProfile`/`SpriteSizing`, snapshots relationship tuning, warm-bond and repeat-friend evidence at construction, and moves left until passed. Its special-safe-departure path activates only once, when mercy hearts reach five, then awards the normal kindness reward, starts the wave/spare effects, records the spare and counts it in run state. Ordinary safe pass awards bonus and relationship-aware seeds/points, optionally emits a seed glint for warm/repeat familiarity, and presents authored PASS dialogue. Spare mode moves right briefly and disables collision; spare persistence is gated by entity policy. Collision uses exact primary hitbox first and readability plus relationship mercy padding second.

`Cactus` uses ground-relative readability size and hit insets, a staging halo pulse and small sway. Passing grants points, then derives authored flora copy from persisted encounter/hit/pass counts (anticipating the central pass count that EntityManager records after callback) and emits seed particles. Collision is exact hit then expanded mercy miss. `Eucalyptus` is a larger animated/swaying flora hazard: a sinusoidal gust pulse modulates a broad approach lane, danger band, three wind/leaf guide strokes and whip tip; sprite rotates around its foot. A clean pass gives score + one seed, emits drifting petals, and presents repeat-hit-sensitive copy. Both use central memory recording and return HIT/MERCY_MISS/NONE consistently.

`Hyacinth` separates lethal core from a drawn soft brush: exact core overlap is HIT; present or same-time swept brush overlap is STUMBLE; only expanded brush fringe is MERCY_MISS. It exposes the whole brush as encounter bounds so pass credit waits until the visible collision span clears. Its rhythm animation and sway are visual cues; clean pass awards points and pollen with encounter/hit-sensitive flavor. `LilyOfValley` uses a lower hit top than its luminous blossom and renders a pulsing lure column and seed-trap band; its collision contract remains lethal core then padded mercy fringe. Pass gives score + seed, night-glow and seed particles, and history-aware line. `VanillaOrchid` renders one sprite as overhead branch and low vine, with distinct corresponding top/bottom lethal hitboxes and an intentional safe thread between. It snapshots both bands each update and uses same-time swept collision per band, never a union across the gap. A player wholly contained in the currently computed thread is explicitly safe; otherwise asymmetric readability padding yields a mercy miss. Pass awards 150 points + seed and separate blossom/pollen effects and flavor. Collision geometry recomputes at its current x from the two visible segment proportions.

`Dog` is a two-mode entity selected at construction (default 20% buddy): hazard mode scrolls and barks on a 1.8 s timer, emitting moving `BarkProjectile` rectangles; buddy mode remains alongside the player for a randomized 3–5 s plus relationship bonus, sequences authored dialogue/SFX/particles, then dashes left at 5x scroll speed. Projectiles snapshot prior rectangles and use current plus same-time swept core tests; every lethal projectile and body test resolves before any mercy-ring result, avoiding a near-miss masking a separate hit. Hazard bark/near-miss/body padding includes relationship tuning. Buddies are collision harmless; dash culling defers while encounter outcome is still pending so EntityManager can arbitrate a same-frame clean pass. Hazard pass awards tuned points/seeds and PASS dialogue. Buddy reward is guarded once, relationship/warm-bond/celebration-scaled and emits seed/star cues and SPARE dialogue. Buddy dialogue cadence and finale also vary by stage/warmth; the actual mode and reward remain per entity.

`Fox` walks until the Player's airborne state enters its relationship-scaled mirror-detection lane, then jumps with independent force/gravity, landing cues and familiarity particles. At five mercy hearts it can stand down, become a harmless rightward-moving spared state, record one spare, and receive tuned rewards. A mirrored successful jump earns one pass reward only once; simply passing before it reacts is not rewarded by that branch. Body collision is nonlethal STUMBLE with relationship-adjusted mercy fringe. `Hedgehog` approaches at 1.15x scroll and snapshots prior hit count for its authored cue variant. Player entering a reaction-time-derived warning rectangle starts a 0.16–0.22s lead before the hop lane is armed; lead includes one maximum admitted simulation frame so sampling cannot consume the whole window. Body contact is always STUMBLE and applies a 50% speed debuff for 3 seconds only once, curls/holds final sprite frame, and cues hit; a miss invokes `Eep!`, while pass rewards differ if the telegraph was read. `Wolf` walks to half-screen, triggers threat dialogue/howl SFX/camera shake, pauses through a readability-and-relationship-scaled howl duration, then charges at a speed combining walk speed, world scroll, and aggression tuning while emitting dust. Eight mercy hearts permit one stand-down spare and tuned multi-particle/repeat-respect presentation; only a clean pass during CHARGING gets its pass reward. Body hit is STUMBLE and expanded proximity is MERCY_MISS.

Bird family contracts: `Duck` places a flyer relative to `Player.BASE_HEIGHT` so its body overlaps a standing Player yet clears the crouched body. Approach rectangles cue quack and duck lane once; actual duck-state overlap records `stayedLow`, while a duck after quack records `answeredQuack`, which controls escalating pass score/seed and copy. `Owl` sleeps high, becomes ALERT only if a jump occurs while horizontally visible, snapshots the Player center, warns once, then dives toward the stored point after a relationship-scaled telegraph; only diving owl collision is lethal/mercy-relevant. The attack can count as completed escape after cull for pass arbitration. `Eagle` spawns above viewport with placeholder lane, updates target from live Player until a delayed lock, and announces once with screech/shake/dialogue; then travels at normalized vector speed toward target. Cue geometry renders a reticle, target zone and corridor; after lock plus 0.18s grace, entering the zone negates the held mark. Horizontal departure is meaningful only after entering viewport, preserving staged scenarios; completed marked escapes are surfaced for post-collision arbitration. Reward depends on preserving the mark and relationship tuning.

`ChickadeeGroup` clamps flock size to 2–4, samples independent random altitudes/intervals, and interpolates every member separately. Collision checks each physical bird rectangle, including same-time sweep, before applying mercy; the aggregate hitbox only supports broad pass/cull ownership. Its highlighted safe pocket is computed by scanning actual vertical gaps between bird hitboxes and is shown only when wide enough for the full airborne Player envelope; if none, it may show a ground lane only after proving every bird clears it. Prompting and read credit are distinct: credit requires the whole Player collision body contained in the pocket. Pass bonus scales with flock altitude spread and pocket read. `TitGroup` clamps size to 3–5, moves all birds on one shared sinusoidal vertical wave and maintains individual current/previous cores. Its trough guide is sized for the full airborne Player body, not a cosmetic narrow slit. Count-in/trough prompts are one-shot, while kept-beat bonus requires full Player-body containment. Collision returns HIT on any current/swept individual core before accumulating mercy proximity. Both groups avoid using their aggregate rectangle for lethal geometry.

Tree family contracts: `CherryBlossom` is a ground-anchored swaying canopy with independent trunk and mid-height branch danger cores plus a wider storm veil; the public encounter rectangle encloses both. Current and same-time swept checks are against the individual solids; mercy can come from the veil or trunk. `Jacaranda` has upper branch + trunk solids and advertises a lower duck underpass. It tests full Player-body containment in the lane before either solid (including trunk) to keep the visually promised route open across the entire tree. `WeepingWillow` follows the same full-width safe-lane contract beneath its hanging curtain and trunk, drawing the opaque lane over decorative pixels; branch/canopy visual guides do not replace collision cores. Both grant fixed pass bonus/seed plus history-aware prose and petals/seeds. `Bamboo` constructs five stalks split into ten independent top/bottom physical rectangles with four gaps; the second gap is wider and visually featured. Only individual cores are current/swept lethal geometry, preventing the enclosing pass box from filling real gaps. Random vertical gap placement is constrained by `BambooGapPlacement`: finite valid surface, full Player-height gap, conservative jump rise using the maximum admitted step, margin, and authored mid-jump center band must produce a nonempty range or construction fails. The full-span aggregate still bounds pass/cull arbitration using swayed x. Passing awards points/seed and history-aware copy.

`HUD` preallocates Canvas paints/geometry, loads the pixel font with monospace fallback, and uses central constants for segment count. `update` clamps dt to 50ms, lerps displayed Bloom toward meter or full-active target and repairs nonfinite animation state; active Bloom and new-high-score badges use bounded pulse oscillators. `draw` composes the top strip, run seeds and fixed ten-heart strip, Bloom meter, score/distance, optional opening cue. Bloom fill is segmentwise with a partial final segment and green vertical gradients; presentation mode (charging/ready/active/afterglow) controls panel, labels, count/status colors and active glow. Score is right-aligned; distance formats metres or two-decimal km, score comma-separates thousands; NEW badge pulses, otherwise previous high score appears as ghost-best. Opening guidance draws cue title/body and completion chips in a lower panel. Displayed HUD hearts are hard-coded to ten, matching MercySystem's cap.

`GameOverScreen` is a draw-only full-screen rest composition; GameView owns tap progression. It memoizes `RestComposition` by value-equal `RunSummary`, combining `SessionArcComposer.restCopy`, `GardenSanctuaryPlanner`, and `PostRunReflectionPlanner`. Update clamps accepted frame time to 50ms for prompt/cinematic pulse. Draw scales scrim/panel/atmosphere/cinematic polish with recovery progress, then renders the REST header, recovery title/subtitle/line, optional arrival and home-character badges, base rest quote/mood, conditional route/world-opinion/world labels and explanations, score/distance/new-best/seeds/Bloom/hearts/run counters/kindness chain, reflection and up to two homecoming consequence cards, run-kept stats card and carry-home card. Sanctuary lighting effects are derived from the REST identity: canopy tint, up to three mist bands, ground glow and up to four lantern glows. The tap prompt fades while recovering and pulses otherwise. Text wrapping is a simple space-based, two-line measure; info cards likewise cap at two lines. `formatNumber` provides grouped thousands. The view uses immutable summary + synthesized long-horizon projections rather than writing persistence.

`MainMenuScreen` implements an IDLE → STANDING_UP → READY willow ritual and latches a one-shot run-start request for GameView. Taps first reject nonfinite coordinates, then route IDLE comfort/Journal controls and bottom-left Garden button before the ritual; taps during stand-up are ignored. Accessibility primary action mirrors the ritual but not Garden/settings controls. Update clamps dt to 50ms, advances copied willow/bird/player sprite animations and sanitizes timers; stand-up ends by timer or sprite completion. `refreshCopy` recomposes menu language plus sanctuary projection from last summary; `resetRitual` resets state and sprite poses; `consumeStartRunRequest` clears the latch. Draw creates sky/ground, sanctuary atmosphere, ambient animated bird and willow, state-scaled character, cinematic polish, title/copy, arrival/home signs/character, launch cue and first two consequence chips, then comfort controls in IDLE and phase-specific pulsing prompt/support. The sanctuary painter uses fixed menu lighting with bounded canopy, mist, fireflies, lanterns, petals, ground glow and Bloom patches. STANDING_UP/READY additionally draw a growing illuminated launch lane and footsteps; launch cue/help labels change by phase. Character uses seated sprite at 0.65 height, interpolated stand-up plus squash, then run sprite with subtle bounce. Wrapped menu text is simple space-based and limited to two lines. Sprite sheets are copied from SpriteManager before local frame-rate changes, avoiding changes to shared animation owners.

`GardenScreen` binds a nine-entry visual plant catalog and copied sprite sheets to canonical `GardenEconomy.catalogueSize`, and fixes panel/card geometry through `GardenLayoutPlanner`. `load`/`refresh` read progress/Seeds, sync costume unlock/equip state, then refresh long-horizon projections; `refresh` marks actual garden entry. Update clamps dt to 50ms, wraps elapsed time, advances local sprite/visitor/particle animation, and bounds unlock and wardrobe-message timers. Tap order: reject nonfinite coordinates; synchronize layout; RUN callback; wardrobe card (equip NONE/unlocked through facade then adopt committed state, or show locked label); bottom-edge back callback; plant card (only next index enters purchase coordinator), otherwise false. `adoptPurchaseResult` only updates local presentation, and on success starts card scale animation plus seed particle; persistence and haptic are owned upstream. `adoptCommittedCostume` updates local selection/message and refreshes stats.

Garden draw composes mood-dependent sky/ground, sanctuary ambience and cinematic emphasis, mood title, return moment or featured visitor/arrival line, sanctuary line, arrival/home-character/traces, lifetime Seeds, then stats/run/last-run/wardrobe panels and all plant cards. A pending return moment is acknowledged only after the draw issues its presentation commands, using the timestamp captured when entering; merely refreshing doesn't consume it. Cards distinguish unlocked, next, and farther locked states; only unlocked/next draw copied plant sprites, others show dim emoji; the next cost pulses; the just-unlocked card scales. Stats include best distance, last killer, totals of cat/fox/wolf spares and all biome friendships, strongest bond, and memory-page count. Last-run panel clips and ellipsizes compact summary plus route afterglow, world opinion, homecoming consequence and two reflections. Wardrobe cards render per-style procedural icons and distinguish equipped/unlocked/locked; unlock/equip messaging fades. `refreshStats` reads latest run/mood/relationships, previews or consumes story helpers based on entering flag, snapshots local day for return moment, then deliberately builds memory count and sanctuary only after StoryFragmentSystem calls because those can persist Garden-context pages. It composes milestone reward/presence/ritual, costume signs, arrival line and reflection entries. `syncWardrobe` lets CostumeManager refresh unlocks and surfaces new unlock copy. Text wrapping is whitespace based, bounded by line count, with truncation on last line; distance and entity names are formatted locally. The screen coordinates Garden currency/progress through the application facade and delegates purchase to `GardenPurchaseInteractionCoordinator`.

- `GameView` is the top-level integration coordinator. A single `runtimeStateLock` serializes simulation, drawing, pointer decisions, accessibility routing, debug launch, lifecycle geometry publication, and run-state interaction.
- `GameThread` drives bounded frame update and drawing. Shutdown is bounded; an uncooperative old thread prevents a replacement from starting until termination. `LatestRequestGate` invalidates stale resume/restart callbacks.
- Runtime input is admitted only in app `PLAYING` + run `PLAYING`; gesture callbacks call the real `Player` methods. Non-game screens cancel active gestures. Touch is transformed from physical safe-area coordinates to logical coordinates.
- `RunSessionTransitionCoordinator`/`LiveRunSessionEffects` own app/run state transitions and invoke action ports for reset, menu ritual, death/restart, ghost reload, Garden refresh, and music.
- Terminal and nonterminal collision outcomes flow through `CollisionOutcomeDispatcher`; coordinators own result sequencing, while `LiveCollisionEffects` connects cues, effects, haptics, Player changes and persistence adapters to runtime owners.
- `ApplicationPersistenceFacade` is the application mutation boundary, with domain stores remaining separate. Ghost promotion uses asynchronous, namespace-bound durable sequencing; terminal non-ghost state uses a recoverable synchronous journal.
- Accessibility exposes a semantic node provider and action router on the Canvas surface; native `ForestJournalActivity` owns long-form Journal reading.

## `engine/GameView.kt` — complete coordinator path

### Construction and state

The view owns the `SurfaceHolder.Callback`, current `GameThread`, restart gate, cadence clock, runtime monitor, run/app state, `InputHandler`, initialized gameplay owners, and transient presentation state. `GameThread` and all dimension/state publication use volatile fields where cross-thread readers need visibility; the lock provides coherent compound state access. Audio bootstrapping is per view instance so a surface recreation does not reset a live music phase.

Its session coordinator is wired to concrete live effects: fresh run preparation, menu ritual/copy, death, restart start/reset, ghost recorder reset/reload, Garden refresh and menu music. Run mode is separately tracked so debug/scenario/capture paths can suppress persistent writes, ghost recording or ordinary progress cues as specified by `RunMode`.

The view also owns debug-only encounter director/overlay/script and deferred latest debug intent; screen-space mercy/Bloom overlays; reusable paints/render profiles/visibility geometry; persistence facade; ghost recorder/player; terminal impact/outcome, nonterminal outcome and exhaustive collision dispatcher. Live accessibility actions are lazy and route callbacks back through the same state/session owners under `runtimeStateLock`.

### Touch and accessibility provider

`init` registers the holder callback and touch listener. Under the runtime monitor, touch coordinates are stored and mapped through `safeContentTransform`. In gameplay the debug overlay gets first chance on debug ACTION_UP, then `InputHandler` receives the platform event. Outside gameplay, the active gesture is cancelled; ACTION_UP routes to Menu, Garden, or Rest and invokes `performClick`.

`getAccessibilityNodeProvider()` returns the lazily created custom provider. Snapshot construction derives current surface, feedback preferences, bounded distance, nonnegative score/Seeds, Garden progress/cost, costume availability/equipped style, Bloom/jump/duck readiness, and Rest summary from their existing authorities. Semantic bounds are computed in logical coordinates, mapped back to physical coordinates and rounded outward. Announcement polling is cadence-limited and announcement policy coalesces meaningful changes. Capability-change tracking invalidates only jump/long-jump/duck nodes whose real Player admission changed. When Android accessibility is disabled, availability tracking is cleared and framework announcements/events are not emitted.

Settings actions persist through `ApplicationPersistenceFacade`; Garden purchases/equips also use the facade and update `GardenScreen` only after authoritative commit. Accessibility jump synthesizes the same press/hold/release callbacks. Accessibility duck takes a token from `LatestRequestGate` after the actual press and releases after 280 ms only if that activation still owns the token and gameplay remains live/ducking. A newer duck or session change invalidates the old release.

### Surface initialization, resize, pause/resume

`surfaceCreated` begins a restart token and delegates to `initializeSurfaceWhenThreadStopped`. It rejects stale callbacks, retries while an old stopped-but-alive thread is exiting, then initializes inside the runtime lock. It captures dimensions and safe transform; lazily creates GameStateManager, parallax, sprite/entity managers, text/dialogue, Rest UI, ghost playback, audio/haptics, Menu/Garden, HUD, optional debug overlay and Player. Audio systems initialize once, then select a phase based on current Menu/Game Over/Bloom/run state. Screen-bound owners are constructed once, so thread recreation does not reset live gameplay state.

`surfaceChanged` runs the resize policy while locked. Safe/incremental changes rebuild one immutable transform under the same lock. A dimension-bound system requiring full recreation keeps its old coherent dimensions and posts the Activity recreation callback after leaving the lock. Insets similarly update and publish atomically. `surfaceDestroyed` invalidates pending restarts, then boundedly stops the thread.

`pause` marks paused and cancels pending handoff before stopping; pauses music, stops SFX and cancels haptics. It persists high score only if the thread is confirmed stopped and progress is persistent; otherwise logs and skips unsafe concurrent reads. `resume` starts a new generation and initializes only if the surface is valid. Handoff refuses to overlap thread instances and asynchronously retries while an old instance remains alive.

### Debug launch

`applyDebugLaunchIntent` is locked and debug-build-only. It reads scenario, requested run mode and autostart. If core owners or a valid surface are not ready, it stores one copied latest pending `Intent` rather than queueing stale captured closures. On ready scenario launch it resolves enum/mode, prepares scripted actions, optionally installs an in-memory ghost readability trace, selects director scenario, adopts the typed debug PLAYING transition, then resets/prepares the authored encounter scenario. Autostart instead clears scenario scripting, prepares an ordinary reset, and adopts the same typed state event. `matchesDebugLaunch` confirms published app/run/mode and active scenario consistency. MainActivity owns latest-request retry; this view holds at most one pending request.

### Frame update order (`update` → `updateBounded`)

`update` admits only finite positive delta and dispatches once with a maximum `0.05s` bounded delta while holding the runtime lock. The bounded body:

1. increments frame counter/cadence and camera shake;
2. ticks input only during live play, otherwise cancels it;
3. exits safely before uninitialized game state; updates Bloom-ready audio and presentation timers;
4. updates Menu and consumes start request, or updates Garden, with no world physics;
5. gates DYING/GAME_OVER/RESTARTING and advances only their own transitions/presentation;
6. advances GameState and parallax with its authoritative scroll speed; updates HUD and biome/pacifist-biome state and applies scene/colors;
7. transitions Player Bloom state and aura/power from GameState: activation owns music/SFX/haptics/camera/particles; expiry calculates afterglow and closing cue; conversion deltas drive tiered audiovisual cues without restarting the gameplay timer;
8. applies deterministic scripted input before this tick's Player physics, then updates Player and EntityManager;
9. resolves one collision result through the dispatcher, building detached terminal capture or stumble/mercy inputs lazily. A terminal result adopts immutable summary/quote, transitions to DYING and returns immediately so no post-terminal ghost frame/reward is recorded;
10. otherwise records ghost frame only in recording modes, updates visible ghost playback, consumes pacifist rewards and milestones, persists biome friendship only for persistent mode, emits bounded progress cues, advances text/particles, updates semantic action availability and cadence-limited accessibility announcements.

### Rendering order (`draw`)

Drawing is locked. It clears physical surface black; Menu and Garden render alone through safe-content transform. Gameplay applies camera shake to background, entities/orbs, ghost, live Player, optional debug hitboxes and world-space dialogue/flavor/particles. Then screen-space mercy flash, biome ambient darkening, Bloom frame/afterglow/flash, cinematic lighting, and safe-content HUD draw without camera shake. Optional debug panel and DYING/GAME_OVER Rest composition follow; RESTARTING adds the fade. Reusable paint/profile objects keep allocations out of hot draw paths.

### Reset, cues, scripted scenarios, and ghost context

`prepareFreshRun` restores NORMAL mode, stops scenario/script, validates initialized owners, retries/reinitializes run-outcome persistence, applies saved costume, resets game/entities/player, clears forced biome and seeds opening, resets/reloads ghost and all run-summary/Bloom/cue state, then starts run music.

`prepareEncounterScenario` uses the debug director, prepares the script, resets run owners, forces authored biome, primes/activates Bloom as declared, resets ghost and presentation, starts director and music. Only REST_LOOP gets an immediate forced Cactus; other entity steps come exclusively from the scenario director.

Ordinary mercy/kindness/clean cues have increasing threshold tiers (2/4/6; 3/5/8; 4/8/12) and are emitted only in modes that allow them. `resetOrdinaryProgressCueState` clears latches per run. Debug script advancement uses `GameStateManager.runTimeSeconds` and direct Player input methods at the top of a frame. Ghost playback policy distinguishes ordinary modes from scenarios that explicitly allow ghosts. Visibility context counts intersecting active hazard bounds near the real Player and computes nearest rectangle gap for ghost presentation attenuation.

## `engine/EntityManager.kt` — complete encounter lifecycle and outcome authority

### State and update

`activeEntities` owns live encounter objects; heterogeneous pooling is disabled because no universal reset contract exists. `completedBirdEscapes` temporarily retains an alerted Owl/Eagle removed by vertical flight until collision arbitration and safe departure can finish. Random spawning tracks world-distance since spawn. Bloom proximity tracks a cooldown, previous active state, and an identity ledger keyed by actual `Entity` references (not hash codes/equality).

`update(dt, gameState, player, director, mode)` rejects nonfinite or negative delta and publishes workload counts. Bloom transitions clear per-window identity/cooldown. The optional director advances time and spawns each due authored directive with its deterministic seed and persistence disabled. Random spawning advances distance, computes required world-space gap, honors opening lock, and only admits when real player/action state allows it. Every entity captures its prior hitbox, marks motion sample only for finite nonempty geometry, updates with the same current scroll speed, then updates player interaction only while active and PENDING. Inactive pending Owl/Eagle objects with completed escape state enter the deferred list before removal. Bloom nearby world reaction runs on active Bloom; orb update always runs after encounters. Count telemetry publishes at both rejected and accepted update boundaries.

### Collision arbitration

`checkCollisions` suppresses ordinary collision outcomes during Bloom. Otherwise it first checks deferred bird final motion so an escaping vertical dive can still hit before any departure reward. For active PENDING encounters it obtains the species sample and an additional swept-core result for the explicitly eligible narrow single-body species; severity priority is HIT 3, STUMBLE 2, MERCY_MISS 1, NONE 0. A sampled/padded mercy observation only marks `observedMercyContact`, remaining nonterminal. The strongest direct HIT/STUMBLE wins independent of list ordering; HIT short-circuits once found. The winner receives `onOutcomeSelected`, one terminal enum, cleared provisional mercy, passed state, and resolved-encounter persistence, then returns one `CollisionFrame`.

`sweptNarrowCoreResult` applies swept AABB to specific single-body hard cores (Cactus, Lily, Hyacinth, Eucalyptus, Duck, Cat as HIT; Hedgehog, Fox, Wolf as STUMBLE) only when both previous/current rectangles are valid and sampled. Aggregate flock/tree/window geometry and other authored mechanics remain under concrete species logic. `resolveCompletedBirdHit` applies equivalent sampled/swept final contact to completed escaping Owl/Eagle, then terminally records hit before departure. `resolvePassedEntities` checks the full encounter bounds (falling back to valid hitbox) and resolves only when its right edge is behind the Player. It processes every departure but surfaces at most the first mercy cue in that frame.

### Departure outcomes and side effects

`resolveSafeDeparture` marks passed, then Bloom conversion takes exclusive precedence. Otherwise a prior provisional mercy observation becomes one MERCY outcome, persistence event and Heart. Without it, the encounter gets a CLEAN_PASS. Bloom conversion clears mercy, records BLOOM_CONVERTED and encounter history, increments conversion count, emits class-aware environment FX, and deactivates the entity; it does not grant ordinary pass, unique-action or Orb rewards.

`resolveCleanPass` records CLEAN_PASS once. A species-specific `resolveSpecialSafeDeparture` may own the complete result (e.g. relationship buddy/spare), suppressing ordinary pass history/cue and optional Orb. Standard departures perform unique action, record clean pass, persist pass count when authorized, create authored dialogue/flavor cue, and stage an optional Seed Orb. Orb spawn uses real player/ground/speed geometry; it is skipped if any other unresolved encounter makes the approach unsafe, but that does not erase the clean-pass result. Species modifiers only raise rates for Lily (1.35) and Dog/Wolf (1.20).

`recordResolvedEncounter` and pass recording are guarded by per-entity `shouldRecordPersistence`. `draw` renders live entities; `drawOrbs` delegates to orb manager. `emitBloomEnvironmentReaction` differentiates flora, trees, cactus and other families; proximity reaction is once per actual entity object for that Bloom window, with world FX per family and rate-limited flavor text. Reset clears entities/deferred escapes/orbs/spawn distance/Bloom identity/cooldown and workload state.

### Random admission and factory boundary

`canAdmitRandomEncounter` requires Player RUNNING or LANDING and no active unresolved persistent encounter, preventing concurrent random encounters during a committed action/recovery. `spawnRandom` obtains the distance/biome difficulty pool, filters species by surface reachability, then applies the opening-biome pool. It raises start X to at least `player.hitbox.right + SpawnPacing.minimumRandomEncounterLeadPx(max(current, undebuffed speed))`, so temporary debuffs cannot make a spawn unfair when speed rebounds. The entity-selection bag has already selected the one authorized type.

`spawn` skips Bamboo on surfaces where its entire authored traversal geometry cannot be staged; it never silently substitutes another family. Nonfinite start positions fall back to canonical offscreen spawn. `EntityFactory` constructs by `EntityType`, with variant and deterministic `Random`; the persistence authorization flag is set afterward. The opening sequence is Duck → Lily → Cat → Tit at fixed world-X offsets. Debug spawn disables persistence. `entityTypeOf` is the exhaustive runtime concrete-class-to-catalogue mapping for all 19 families. `liveBounds` validates aggregate bounds then a hitbox fallback; empty/nonfinite rectangles are rejected.

## `engine/GameStateManager.kt` — complete per-run state/economy authority

`GameStateManager` owns volatile-to-callers, privately-set per-run values and sanitizes numeric ingress before arithmetic. Constructor keeps only `applicationContext`, local pacifist/mercy trackers, opening-input state and an encounter selection bag. A `persistProgress` supplier separates normal play from deterministic/debug/capture runs. Persistent high-score floor and exact record publication owner prevent nonpersistent scores from leaking on reset and prevent a stale concurrent run from claiming a durable tie it did not publish.

`update(dt)` admits finite positive dt; saturating-adds run time; snapshots distance-based base speed and normalized temporary debuff before movement; caps effective speed; uses that same speed for distance and score movement this frame; expires debuff only after this frame's speed; integrates fractional score and emits one-shot 1000-point milestones; advances Bloom timer once and clears active state at the authored duration. `undebuffedScrollSpeed` is the projected recovery ceiling used by safe spawn lead.

`addBonus` normalizes nonnegative points/seeds, applies normalized score multiplier, saturates score, delegates Seeds and then installs a valid positive multiplier boost. `collectSeed` is one Seed. `recordBloomConversion` saturating-increments conversion count and awards 140 points/one Seed. `addSeeds` saturates run currency, atomically persists lifetime reward only if authorized, then leaves the active Bloom timer/meter alone; otherwise it clamps meter below target and activates exactly once when reward crosses the remaining threshold, zeroing meter and timer. Debug Bloom helpers clamp a meter or force active state without persistent consequences.

`buildRunSummary` re-reads durable high score only for persistent mode. It reconciles local/durable best and allows NEW HIGH on equality only if this run's publication token matches that exact score. It snapshots run metrics, forest mood classification and current route tier. `applySpeedDebuff` rejects invalid multipliers/nonpositive duration and caps at 1. Mercy, clean-pass, spare, hit and biome methods forward to the dedicated owners and update route reward eligibility. Pacifist reward consumption is explicit. Opening guidance, random spawn lock/interval/pool, and jump/hold/duck discovery all derive from the opening-guide state and seeded no-repeat selection bag.

`resetRun` reloads durable high score and lifetime Seeds, preserves only the legitimate persisted record floor, clears run metrics/Bloom/reward/timers/ownership/trackers/opening knowledge/selection state. `save` is persistence-gated: `SaveManager.publishHighScoreIfBetter` atomically arbitrates record ownership; a subsequent durable read reconciles stale local value and NEW HIGH; best distance is saved independently from ghost promotion; Seed balance is reloaded rather than overwritten. Private helpers provide saturating integer increment/add, finite float addition/products, bounded base speed, and normalized multiplier. `addBonus` applies the currently active multiplier to this call's points before installing an optional finite positive `multiplierBoost`; positive finite `durationSec` expires after the complete frame that reaches its deadline, while zero duration lasts until another boost or run reset. Invalid durations do not install a boost, and reset clears its timer.

## `entities/Player.kt` — complete movement, collision body and animation owner

Player's x is fixed from surface width; y/velocity, `PlayerState`, timers and animations own locomotion. Ground Y comes from the parallax floor when valid, else a screen-height fallback. The real Player body is 72×100 with fixed gravity, short/full jump velocity limits, 0.2-second reduced-gravity apex, 0.55 duck height, 10-pixel collision inset, and 0.05-second maximum local update delta. `jumpVelocityForHold` clamps hold into 0..0.6 seconds and linearly maps from short to full upward velocity.

`canStartJump` allows only RUNNING/LANDING; `canStartDuck` includes JUMP_START so an admitted swipe can cancel a jump-start. Jump press launches at full force immediately and emits sound/haptic; release only caps upward speed (never adds energy). Duck press transitions RUNNING/LANDING directly, or cancels JUMP_START and returns to ground; release restores RUNNING. Bloom is an orthogonal `isInvincible` flag, never a locomotion state: activation normalizes kinematics and creates aura/trail emitters; deactivation clears presentation and emitters. REST snaps to ground, clears Bloom and emits death FX. STUMBLE only begins outside REST/non-Bloom.

`update(dt, speed)` rejects invalid/nonpositive delta, caps dt, snapshots prior hitbox before physics, repairs malformed kinematics, advances presentation/state timers, then updates by state: REST freezes; DUCKING maintains foot anchor; STUMBLE recovers after animation/0.8 seconds and applies falling gravity if displaced; deprecated BLOOM migrates back to run/fall; jump phases apply gravity, JUMP_START timeout, APEX reduced gravity/time and FALLING; LANDING returns to RUNNING. Landing emits SFX once on transition. Bloom emitters track Player each frame. Run animation rate maps bounded scroll speed to 24–32 FPS. Current animation, face and costume overlay advance once.

`transitionTo` emits jump dust/landing thud/slide grass at the foot point, changes state/timer/animation, resets animation, pins REST to its final frame, and immediately recomputes collision geometry. `updateHitbox` follows the rendered foot-centered scale: horizontal center/inset are state-aware, top is `feetY-currentHeight+inset`, bottom is `feetY-inset`. This makes accessibility actions and same-tick collision sweeps use the actual new stance rather than interpolating a phantom body. `normalizeKinematics` repairs nonfinite position/velocity and invalid timers. `draw` resolves secondary motion tilt/lift, optional Bloom power aura/scale, rotates/scales around feet, and layers sprite, costume overlay and face. Costume assignment is presentation-only.

## Session transition and collision-owner cluster

### `RunSessionTransitionPlanner`

The planner is pure and treats `AppGameState` and `RunState` as orthogonal coordinates. The event table permits: Menu→Play with fresh-run preparation; Menu→Garden with refresh; Garden→Play with fresh run; Garden→Menu with ritual/copy/menu music; terminal Play→DYING with death effect; DYING→GAME_OVER; Rest tap→RESTARTING; completed restart fade→Garden with run reset, ghost reset/reload, Garden refresh and menu music. Debug PLAYING request is the special state-agnostic, idempotent route; it always plans PLAYING/PLAYING after debug caller has validated payload/prepared state. Other stale or invalid event/state pairs return unchanged, unaccepted no-op. Bloom is absent by design.

### `RunSessionTransitionCoordinator` and live effects

The coordinator asks planner, returns `NO_OP` for unchanged transitions, otherwise applies ordered effects through `RunSessionEffectSink`. Runtime exceptions are contained as failed effects; the first failure stops later effects and produces `EFFECT_FAILED`, so the caller cannot adopt planned state. It does not publish state itself. Earlier effects are not rolled back if a later effect fails, so effects must be safe/idempotent or transitions need caller recovery; this is an ownership property, not a transaction. `mayAdoptAfterState` is accepted route and no effect failure, allowing the special accepted idempotent debug route. `LiveRunSessionEffects` maps each enum to a single injected callback and owns neither state nor planning.

### `CollisionOutcomeDispatcher`

Dispatcher is exhaustive over HIT/STUMBLE/MERCY_MISS/NONE and receives lazy callbacks so only the selected branch samples mutable runtime state. HIT runs immediate impact first, captures after those effects, then completes terminal summary/persistence. STUMBLE and MERCY invoke only their relevant input builders; NONE is ignored. Return type tells GameView whether the run stays active or must transition to death.

### Terminal impact and completion

`TerminalHitImpactCoordinator.apply` owns fixed order: record run hit → suppress ghost 1.35s → Player REST → hit shake → hit SFX → Rest music → terminal long haptic → invoke capture callback. Captured presentation killer must match captured killer type. This preserves post-impact coordinates/ghost detachment.

`TerminalHitOutcomeCoordinator.complete` validates killer identity, records persistent relationship hit only when authorized and known, presents authored HIT dialogue/flavor, obtains one summary preview, resolves a Rest quote from preview/biome/killer, copies quote into immutable completed summary, and calls exactly-once `RunOutcomeCommitter` with summary + detached ghost + persistence authorization. Android adapters isolate relationship, copy and quote owners. The caller stores the returned final summary and transitions to DYING.

### Nonterminal outcomes and effect adapter

Stumble order: record run hit → optional persistent relationship hit → suppress ghost 0.9s → Player stumble → colored flash → nonlethal hit SFX → hit shake → medium haptic → authored stumble text → deactivate entity. Mercy order: green flash → mercy SFX → double-tap haptic → authored mercy copy → stars → mercy shake. `LiveCollisionEffects` maps these narrow interfaces to GameView callbacks and semantic haptics; it contains no branching or ownership order. Android presenters only format/run cue rendering. Data carriers snapshot killer/route/player position/color/persistence and mercy values so effect order can't observe later mutable state.

## Persistence application boundary and terminal commit journal

### `ApplicationPersistenceFacade.kt`

This is a composition boundary, not a cross-store transaction. `ApplicationRunOutcomePort` exposes reset + commit; `ApplicationEncounterPersistence` exposes resolved encounter/pass/hit recording; `ApplicationRecoveryMaintenance` exposes inspect/retry/domain-specific corrupt or pending discard. Android adapters pin `applicationContext` and delegate to existing authorities. The facade additionally owns injected ports for Garden purchase, feedback preference writes and costume equip, and implements terminal/nonterminal relationship recorder interfaces. `android(context)` assembles `RunOutcomePersistenceCoordinator(AndroidRunOutcomePersistenceSink)`, `GardenPurchaseManager`, `FeedbackSettings`, `CostumeManager`, persistent memory and recovery maintenance. It does not merge their storage domains.

### `RunOutcomePersistenceCoordinator.kt`

Commit dispositions distinguish committed, nonpersistent, already committed, recovery pending and blocked. The coordinator synchronizes commit/reset and claims `terminalOutcomeCommitted` before any persistence gate; even a nonpersistent run consumes its token. Only reset reopens it, and reset first retries recovery. Constructor recovery attempt initializes blocked status. Persistent commit prepares and synchronously saves a recoverable before/after record before any ghost admission or state mutation. Invalid/corrupt/unreadable recovery blocks new mutation. Summary distance is normalized; ghost promotion requires distance strictly over the manager's durable-or-pending floor and nonempty detached frames. Ghost work is accepted asynchronously before the journaled non-ghost bundle, but ghost durability is independently owned.

With recoverable sinks, the record contains summary, previous/next forest mood, previous/next return moment, previous/next route count and phase. `ensureMoodState`/`ensureReturnState` accept either exact after-state (already committed pre-crash) or exact before-state (write after and reread); any third state conflicts without overwrite. `ensureSummaryState` also checks exact route counter and uses atomic `RunOutcomeSummarySnapshotStore.save`, because summary save also increments route and cannot safely be replayed as a pair of separate writes. Each applied phase checkpoints the journal; phase-write results after state mutation do not replace before/after state comparisons as recovery truth. Final journal clear is required. Exceptions/conflicts retain evidence and mark recovery blocked/pending. Legacy nonrecoverable sinks use direct ordered mood/return/summary writes.

### `AndroidRunOutcomePersistenceSink`

Captures active preference namespace when constructed and creates namespaced run-outcome recovery/summary stores. Construction also retries pending ghost-promotion recovery. It delegates best distance/async ghost promotion to `GhostPersistenceManager`, mood and return transforms to their domain systems, and summary/highscore/distance to `SaveManager`. Recovery uses explicit state reads/writes and route-tier count. This namespace capture matters: coordinator lifetime is tied to selected persistence namespace.

### `RunOutcomeRecoveryStore.kt`

Schema-v2 journal uses `SharedPreferences` namespace `forest_run_outcome_recovery_<sanitized namespace>`, synchronous `commit()` and a full replacement transaction. `load` returns Empty only without presence marker; wrong schema, missing keys, wrong preference types/ClassCastException, unknown enum, invalid snapshots/summary/count or impossible transition returns Corrupt; otherwise Pending. Summary includes score/distance/new-high/high score, mercy/kindness/pass/spare/hit/Seeds/Bloom metrics, nullable killer sentinel, bounded quote, forest mood and route tier. Mood/return snapshots and route counts are read/written with exact typed keys.

`isValid(record)` does not trust only schema: it checks nonnegative bounded fields and recomputes expected mood, return transition (using recorded resulting timestamp), and route count from previous state + summary. `safeNamespace` admits only alphanumeric/underscore/hyphen, substitutes other characters, caps at 96 and supplies `default`. `RunOutcomeRecoveryTransitions.nextForestMood` updates current mood, streak and total plus exactly one mood counter with saturating arithmetic. `nextReturnMoment` uses authored rough-run predicate and safe saturating streak; it preserves greeting day and sets last-active to supplied completion time. `nextRouteTierCount` leaves NONE unchanged and saturates at `Int.MAX_VALUE/16`. `persistedSummary` normalizes negative counters and nonfinite/negative distance before atomic snapshot.

## Ghost recording, identity, worker scheduling and promotion recovery

### In-memory contract and recording

`GhostFrame` snapshots elapsed seconds, world position, Player state ordinal and squash/stretch scales. `GhostRunValidator` requires 1..MAX_FRAMES, finite monotonic nonnegative bounded times, finite x/y, known state code and scales in 0.1..4. `GhostStateCodec` maps state enums to stable disk codes, reserving legacy Bloom code 7; enum reorder cannot silently reinterpret versioned files. `GhostRecorder` runs only when GameView says the mode records ghosts, samples at 30Hz, bounds to 20 minutes / 36,000 frames, tracks elapsed even between sample instants, and rejects invalid samples. `snapshot` copies; `detachSnapshot` transfers the completed backing `ArrayList` in O(1), installs a fresh buffer and resets clock/count so worker code cannot observe future mutation.

### `GhostRunIdentity`

Canonical SHA-256 bytes are big-endian accepted-distance raw bits, frame count, then each frame's time/x/y raw float bits, state ordinal and scale bits. A historical 64-bit FNV fingerprint remains for diagnostics and v1 receipt/manifest compatibility. `matches` validates structure/count, checks legacy fingerprint when digest absent, and otherwise requires canonical lowercase 64-hex SHA plus matching FNV and distance-bound SHA. SHA is local identity, not authentication. Digest encode/decode is fixed-width 32 bytes/lowercase hex. `GhostRunIdentityTest` pins a golden digest/fingerprint vector, proves changes to every persisted frame field, frame count, or accepted distance change the strong identity (while legacy fingerprint remains frame-only), checks full-byte hex round-trip and rejects uppercase/malformed/wrong-length digests; matching permits legacy fallback only when digest is absent.

### `GhostPersistenceNamespace` and artifact adapter
