The ghost-focused Robolectric cases reviewed here cover v1/v2 artifact manifest and promotion receipt codecs, digest/version/size/trailing-data rejection, AtomicFile cleanup and valid-record preservation; color-matrix channel composition; namespace pending-write registry stale-future cleanup and bounded await semantics; per-namespace serial FIFO with cross-namespace overlap and failed task recovery; candidate admission against stronger accepted runs; namespace capture and bound artifact isolation through active preference changes; malformed-delta playback admission; Ghost reveal/fade, occlusion, dense hazard suppression and recovery, invalid recordings and completion; abandonment behavior retaining strong manifest evidence or upgrading legacy evidence; recorder invalid samples, caps and detached snapshot ownership; strict run frame validation; stable state-code compatibility; and reusable visibility context identity. The corresponding tests (`GhostArtifactManifestStoreTest`, `GhostColorMatrixTest`, `GhostNamespacePendingWriteRegistryTest`, `GhostNamespaceSerialSchedulerTest`, `GhostPersistenceManagerAdmissionTest`, `GhostPersistenceManagerTest`, `GhostPersistenceNamespaceIntegrationTest`, `GhostPlayerMalformedDeltaAdmissionTest`, `GhostPlayerTest`, `GhostPromotionReceiptAbandonmentTest`, `GhostPromotionReceiptStoreTest`, `GhostPromotionRecoveryCoordinatorTest`, `GhostRecorderTest`, `GhostRunValidatorTest`, `GhostStateCodecTest`, `GhostVisibilityContextTest`, and `NamespaceBoundGhostPromotionArtifactStoreTest`) were read individually and not executed. `GhostRunIdentityTest` is detailed above.

`GhostPersistenceManagerTest` uses actual preferences, ghost bytes and sidecars to check immediate in-memory publication followed by durable promotion; recovery from matching strong/legacy receipts and manifests; unwritten candidate abandonment; tampered/corrupt identity blocking distance repair and later promotion without deleting the prior ghost; frame round-trip; truncated/trailing/oversized payload rejection; and invalid frame admission. Teardown awaits namespace writes and clears all artifact sidecars. `GhostPromotionRecoveryCoordinatorTest` uses event-recording memory stores to pin receipt→ghost→manifest→distance→receipt-clear ordering; legacy upgrades; already-applied idempotence; preserving prior valid artifacts when candidate bytes never landed; mismatch rejection; retries after write failures at ghost, manifest or distance stages; receipt-free manifest repair; lazy validation shortcuts; corrupt evidence fail-closed behavior; matching receipt repair of a stale manifest; and strong identity sensitivity to all frame fields and distance. Both files were read fully; not run.

`capture()` reads the active SaveManager preference name once and derives canonical paired filename: primary `forest_run_prefs`/`ghost_run.bin`, compatibility `forest_run_prefs_compat_vN`/`ghost_run_compat_vN.bin`. Unsupported names and unsafe filenames fail. `NamespaceBoundGhostPromotionArtifactStore` binds one explicit SharedPreferences and AtomicFile at construction; future active namespace switches do not redirect it. It reads both current versioned and legacy count/raw-ordinal payloads with strict file size/count/trailing-size and frame validation; writes v2 with stable state codes via AtomicFile finish/fail protocol. It commits distance synchronously. `AndroidGhostPromotionArtifactStore` is a separate SaveManager-based adapter; current manager construction uses the namespace-bound implementation. `NamespaceBoundGhostPromotionArtifactStoreTest` checks v2 header compatibility and round-trip through both bound store and SaveManager, legacy raw ordinal reads, unknown-version/trailing-byte rejection, invalid-candidate preservation, and that lifecycle best distance and ghost-promotion distance advance independently.

### Scheduler, manager and publication semantics

`GhostNamespaceSerialScheduler` gives each immutable namespace a FIFO `SerialExecutor` queue and shares a fixed two-daemon-thread backend, permitting disjoint primary/compat work to overlap. `GhostNamespacePendingWriteRegistry` tracks latest future per namespace; same-namespace serial ordering makes that future represent preceding writes. Recovery blocks only while its own namespace task is active. `awaitAll` uses one monotonic deadline for every namespace and fails on timeout/task exception; exact namespace/future removal prevents an old future clearing a newer registration.

`GhostPersistenceManager.saveBestRunAsync` captures namespace, validates frames/distance, recovers before accepting when idle, rejects candidates below max(durable distance, current accepted in-memory distance), snapshots frames, computes identity, publishes immediately for playback and schedules worker. Worker re-recovers in that namespace, writes promotion bundle, conditionally clears only matching failed publication and records I/O duration/success telemetry. A failed write after the ghost is durable preserves in-memory frames because the artifact may still be recoverable. `loadLatest` returns current namespace publication if present; otherwise recovers and loads its bound artifact, creates distance-bound identity and atomically inserts publication. Test reset waits boundedly for all worker queues to quiesce before clearing state/telemetry.

### `GhostPromotionRecoveryCoordinator`

Persist transaction order: validate → calculate distance-bound identity → write v2 receipt → write ghost → write v2 manifest → commit `max(currentBest,candidateDistance)` → clear receipt. Result reports each durability stage. Receipt v2 is 56 bytes (`FRGP`, version, float distance, frame count, FNV, 32-byte SHA); v1 is 24 bytes and readable only. Invalid/missing/corrupt receipt/manifest or failed I/O blocks new promotion; automatic recovery never deletes evidence.

Recovery first loads receipt. Corrupt receipt blocks. Pending receipt loads/validates the durable ghost against count/FNV and either legacy FNV or distance-bound SHA. A match ensures strong manifest, repairs distance only if below, then clears receipt. A mismatch clears only stale receipt and checks older manifest against the already-loaded ghost; healthy old state is reported as abandoned unwritten ghost. Without receipt, manifest is authority linking artifact and distance: if best already meets manifest distance, automatic recovery can return ALREADY_APPLIED without decoding/hashing frames; if repair is needed, it fully validates frames and upgrades v1 to a strong v2 manifest before advancing distance. `AtomicFileGhostPromotionReceiptStore` validates exact record lengths/header/version/typed ranges, accepts base or `.bak`, and clears base/.bak/.new with postcheck. Namespace-bound manager passes same namespace to receipt, artifact and manifest. The old Android SaveManager adapter captures prefs at creation but delegates ghost reads/writes dynamically, hence it must not be used for the namespace-stable manager path.

## `engine/SaveManager.kt` — SharedPreferences persistence and legacy ghost codec

### Namespace and lock

One reentrant `gardenWriteLock` serializes active preference/ghost namespace changes and Garden currency/progression writes; it also protects high-score compare/write, return/mood transforms and derived counter read-modify-write. Primary prefs/ghost are `forest_run_prefs`/`ghost_run.bin`; compatibility schema N uses `forest_run_prefs_compat_vN`/`ghost_run_compat_vN.bin`. `activePrefsName` and `activeGhostFilename` are separate volatile values; canonical namespace consumers read prefs once and derive ghost filename. A legacy Garden follow-up marker records namespace, thread, timestamp and canonical Seed count to prevent an old two-call progress/currency path from overwriting concurrent currency changes.

### Scores, distance, and Seeds

High score is monotonic: `saveHighScore` delegates to synchronized `publishHighScoreIfBetter`, which performs preference selection + comparison + apply atomically and returns whether this caller raised the record. `loadHighScore` clamps nonnegative. Best run distance and best Ghost distance are separate keys; `saveBestDistance` rejects invalid/nonpositive measurements and writes only if greater than both the legacy ghost floor and current best-run distance. `loadBestDistance` returns their maximum. `awardLifetimeSeeds` reads/adds/saturates/writes under the same lock as Garden purchase; it also updates any pending legacy follow-up's canonical total. Legacy absolute `saveLifetimeSeeds` resolves same-thread stale follow-up versus legitimate concurrent writes using marker age/namespace/thread.

### Ghost frame file

Current file is AtomicFile `ghost_run.bin`/compat counterpart. V2 payload has magic `FRGH`, version 2, count, then 24-byte frames (float t/x/y, stable state code, float scales). Writer validates 1..36k frames, finite monotonic time/position/state/scales and uses start/finish/fail. Reader accepts v2 or legacy count + raw enum ordinal, enforces max file bytes/count/exact payload size before allocating, validates each frame, maps v2 stable code to ordinal and returns empty on corruption/unsupported/truncation. `hasGhostRun` considers committed base or `.bak`, not `.new`; it means recoverable file exists, not that structure is valid. Production ghost manager uses its namespace-bound adapter to bind distance+ghost together.

### Garden, wardrobe, encounter history and terminal summary

`saveGardenProgress` clamps order and treats only one-step advancement as a real atomic purchase using catalogue cost and `GardenPurchaseManager`; it writes requested index directly only for compatibility/recovery cases, and records follow-up ownership for legacy call sequences. Costume unlock saves union-merge, remove NONE sentinel from merged set, parse unknown entries defensively; active/featured costume are individual enum preference writes/reads.

Encounter/pass/spared/hit/clean-pass/kindness/tender and biome friendship counters use typed keys, generic bounded reads, saturating increment under the shared lock; streak reset writes zero. Last killer and last-run summary are separately stored; summary normalizes all numeric counters/distance and records quote, mood and route tier. It then increments a non-NONE route tier counter, so it is not idempotent alone; recovery uses `RunOutcomeSummarySnapshotStore` for atomic summary+expected-route transaction. Mood and ReturnMoment offer lock-protected load/save plus transform APIs that read, apply, write and reread canonical state. Return writes sanitize active/day/streak, including preserving Int.MAX saturation. Memory pages, relationship milestones and history marks use monotonic set-union saves; relationship stage is per-family enum value. Preferences preserve unrelated unknown keys except specific journal `clear` boundary.

Private `prefs(context)` resolves the current active name per operation; `ghostFile` resolves active filename. `boundedDerivedCounter` rejects wrong stored types and clamps to `Int.MAX_VALUE/16`; `incrementInt` serializes read-modify-write to avoid lost concurrent events.

### `GhostArtifactManifest.kt`

Manifest is the durable association remaining after transient receipt clear. `FRGM` v2 has the same 56-byte identity structure as receipt, v1 24-byte FNV records remain readable, only strong v2 records can be newly saved. Store is AtomicFile-scoped to one supplied ghost filename, recognizes base/backup recovery, rejects invalid size/header/version/distance/frame-count/digest, writes finish/fail safely, and explicit clear removes base, `.bak`, `.new` with a postcondition. Corruption is reported, not auto-erased.

### `GhostPlayer.kt`

Player copies only a validated detached frame list; `reset` clears timeline, pose, fades, wave and suppression. `update` requires active and finite-positive dt, advances bounded playback time, decrements explicit HIT suppression and dense-hazard suppression, handles final 0.8-second wave/fade, binary-searches last frame at or before time, and activates brief dense suppression if at least two nearby hazards are within 1.65 live-player widths. Alpha target combines reveal delay (1.15s unless immediate), 0.65s fade-in, proximity/overlap between ghost and live Player, nearest hazard distance and count crowding. Visibility context must have finite positive player dimensions and nonnegative count/distance; invalid context hides the ghost. Alpha approaches target asymmetrically (fade-out 8.5/s, fade-in 3.1/s). `suppress` extends, never shortens, timed suppression.

Drawing is before live Player, with 40% maximum alpha, cached desaturated white-blue color matrix, exact state-selected sprite sheet, recorded scale and reusable rectangles. Draw geometry is computed in Double and rejected unless all coordinates/pivot fit Float. Finished wave turns inactive; caller owns sparkle effect. Final pose remains available through `lastX/lastY` for that presentation. This is visual best-run playback and should not be confused with simulation, collision or progress authority.

## `engine/RelationshipArcSystem.kt` — persistent relationship state and encounter voice

Complete 1,434-line read. This is a stateless presentation/query facade over `PersistentMemoryManager`: it defines tracked species (Cat, Fox, Wolf, Dog, Owl, Eagle), stage/tone/strain/warmth projections, stage refresh and milestone reward coordination, then dispatches authored event/cue lines by species. It does not own the durable counters. Its public entrypoints separate stage refresh (which may persist a newly reached stage and grant an idempotent milestone) from stage lookup and from line composition. Callers that need a pure read should use the read path; stage resolution has a write-capable path. `FamiliarityWarmthScoring` supplies the familiarity warmth calculation.

Stage progression is evidence-gated: familiarity/pass exposure alone is capped at Recognition; positive encounter outcomes are required for Trust and higher, with stage thresholds and family-specific predicates. Tone is projected from remembered pass/spare/hit/strain counters; strained consequence is selected from repeated negative evidence rather than treated as a permanent species trait. The milestone catalogue supplies one-time rewards and copy; milestone refresh coordinates durable stage and reward updates through the persistence owner. Choice/featured relationship helpers provide deterministic candidates from tracked families, while tuning constants keep stage and warmth thresholds centralized.

Line composition accepts event (`PASS`, `SPARE`, `THREAT`, `RETURN`) or encounter cue (e.g. mercy, Fox landing, Wolf charge, Dog greeting/middle/farewell, Owl alert, Eagle lock), resolves current stage/tone/warmth/strain and relevant counters, and chooses an authored branch. Species are intentionally distinct: Cat uses quiet/familiarity and suddenness; Fox frames learned tricks/lines; Wolf frames keeping calm and fear; Dog uses enthusiastic return/pace language; Owl tracks repeated-hit shadow memory; Eagle uses marked lines and fear. Cue handlers prioritize bonded/personal warmth, then warm stage and counters, then strain/caution, then recognition/first-impression fallback. This ordering can cause a more specific strain branch to override otherwise friendly lower-priority copy. Fallback cue events delegate to the species' threat or pass event. The remainder of the source is primarily authored branching copy, so exact text is intentionally not duplicated here.

## Narrative, return, and long-horizon history cluster

`SessionArcComposer` is a pure copy composer over the last persisted `RunSummary`, `GardenSanctuaryPlanner`, relationship and history projections. Menu copy precedence picks a featured state for atmosphere, supporting atmosphere, home sign and idle/standing/ready prompts; a first run has explicit Quiet Start copy. Rest copy uses a *preview* of return moments, sanctuary state and summary to produce recovery title/subtitle/recovery line/carry-home copy; it does not acknowledge the moment. Garden arrival suppresses its fallback when a `ReturnMoment` is supplied, then chooses sanctuary copy or mood/route-specific fallback. This composition order is important because higher priority featured signals suppress lower-priority signals.

`PostRunReflectionPlanner` reduces candidate post-run prose into unique, ordered entries by trimmed lowercase text and caps Garden to three. Rest chooses route, world, home, then recovery candidates and returns the first. Garden priority changes when route exists (route first) or creature thought exists (creature first); otherwise reflection/weather lead. It uses exact normalized text dedupe, not semantic similarity.

`ReturnMomentsSystem` writes rough-run streak and last-active time at run outcome, and maintains a separate once-per-local-calendar-day greeting cursor. Preview is read-only; `resolveGardenMoment` builds the candidate then acknowledges it, while the explicit acknowledge API exists for callers that know presentation was emitted. The chooser has strict ordered precedence: long absence (36 hours) variants first; repeated killer/harm and strained-bond pressure; repeated-harm carryover; high-quality peaceful/milestone/mercy/clean/Bloom/friend branches; rough streak; then ordinary kindness/clean/high score/spare/afterglow/daily greeting. Because it returns the first matching branch, ordering prevents a weaker generic moment from shadowing a higher specificity. It uses persisted relationship and encounter counters, last-run summary, route/mood, biome peace and repeat-friend features. The remainder is extensive authored per-species copy for missed/return, harm, kindness, route, Bloom and clean-run cases.

`ForestMoodSystem.classifyRun` has ordered classification: spare or high-heart hitless run → Gentle; short, repeatedly hit run with few clean passes → Fearful; multi-conversion Bloom or hit+seed+score pattern → Reckless; sufficient kindness/clean/distance → Steady; otherwise hit → Fearful else Steady. `recordRun` updates saturating total/streak/per-mood counters through SaveManager; dominant mood is a deterministic max with enum ordinal tie-break. Colors and short labels live on `ForestMood`.

`ForestJournalComposer` is explicitly read-only and projects canonical encounter profiles plus persisted encounter/pass/spare/hit counts, relationship stage, biome/variant metadata and authored lore. For a missing legacy relationship stage it displays First Impression without calling mutating relationship refresh. Aggregate counts, memory-page count, unlock marks, strongest relationship and peaceful biome marks are composed into a validated snapshot. Strongest relationship sorts by stage, spare-minus-hit balance, clean passes, encounters, then entity ordinal.

`ForestCollectionProgressComposer` derives Journal tracks, milestones, relationships, wardrobe, and story pages from existing progression owners. It validates uniqueness and bounds; bonds are discovered tracked families at Milestone, Garden and Costume progress comes from their existing managers, biome peace from friendship marks, and route totals from SaveManager. Journal relationship tone deliberately reimplements a read-only projection from kindness/tender streak and spare/hit counts; milestone details come from `RelationshipArcSystem`. Story page IDs come from `StoryFragmentSystem`, then stable presentation categories/titles/copy are generated by `ForestMemoryPagePresenter`; `ForestMemoryPageNarrative` is a secondary prose resolver for persisted page identifiers. `ForestPathHistoryComposer` maps the three non-NONE route tiers to persisted route counts and copy; `ForestGardenHistoryComposer` makes canonical GROWN/NEXT/LOCKED entries and current affordability from Garden catalogue and lifetime Seeds. `ForestGardenHistoryTest` checks initial, threshold, completed, and corrupt-persistence projections plus the affordable-state invariant. `ForestCompletionCapstoneComposer` derives completion from all collection tracks plus seeing all route shapes; it persists nothing, so catalogue growth changes its target automatically.

`PacifistTracker` is per-run, with saturating clean/spare/hit counters plus biome-local clean/spare/hit state and a bounded 16-reward queue. Biome friendship reward is emitted on leaving a biome after at least three clean passes and no hit in that biome; run rewards trigger every fifth clean pass and every second spare. Route reward tier is monotonic within a run and queued only when capacity allows, so queue saturation does not advance the rewarded-tier latch. Route thresholds are ordered Peaceful, Merciful, Kind, None and account for hit limit, hearts, kindness chain, clean passes and spares.

### Persistent memory and story unlock path

`PersistentMemoryManager` is the canonical cross-run encounter wrapper over SaveManager. Encounter/pass/spare/hit mutations refresh history unlock state and tracked relationship stage. Spare and hit compound transitions execute under SaveManager's Garden currency lock: spare increments spare and kindness streak while clearing tender streak; hit increments hit and tender streak while clearing kindness streak and publishing killer. History marks are monotonic IDs derived from per-family kindness/tender margins, repeat-hit count, clean-pass-vs-hit count, and biome friendship; only changed sets are rewritten. Feature selectors rank warm/tender by streak then margin then enum order; repeat killer by hits then severity; clean pass by margin, passes, enum; biome by friendship then canonical biome order. `repeatedHistorySnapshot` aggregates those projections without creating another authority.

`StoryFragmentSystem` distinguishes mutating presentation from preview: `restQuote`, Garden reflection, creature thought, and weather thought can persist page unlocks; their `preview*` forms compute copy without writes. Rest fragment selection is precedence ordered: peaceful complete route, merciful route, hitless clean route, repeated killer, strained tracked killer, any killer, repeat friend, warm creature, then biome-specific fallback. It unlocks the selected fragment page and contextual rest pages for biome/mood/route/clean pattern/Bloom/killer. Garden reflection prioritizes cactus-clean history, gentle milestone relationship, merciful friend, clean repeat friend, strained bond, warm clean/gentle history, Kind/Peaceful/Merciful route, strongest bond/trust, peace-biome composition, repeated harm, gentle/clean/Bloom/high-score, then mood fallback. Garden context pages are separately unlocked for mood/route/peace biome/warmth/repeat killer/Bloom. Creature thought first delegates to relationship copy, then species-specific pass/hit fallback; writes species, family, learned-pass and caution-hit pages. Weather thought combines mood with route, peace biome, strongest/repeat/strained/milestone relationship, repeated harm and Bloom, with matching durable weather-page unlocks. Page writes are set-add then persist only when newly added.

## Encounter planning, fairness, and Garden economy

`EncounterDirector` owns the authored deterministic scenario catalogue (scenario metadata, optional forced biome/Bloom/ghost policy, timed typed steps, variant and offset), wraps selected/active index/time/step cursor, and emits every due step once. Per-step seeds use a version salt plus scenario name hash and step index, independent of update partitioning and previous random draws. `EncounterFamilyCatalogue` supplies the exhaustive 19-family identity/group/source mapping and explicit ownership dimensions; derived profiles query existing biome pools, scenario steps, variant definitions and relationship tracking, then validate catalogue order and full coverage. `EncounterSelectionBag` shuffles each distinct active pool, emits each member once before refill, and avoids immediate repeat across refill when possible; pool changes reset the bag.

`DifficultyScaler` sanitizes distance and delegates gap curve to `ReadabilityProfile`; it supplies an early/mid/late pool unless biome rotation is authoritative. `SpawnPacing.requiredGapPx` converts default gap to interval, applies the opening guide, converts back to distance, and never drops below base readability gap. `minimumRandomEncounterLeadPx` additionally models source-side gesture/safety reaction, maximum immediate approach multiplier, sampled full-jump rise, and one admitted frame; its code comments explicitly distinguish this geometric origin lead from measured human/device timing. `SpawnFairnessEnvelope` is a pure report over those production functions and validates declared bounds, not full entity/recovery feasibility. `EncounterActionFeasibility` uses the same 50 ms semi-implicit Player update to derive sampled maximum rise and earliest sampled clearance. Inputs are sanitized for diagnostics, but malformed input returns both actions infeasible; finite double arithmetic, stable root, near-integer snap and direct sampled-height validation avoid a continuous-parabola false positive.

`SeedOrbSpawnPolicy` bounds all randomized vertical Orb offsets inside both screen and a conservative fraction of full-jump reach, accounts for bob/radius and response-time horizontal approach (including random x jitter), then rejects any unresolved encounter in the player-to-farthest-Orb corridor without trusting a static vertical separation. This intentionally treats a possible lure through moving hazard geometry as unsafe.

`GardenEconomy` is the stable nine-entry catalogue and sole order/name/cost source. `GardenPurchaseManager` locks on SaveManager's currency mutex, accepts only the exact next index and canonical price, checks completion/funds, and commits unlocked count plus lifetime Seeds atomically. The compatibility overload rejects any caller-supplied price/size mismatch. Its result differentiates invalid, out-of-order, complete, insufficient and commit failure, preserving pre-write values on failure. `GardenPurchaseInteractionCoordinator` emits growth feedback only on successful result. `GardenSanctuaryPlanner` is a read-only projection combining mood, route, relationship and milestone state, repeated-history snapshot, story page count, world opinion, costume presentation, peace biomes and Garden atmosphere. It derives prioritized traces, arrival badge, sanctuary[local absolute path omitted] lines, up to five deduplicated consequences and atmosphere quantities; visual ownership remains elsewhere. Its first-match precedence makes strain/repeated-harm/route/milestone signals supersede generic home mood.

## Biome, readability, input, and presentation support

`Biome` is the ordered five-biome cycle and owns palettes, ambient factor and preferred encounter pools; `at` sanitizes distance and resolves segment by fixed biome length. `BiomeManager` advances the current biome, crossfades from 80% through segment end, blends ARGB channels and unions adjacent spawn pools during transition; debug force bypasses distance. `ReadabilityProfile` holds per-entity geometry/cue/movement templates, scales dimensions by three screen-height buckets, estimates screen height from ground position, and defines monotonic bounded spawn-origin gap. `OpeningReadabilityGuide` applies a 6.75-second random-spawn lockout, early interval floors through a 28-second guided window, fixed tutorial pool, and priority ordered input/route cues.

`SafeContentTransform` preserves the full logical surface coordinate system but scales/centers it into the inset-safe rectangle; mappings clamp finite/infinite coordinates. `SurfaceResizePolicy` forces Activity recreation when already initialized systems are dimension-bound and valid old/new dimensions differ. `FrameInputAdmission` rejects nonfinite/nonpositive deltas and invalid speed, then bounds accepted frames to 50ms and speed to game maximum. `LatestRequestGate` uses object identity tokens to invalidate stale asynchronous retries without numeric wrap. `RunMode` is the policy boundary for persistent progress, ghost writes, random spawns, ordinary cues and default ghost playback; all named scenario modes are deterministic and reject NORMAL input. `RunState` owns playing/dying/game-over/restarting flow; AppGameState owns menu/garden/playing, with deprecated Bloom/Rest compatibility values. `RunSummary` is the canonical detached post-run payload.

`InputHandler` owns one primary touch pointer. It holds an undecided press through a 75ms arbitration window; downward displacement over 80px before jump start becomes duck; a held touch starts jump after delay and receives per-frame hold callbacks; a quick up classifies, presses and releases as a tap. Extra fingers do not steal ownership; primary pointer loss/up/cancel releases appropriately, with cancellation never inventing a tap. Gesture classification and gameplay callback timestamps feed input-latency telemetry. `cancelActiveGesture` clears pending latency state and only emits duck release on explicit request.

`PacifistPresentation` maps route-history counts to world-level remembered route states, gives ordered mercy-miss copy/color variants, converts reward kinds into HUD cues, and supplies route afterglow lines. `WorldOpinionPresentation.current` selects a single precedence-ranked world stance from repeated harm/shadow, strained bond, peace, established route, warmth/repeat friendship, then sustained mood. The four small `*EncounterFlavor` functions are pure authored-copy helpers, choosing cue text from family counters or successful read properties (e.g. flap spread/beat/answer); entity implementations own when they invoke them.

## Run cue presentation and entity contracts

`RunFlavorPresentation` translates progress kind/current tier into concise colored cues; mercy cues select entity-specific prompts using relationship copy, family flavor and hit history; pass cues choose relationship, bird/animal/plant/tree copy and tier palette; pass bubble alternatives prioritize warm tracked bond then other tracked bond, route tier, and family. Collision cues prioritize repeat hit, strained tracked bond, generic tracked relationship, then family-specific default; fatal vs stumble controls palette and route modifies generic bubble. Milestone presentation prioritizes new high score, unlocked relationship milestone reward, world opinion, route tier, then score/default, with per-species milestone colors. These functions are presentation-only.

`Entity` defines the mutable encounter contract: primary collision rect, previous physical core/motion sample, aggregate `encounterBounds` override for multi-part encounters, active/pass/outcome state, persistence opt-out for showcase runs, and pending mercy contact. Outcomes are single-instance terminal vocabulary. `onCollision` is an abstract pure query; telegraphs/mechanics advance in `updatePlayerInteraction`, mechanics/presentation happen only after arbitration in `onOutcomeSelected`, and exclusive safe departures can override ordinary clean pass accounting through `resolveSpecialSafeDeparture`. Allocation-free expanded hit tests validate rectangles/padding and compare in Double. `CollisionResult` differentiates none, terminal hit, pending mercy-band contact and nonterminal stumble. `PlayerState` ordinals are persisted in ghost frames; reserved legacy BLOOM remains for compatibility while real Bloom is orthogonal.

`EntityFactory` sanitizes screen size/start coordinates and constructs all 19 concrete implementations with copies of shared sprite sheets, passing seeded randomness where mechanics require it. It controls Bamboo eligibility based on full gap staging geometry; ordinary pools omit unstageable Bamboo while explicit attempts fail at spawn admission. Dog default variant delegates buddy chance to persisted relationship history; explicit hazard/buddy variants override that random choice. `EntityType` order is canonical and cross-cutting (catalogue/serialization/report order).

## Asset loading, animation strips, and wearable presentation

`AssetPaths` is the central packaged asset path table for runner strips and the 19 encounter families. `RuntimeAssetValidator` is a release-only fail-fast preflight: verifies the pixel font, sprite and raw-resource presence/nonempty streams, then uses bounds-only decode to reject empty/oversized sprite sheets before full allocation. Bloom accent audio is optional because SFX code has fallbacks. `SpriteManager` loads exact frame-count strips into sheets, subdivides shared runner jump art into jump-start/ascent/apex/fall/landing plus menu-only stand-up ranges, uses obvious generated placeholders only in debuggable builds, and throws on missing/malformed release sprite. Bird strips are copied to ARGB and edge flood-filled independently per frame using top-left seed color/tolerance; frame bounds prevent crossing between cells. Tree and ordinary entity strips get fixed FPS presets. `SpriteSheet` validates strip geometry, caches source rect and paint, updates in O(1) across huge delta by whole-frame modulo/fractional credit, freezes nonlooping final frames, rejects invalid timing/draw rectangles, supports reset/frame seek and cheap logical copy sharing bitmap. `SpriteSizing` preserves aspect ratio with finite lower bounds.

`CostumeStyle` ordinals/catalogue include Classic plus seven unlockable styles. `CostumeManager.refreshUnlocks` evaluates canonical unlock predicates, removes NONE from stored unlocked set, persists newly unlocked styles and makes the last new one featured, then repairs invalid equipped style. Costume rules are three family-spare-or-bond unlocks, three creature Bond unlocks, and Bloom ribbon via distance or lifetime Seeds. `equip` refuses unavailable style and updates active/featured. Presentation projection selects featured, active or highest-ordinal unlocked and returns separate sign/active copy. `CostumeOverlay` is vector-only with reusable paints/path; finite body/motion input is required/sanitized, delta bounded to 50ms, and seven style-specific layers are drawn from body-relative geometry with motion/duck/Bloom color variants. `PlayerSecondaryMotion` maps locomotion state, velocity, elapsed phase and invincibility to bounded tilt/lift/swing/trail/head offsets; reserved Bloom enum keeps legacy behavior.

`FaceManager` owns a wrapped blink clock and reusable paints. It derives eye shape/color and mouth expression from locomotion, velocity and Bloom/invincibility, rejects unusable body geometry and dispatches small drawing helpers for smile/frown/flat/open/Bloom arc.

## Application lifecycle, Journal UI, and frame loop

`MainActivity` locks landscape/fullscreen/keep-screen-on, repairs typed persistence and handles debug recovery commands before constructing `GameView`, initializes feedback and release asset validation, wires safe insets and recovery dialog, and posts debug launch only after Surface readiness. Configuration changes are compared in dp and dimension-bound initialized runtime requests full Activity recreation. `singleTask` intents supersede stale queued debug requests using `LatestRequestGate`; readiness retries are bounded (100ms × 150), and READY logging is emitted only after the view reports the exact scenario/mode. Production rejects debug/capture launches. Recovery inspect is debug-only; mutation is allowed only at initial creation, actions are one-shot intent extras, and valid domain parsing is required. Lifecycle pause/resume avoids duplicate initial thread start; destruction cancels retries, dialogs, GameView and global audio/haptics.

`RecoveryEvidenceDialogCoordinator` consumes only privacy-safe rows from the recovery controller; it never displays raw journal/ghost payloads or exception text. It offers safe retry or a second-confirmed destructive discard, then moves through remaining actionable rows; failures get generic unchanged-data text. `RecoveryEvidenceDialogCoordinatorTest` drives retry and destructive UI clicks through Robolectric’s main looper, verifies discard requires its follow-up confirmation, checks raw detail privacy, and covers clean-state dismissal. `ForestJournalActivity` repairs persistence on creation, restores selected section, snapshots read-only projections on resume, and builds accessible native scrollable sections for run legacy, collection tracks/capstone, Garden, routes, milestones, wardrobe, bonds, memory marks/pages and encounter families. It does not mutate progression and explicitly states purchases happen only in Garden. Filters rerender one selected section; family cards hide undiscovered details and display authoritative counters, biome/variant profile, and relationship stage.

`GameThread` runs update then render at 60Hz target cadence, clamps frame delta to 50ms, records update/render/processing times, records input-to-frame telemetry only for production Surface posts, reports and stops on update/render exceptions, and boundedly joins on shutdown while preserving caller interrupt. Surface rendering always unlocks in `finally`; post failures count as render failures. `RunResetManager` owns 1.2s DYING and 0.5s restart-fade timers, saves on death, bounds finite positive deltas, and at completed reset clears GameState, entities, Player, UI copy, particles and camera.

## Accessibility semantic tree and Android provider

`GameAccessibilitySemantics` validates an immutable snapshot then emits stable node IDs and explicit focus order for Menu, Settings, Playing, Garden and Rest. It enforces numeric bounds, Classic availability and equipped-costume availability; Garden purchase activation exists only for next plant when cost/funds permit. `GameAccessibilityGeometry` ties settings/Garden bounds to their existing layout planners and uses generous normalized semantic regions for other controls. Bounds are logical until GameView maps them through safe-content transform.

`GameAccessibilityActionRouter` rebuilds the live tree per action and fails closed for malformed semantics, stale/missing or disabled nodes, unsupported semantic action, and runtime handler errors. `LiveGameAccessibilityActions` independently matches each stable node/action to its typed owner (session, input, preference, purchase or equip); a successful Garden purchase alone emits growth feedback. `AccessibilityGameplayActionAvailabilityTracker` ignores animation transitions that do not change jump/duck capability. `AccessibilityAnnouncementPolicy` announces surface changes immediately, Bloom transitions and Garden/settings changes at priority, and coalesces run updates into new 100m buckets with a 10s rate limit.

`GameAccessibilityNodeProvider` rebuilds semantics on every query, publishes virtual node descriptions, checkable settings toggles and live run status; integer rectangles are clipped outward into valid host bounds. It maps Android click only to a single semantic action; Journal navigation is a validated special case to native `ForestJournalActivity`. Focus is identity-tracked and focus/content events are emitted only while Android accessibility is enabled. Runtime action routing remains deterministic if service is off. Content mutations/transition owners must publish semantic-tree changes; provider only emits click/focus events. `toRect` floors left/top and ceils right/bottom to avoid shrinking targets.

## World rendering, Bloom visuals, particles, and Seed Orbs

`ParallaxBackground` creates four deterministic 2×-wide procedural bitmap layers and scrolls them at 0.10/0.30/1.0/1.5 speed fractions. Biome profile selects sun/moon, ridge and ground accent, with deterministic seeded layer art; biome scene swaps rebuild while preserving layer offset. Live palette tints are stored and dynamic atmosphere adds biome wash, shadow, wind ribbons, drifting leaf/petal layers, mist, fireflies and motes. Atmosphere derives density/scales from speed ratio, sky luminance and Bloom. Reusable lighting/profile/paint/color arrays limit per-frame allocation. Dynamic gradient shaders rebuild only when quantized RGB inputs change; Bloom visual state separately eases toward active target, tracks activation/afterglow, draws horizon/floor/orb/ray treatment. `ParallaxLayer` wraps modulo bitmap width in O(1), ignores malformed/reverse updates and draws two adjacent copies. `CameraSystem` uses squared trauma envelope, random phase, decays at fixed rate and honors reduced-motion; Canvas save/restore is protected by `finally`. `SwayComponent` is a bounded sine phase with validated fixed parameters and capped wind multiplier. `LightingIdentityProfile` derives reusable run light colors from night/Bloom and stable MENU/GARDEN/REST identities. `CinematicPolish` is pure per-scene profile derivation plus cached gradient overlay rendering (vignette, edge glow, center lift, letterboxes, reduced-motion shimmer). `SanctuaryAtmosphere` explicitly adds independent mood/bond/route/history modifiers, then clamps visual alpha and nonnegative counts.

`BloomPresentation` reuses a HUD presentation object and changes text only when cached discrete inputs change; mode priority is active, afterglow, ready, charging. `BloomPowerPresentation` maps burst conversion tiers/time/recent surge to bounded scale/aura/surge strength. `BloomWorldReaction` has authored per-family copy and only reacts once per reference identity in active Bloom; eligibility uses forward and vertical band. `BloomPresentationAdmission` clamps visual-only strength.

`ParticleEmitter` validates configuration, randomizes each particle from ranges, caps configured count and per-update continuous catch-up, drops whole backlog while retaining fractional emission time. `Particle` is a pooled fail-closed value object: birth snapshots reduced-motion policy, invalid kinematics kill it, reduced motion retires only already-active full-motion births, and drag/gravity/position/spin advance with finite saturation. `ParticleManager` uses a 512-slot circular pool with no allocation in acquisition/draw/update paths, owns continuous emitters on the GameThread, caches one-shot preset emitters, and marshals cross-thread one-shots through a 32-entry primitive queue that drops oldest on overflow. Named `FxPreset` entries author every effect configuration. Reset clears particles, emitters, queued effects and telemetry.

`SeedOrbManager` caps at six, applies 95% base spawn chance times supplied multiplier, sanitizes RNG output and reports workload. Collection claims award particle/Seed/SFX/haptic exactly once, removing inactive Orbs in place. `SeedOrb` stores previous and current centers, moves with world scroll, sinusoidally bobs, expires at six seconds/offscreen, and draws halo/core/highlight with Bloom tint. Pickup uses synchronized swept player+Orb core overlap when both Player samples exist; stationary fallback uses rectangle intersection or a slab sweep against inflated player bounds, requiring common time interval on both axes.

## Audio, comfort settings, cadence, and telemetry

`FeedbackSettings` persists three independent comfort switches, repairs mistyped SharedPreferences values, publishes volatile runtime state, and applies side effects immediately: reduced motion clears camera trauma; disabling audio stops SFX and transitions music ownership; disabling haptics cancels current vibration. Reduced-motion particle counts become 35% (minimum one), shimmer becomes static. `HapticManager` centralizes preference-aware pulse/waveform APIs with old semantic aliases, SDK branching, best-effort service lookup/call, cancellation and release. `SoundSampleReadiness` tracks sample IDs by pool generation so delayed callbacks cannot ready IDs in a replacement pool.

`SfxManager` owns SoundPool, generation-safe load callbacks, required vs optional sample IDs, optional fallback choice, and a bounded recent-stream ledger (SoundPool has no completion callback). Audio-disabled/not-ready requests no-op; stream IDs are stopped on pause/disable/destroy. Bloom cue profiles vary volume/rate by capped conversion count. `LeitmotifManager` serializes MediaPlayer state under one lock and crossfades on a daemon generation-owned thread. It maps distance to three run tracks, uses nonlooping Bloom/Rest, caches prior profile/signature, throttles playback parameter changes to 10Hz unless conversion/state transition forces update, and stops/releases prior players safely. Tempo, volume and motif parameters derive from scroll speed, remaining Bloom time and conversions; music effects are best effort where codecs do not support playback params. `LocalDayClock` uses local calendar year ×400 + day-of-year to avoid adjacent-year collision.

`RuntimeCadenceClock` advances only valid deltas with saturation and retains modulo overshoot for half-second accessibility polls; long accumulated intervals collapse to one poll, avoiding catch-up bursts. `EvaluationThrottle` is a monotonic no-allocation gate, supports force and clock reset, and relies on caller synchronization. `FramePerformanceMonitor.record` writes primitive values to fixed ring buffers and cumulative counters; snapshot copies/sorts only out of the hot path, computes means and ceil-rank percentiles, heap and slow-frame evidence, using bounded sequence-lock retries. `FramePerformanceTelemetry` preserves monitor identity for already-created threads and resets workload/Ghost I/O side telemetry. `FramePerformanceReport` validates all snapshot/workload/ghost counters and writes dependency-free escaped JSON.

`RuntimeWorkloadTelemetry` publishes current/peak manager counts with a volatile sequence lock; snapshot retries three times and normalizes pair invariants on fallback. `InputLatencyTelemetry` uses a 256-sample ring and one pending touch→decision→gameplay-response record. Invalid order, interruption, superseded input and cancellation increment/drop or clear pending; only the next successfully posted production frame closes a sample. It records app-process phase percentiles, explicitly not touch-to-photon. `InputLatencyReport` validates metadata/order/count bounds and serializes escaped JSON; hardware/panel latency remains outside this app instrumentation boundary.

## JVM/Robolectric test review

`ParticleEmitterCacheTest` covers per-preset one-shot emitter reuse with refreshed origins, cache independence across presets and continuous-preset owner timers, plus foreign-thread requests queued until owner update. `ParticleEmitterTest` validates every authored preset's sampled output, fractional interval accumulation, bounded catch-up/backlog drop, invalid delta/stopped behavior, finite origin setter, and constructor rejection of zero count, reversed lifetime and amplifying drag. `ParticleTest` pins invalid lifetime terminal state, invalid-delta no-op, nonfinite kinematic deactivation, extreme finite saturation, no integration beyond lifetime, reduced-motion retirement only for particles born before the setting toggle, continued animation for new births while enabled, and complete pool reset defaults. `SeedOrbManagerTest` checks invalid spawn rejection, exact six-Orb capacity, failed chance preserving capacity and reset. `SeedOrbTest` tests max-speed tunneling prevention and synchronized swept collision windows for moving Player and Orb, malformed historical geometry, separated vertical lane and diagonal-union false positives, lifetime expiry, finite spawn contract, terminal one-time collection and invalid motion no-op. All five files were read; none executed.

UI tests read in the current pass pin fail-closed finite-coordinate handling in debug overlay, feedback panel, Garden and menu input; authored debug actions and layout containment; feedback chip separation, minimum surfaces and malformed-rectangle rejection; bounded rotating dialogue variants, grapheme-safe wrapping/truncation, fixed key/bubble caps, and cached text measurement; bounded/refreshable flavor text; garden layout geometry and purchase/costume adoption, delayed particle effect and first-draw-only return moment acknowledgment; HUD/game-over/menu frame-delta caps, invalid-delta no-ops and poisoned-clock repair; deterministic context-sensitive rest quote safety and robust selection for extreme integer summaries. Individually inspected UI/utility files are `DebugEncounterOverlayTest`, `DialogueBubbleManagerTest`, `DialogueUnicodeWrappingTest`, `FeedbackSettingsPanelLayoutTest`, `FiniteCoordinateAdmissionTest`, `FlavorTextManagerTest`, `GameOverScreenTimeBoundaryTest`, `GardenLayoutPlannerTest`, `GardenScreenBoundaryTest`, `GardenScreenTest`, `GraphemeClustersTest`, `HudTimeBoundaryTest`, `MainMenuScreenInputTest`, `MainMenuScreenTest`, `MainMenuScreenTimeBoundaryTest`, `RestQuoteCatalogueInvariantTest`, `RestQuoteManagerTest`, and `BitmapHelperTest`. The grapheme suite covers surrogate pairs, combining marks, variation selectors, emoji modifiers/ZWJ families, regional indicator pairing, CRLF boundaries and UTF-16 validity; BitmapHelper covers valid placeholder-strip geometry, minimum frame bounds, invalid inputs, multiplication overflow and rejection of excessive pixel allocation. All were read; none run.

Production Kotlin coverage now reconciles to all 190 source files. All 238 JVM/Robolectric files have been read; none of the tests have been executed.

`CollisionOutcomeDispatcherTest` uses injected recording ports to verify branch isolation and event order for HIT, STUMBLE, MERCY_MISS and NONE. HIT calls the lazy terminal-impact capture, then exactly one summary preview and the complete terminal coordinator order (impact effects, relationship/feedback, summary, quote, commit), returns a terminal result with resolved quote, and never builds nonterminal input or deactivates stumble entity. STUMBLE captures only stumble data, orders record/relationship/suppress/stumble/flash/hit/shake/haptic/feedback, then deactivates; it does not capture terminal/mercy or build summary. Mercy captures only mercy input and orders flash/sound/double-tap/feedback/stars/shake without deactivation. NONE leaves all suppliers and effects untouched. The fixture uses recording fakes and fixed summary/ghost data; these are assertions encoded in source, not executed evidence.

`TerminalHitOutcomeCoordinatorTest` verifies five focused contracts: persistent known killer order and single summary build; nonpersistent path skips relationship persistence but still presents, quotes and consumes a commit token; null killer does not invent relationship memory; presentation object coordinates/route pass through unchanged; contradictory killer identity throws before any side effect. `RecordingCommitter` captures exact final summary and ghost list; helper builders create a stable two-frame Ghost and immutable summary. These unit tests establish coordinator-local behavior only, not Android persistence or full runtime integration.

`NonTerminalCollisionOutcomeCoordinatorTest` checks persistent stumble canonical side-effect order; nonpersistent stumble local effects without relationship write; unknown killer suppression of relationship memory; mercy ordering plus center calculation from Player width/height; and verbatim pass-through of even NaN/infinite/extreme presentation coordinates. Local recording effects and presenter fakes assert both sequence and captured values, including deactivation after stumble presentation. These are source-level unit assertions only and were not run.

`TerminalHitImpactCoordinatorTest` verifies immediate effect order before lazy terminal capture, that an exception at `playHit` prevents later cues/capture, and that a capture rejects mismatched killer identity. `CollisionRuntimeEffectAdaptersTest` checks each adapter delegates the expected terminal and nonterminal port methods in caller order, including mapping haptics to long pulse, medium pulse, and double tap. `RunSessionTransitionPlannerTest` covers menu/Garden launches, menu-to-Garden, complete death/rest/restart-to-Garden flow, Garden back order, debug playing-state requests over all snapshots, and exhaustive invalid ordinary state/event pairs as no-ops. `LiveRunSessionEffectsTest` checks every effect dispatch port, that effect exceptions stop execution and prevent state adoption, and that the planner alone owns effect ordering. Tests use deterministic synthetic snapshots and fakes and remain unexecuted.

`RunOutcomePersistenceIntegrationTest` uses Robolectric application preferences and real Android persistence adapters. Setup/teardown clear preference, ghost, promotion, manifest and in-memory state, and await pending ghost writes. It asserts first terminal commit publishes summary/progression plus a promoted ghost/manifest, duplicate commit cannot replace the original outcome, empty ghost does not advance distance, shorter later ghost cannot replace the accepted best, and a nonpersistent commit cannot be retroactively persisted. It verifies manifest identity and 56-byte file length as well as recovery reporting already-applied promotion. These assertions are source-read only.

`RunOutcomeRecoveryCoordinatorTest` uses event-recording recovery-store/sink fakes and fixed time to assert journal-before-side-effects ordering and clear-after-snapshot; idempotent recovery when mood, return, or snapshot writes already landed; blocking on conflicting mood/route counts or corrupt journal; preservation and later cleanup of a pending journal after clear failure without double counting; and exact rough-run streak recovery. The fake legacy mutation methods deliberately error to prove the recoverable path uses state snapshots. It does not test filesystem atomicity (covered elsewhere) and was not executed.

`RecoveryEvidenceMaintenanceCoordinatorTest` uses two domain fakes to verify one handler per evidence domain, privacy-safe summaries, independent retry for pending and I/O-failed evidence, no action on clean/corrupt during safe recovery, domain-scoped corrupt discard, refusing to erase valid pending records, recovering before discard where possible, deliberate discard only after unresolved/blocked recovery, read-failure refusal, and surfacing clear failure while state remains corrupt. Assertions are source-read only.

`RecoveryEvidenceMaintenanceNamespaceIntegrationTest` uses actual SharedPreferences and atomic-file stores to verify that the maintenance object captures its original primary namespace: later switching SaveManager to compatibility preferences does not redirect manifest inspection, run-journal replay/state mutation, or unwritten-promotion-receipt cleanup. It checks primary evidence is repaired/cleared while compatibility state/evidence remains unchanged and the active namespace remains compatibility. `SaveIntegrityPropertyTest` seeds malformed supported preference value types, NaN/infinite/negative numbers, enum and set values, dynamic entity counters, and unknown keys across 128 deterministic seeds; it checks migration/repair to typed bounded values, unknown-key preservation, safe public readers, and idempotent second repair. Across 96 other deterministic seeds it checks future schema versions preserve the primary namespace exactly and switch active prefs to compatibility. Tests are read, not run.

`SaveIntegrityManagerTest` covers concrete migration/repair behavior: malformed primitive/enums/sets/dynamic counters normalize safely; known sets drop invalid entries and obey caps while unknown sets/keys survive; clean current-schema repair is idempotent; future schema is preserved with primary ghost bytes untouched and writes redirected to compatibility namespace; incomplete last-run summaries are discarded; valid fields and unknown entity suffixes survive; and write APIs clamp invalid values and saturate counters. `SaveManagerTest` covers transactional Garden progress/Seed spending and stale-screen write absorption, nonsequential admin progress, unique high-score publication, monotonic score/distance records, malformed record normalization, ghost frame binary round trip, persistent relationship/collision/memory counters, costume unlocks, biome friendship, run summary and route counts, mood/return state, relationship stages, and legacy costume sentinel merging. Robolectric assertions remain unexecuted.

`SaveManagerConcurrencyTest` launches coordinated threads to assert exactly one owner for equal high-score publication, lossless concurrent progression increments, stale Garden follow-ups cannot overwrite concurrent Seed writes, spare/hit transitions remain serializable with mutually exclusive kindness/tender streaks, unlock sets preserve unions across independent discoveries, and run-earned Seeds share a currency lock with Garden purchases before a stale run-state save rejoins canonical balance. It also verifies preference namespace switching keeps namespace-specific transaction markers isolated. The code uses bounded latches/joins and propagates worker errors; source read only, not executed.

`GhostPersistenceManagerTest` covers detached recorder snapshots, immediate in-memory visibility followed by durable asynchronous promotion, startup repair from strong or legacy receipt/manifest sidecars, distance repair after sidecar receipt removal, abandoning an unwritten candidate, corrupt/tampered digest or distance blocking repair and future promotion without deleting the prior ghost, binary frame round-trip, truncated/trailing/oversized payload rejection, and invalid nonfinite/state input rejection. Helpers construct raw legacy v1 sidecars and verify strong v2 manifest identity/size; teardown awaits writes and clears all stores. Tests are not executed.

`GhostPromotionRecoveryCoordinatorTest` isolates the recovery protocol with configurable in-memory receipt/artifact/manifest stores. It asserts the write order (strong receipt, ghost, manifest, distance, receipt clear); recovery of matching durable ghosts and v1 records; already-applied short-circuits; preserving old valid artifacts when a candidate never landed; digest/distance mismatch rejection; retry after failures at ghost, manifest, or distance writes; manifest-only distance repair and legacy manifest upgrade; avoiding ghost loads when already applied; corrupt receipt/manifest fail-closed behavior; stale-manifest replacement from a matching receipt; and SHA-256 identity sensitivity to every persisted frame field and distance. No tests were run.

`AccessibilityAnnouncementPolicyTest` checks immediate surface-change announcements, run-status gating on both distance buckets and elapsed interval, Bloom transitions bypassing routine throttling while preserving the distance milestone, Garden/settings announcements only on meaningful state changes, and reset starting a fresh surface baseline. Its semantic snapshots include score to establish score-only changes do not speak. `AccessibilityGameplayActionAvailabilityTrackerTest` verifies first observation/reset establish baselines, independent jump/duck capability deltas, unchanged states do not repeatedly invalidate, and clear requires a fresh baseline. `AnimalEncounterFlavorTest` checks hedgehog warning/pass/hit text changes with encounter history and clean-pass state. `ApplicationIdentityTest` uses Robolectric context to assert the debug application ID is `com.anurag9000.forestrun.debug` and excludes the placeholder `yourname`. Source-level assertions only; unexecuted.

`ApplicationPersistenceFacadeTest` injects fakes and verifies operations route to their owning durability domain without crossing: run reset/commit, Garden purchase, feedback preferences, wardrobe, recovery inspect/recover/discard, and encounter/pass/hit persistence. It also checks return values/identity and propagates one domain failure without invoking other domains. `AuthoredWritingStyleTest` is a source-inspection guard over four named authored-text files: extracts quoted strings, filters code-like fragments, checks corpus length percentiles/max, and rejects a list of expository phrases. `BiomeManagerTest` covers cyclic biome selection, invalid distance fallback, large finite input, near-boundary crossfade/entity pool blend, continuity of colors/ambient at all biome boundaries, invalid-update palette reset, and persistent explicit debug override. `BirdEncounterFlavorTest` checks species-specific duck/tit/chickadee prompt, warning, pass and hit text using answer state, flock size, and flutter spread. These assertions have not been executed.

`BloomEconomyTest` checks Seeds collected during active Bloom do not reset its timer; a long-lived game state cannot refund Garden-spent currency on save; and `resetRun` refreshes an externally changed balance. `BloomPowerPresentationTest` verifies conversion tiers and recent surge monotonically affect player scale/aura/surge, nonfinite inputs resolve to finite calm defaults, and extreme finite inputs stay within presentation bounds. `BloomPresentationAdmissionTest` checks finite values clamp to unit range while nonfinite values close admission to zero. `BloomPresentationTest` checks HUD modes/labels/status for one-seed-away READY, ACTIVE countdown/conversions, post-Bloom AFTERGLOW, malformed timing/count inputs, extreme finite clamping, and rejection of nonfinite afterglow. `BloomTimerPartitionPropertyTest` checks equivalent positive delta partitions produce equivalent active time and exact expiration; just-below-boundary remains active; invalid deltas consume no time; first post-expiration seed starts a fresh meter. `BloomWorldReactionTest` checks identity-based once-only reaction tracking even for equal/hash-colliding objects, reaction cue families by entity, and nearby/forward/not-yet-reacted admission. Source reviewed, tests not run.

`CameraSystemTest` exercises trauma input rejection/bounds, finite offsets, invalid-delta no-op behavior, immediate reduced-motion shake clearing, Canvas save/restore even when drawing throws, and full reset. `CatalogueInvariantTest` pins entity count/unique names, exactly six tracked relationship species, biome order/readability/pool coverage/next-cycle behavior, costume ordering and unique labels, pacifist severity order and authored lines, and monotonic complete Garden costs. `CinematicOverlayRendererCacheTest` checks shader reuse when only profile emphasis/shimmer changes, rebuilds on shader-input changes, rejects invalid geometry before cache admission, clamps malformed profile/time/center values, and interns fixed sanctuary-lighting identities. `CinematicPolishTest` checks RUN baseline remains lighter than sanctuary scenes, Bloom emphasis strengthens effects, scene profiles stay clamped, nonfinite inputs return calm baseline, and extreme values clamp to endpoints. All reviewed at source level, unexecuted.

`CollisionArbitrationPropertyTest` constructs real `EntityManager` instances with deterministic probe entities and evaluates all permutations of priority sets. It asserts HIT > STUMBLE > MERCY_MISS > NONE ordering, exactly one selected entity with all losers pending, provisional padded mercy contact cannot prevent a later HIT/STUMBLE, safe passage produces exactly one mercy, Bloom conversion suppresses provisional mercy, simultaneous safe passages resolve each eligible encounter once, and many NONE-only arrangements do not invent outcomes. The helper recursively enumerates permutations; probes track selection callbacks and use a fixed overlapping hitbox. This source-level property/integration suite was not run.

`CostumeManagerTest` covers relationship/progression-based unlock batches and order, equip rejection/acceptance, species-specific milestone rewards, featured newest-unlock and active/equipped presentations, NONE-only malformed data not creating blank cards, and persisted repair of a locked active selection. `DebugScenarioPersistenceTest` checks rewards in nonpersistent/debug runs never leak to durable Seeds or high score, ordinary runs restore canonical durable state after debug contamination, and clean-pass/spare histories obey each entity's `shouldRecordPersistence` policy for cat/fox/wolf cases. `DebugScenarioScriptTest` verifies ordered exactly-once timed actions, scenario replacement/reset, clear, nonfinite elapsed-time rejection, manual-only unscripted scenarios, frame-partition-independent timed holds/traces, and callback failure retaining pending work without a false trace. `DeterministicEncounterRandomnessTest` injects Kotlin seeded `Random` into the entity factory; Bamboo gaps, Chickadee altitude/target paths, Dog buddy duration, and director-authored Bamboo layouts are compared across separate instances and different frame partitions. Source-reviewed; not run.

The first five deterministic trace suites verify script partition invariance for all authored scenarios and rejection of backward/nonfinite duplicates; valid replay-contract acceptance and missing/extra/rescheduled/substituted event rejection; canonical JSON evidence generation with SHA-256 and commit/artifact/scenario/contract bindings; atomic file store naming/replacement and refusal of tampered, noncanonical or invalid-directory evidence; and canonical encoder payload stability, finite timing conversion, identity validation, and fail-closed rejection of incomplete, altered, overflowed or unbound traces. `DeterministicScenarioTraceEvidenceAdversarialTest` additionally rehashes semantically tampered actions/schedules/lateness, changes envelope fields, and ensures rejected replacements preserve the previous complete file. `DeterministicScenarioTraceEvidenceVerifierTest` checks all authored scripts round-trip through persist/decode/verify and rejects schema/event/digest/filename/identity mismatches, oversized or malformed UTF-8, missing files, and directories. `DeterministicScenarioTraceManifestTest` covers deterministic order independent of input order, canonical decode, complete unique child set and common identity, semantic/structural tamper rejection, directory verification against each trace plus the manifest, rejection of missing/corrupt/renamed/extra files, and atomic preservation on rejected replacement. `DeterministicScenarioTraceTest` validates all authored action schedules, rejects reversed/overlapping/unbalanced actions, records only successful dispatches in order, preserves pending events on callback failure, fails closed on trace capacity, and detaches snapshots from recorder reuse. `DifficultyScalerTest` covers spawn-gap endpoints, progression-based spawn pools, invalid-distance fallback, and an explicit debug biome overriding distance-derived selection. These suites are source-reviewed only; tests were not run.

`EagleDepartureIntegrationTest` builds real Eagle/Player/EntityManager state and verifies a completed vertical attack escape remains pending until it is credited once as a clean pass, a fast final segment cannot tunnel through the player before departure reward, Bloom conversion remains exclusive, and same-frame direct HIT outranks deferred departure and reset drops the abandoned credit. `EncounterActionFeasibilityTest` exercises the sampled 50ms jump envelope and analytic feasibility model across production spawn pacing, physical apex thresholds, tiny/large numeric cases, monotonicity with lead/clearance, and malformed-field fail-closed finite reports. `EncounterActionPlayerIntegrationTest` cross-checks a real Player's maximum admitted-step jump arc against the feasibility model, confirming the 495px sampled apex cannot satisfy 500px clearance. Source read, no test execution.

`EncounterDirectorTest` covers scheduled deterministic step emission, seeded step replay across frame partitions, invalid-seed rejection, invalid delta isolation, large-delta drain exactly once, restart/stop and wrapped selection, metadata ordering, unique titles/full entity coverage, Dog-only variants, and dedicated Bloom/Ghost and major-family scenarios. `EncounterFamilyCatalogueTest` checks a derived 19-type ordinal-aligned catalogue, package-family counts, implementation identity, explicit ownership mapping for gameplay concerns, safe relative source paths, biome/scenario coverage, focused fairness reads, exact relationship-tracking capability, and Dog variant derivation without duplicating factory rules. `EncounterScenarioFingerprintTest` checks stable unique lowercase SHA-256 per scenario, canonical integer-timing payload shape, known independent cactus scenario/trace digests, exact action schedule in trace contract bytes, and deterministic catalogue digest. Source reviewed; unexecuted.

`EncounterSelectionBagTest` verifies each randomized bag cycle exhausts an authorized pool without repeats, no adjacent duplicate across refill boundaries, pool changes purge disallowed prior choices, duplicate entries do not weight/starve selection, and empty pools/reset fail safely. `EvaluationThrottleTest` covers first-call admission, interval edge, `Long.MIN_VALUE` as a valid timestamp, forced advancement, clock rollback/reset, zero interval, and negative-interval rejection. `FamiliarityWarmthIntegrationTest` joins persisted encounter/pass/spare memory with relationship stage/line selection and tests alternate modifiers reaching the bonded threshold. `FamiliarityWarmthScoringTest` checks baseline and independent additive modifier weights, negative restored counters clamping, and tier thresholds/caps. Source reviewed, tests unexecuted.

`FeedbackSettingsTest` checks default/full feedback, reload persistence, malformed stored types repaired to safe defaults, reduced-motion camera suppression, bounded particle reduction that retains positive cues, frozen shimmer, nonfinite/extreme shimmer safety, and negative-strength normalization. `FloraEncounterFlavorTest` verifies authored Lily, Hyacinth, Orchid, Eucalyptus and Cactus pass lines vary with encounters/repeat hits/clean passes. `ForestJournalProjectionTest` checks canonical Garden catalogue, all gentle route-shape completion, six-pillar capstone composition, legacy mood sanitization before dominant selection, safe distance conversion, and specialized/fallback memory narratives. `ForestJournalReadOnlyTest` snapshots real compatibility preferences before/after Journal and collection projection; it proves opening Journal does not materialize a missing relationship stage/milestone, rewrite existing bond state, count orphan bonds absent discovery, or repair blank memory keys. Tests not executed.

`ForestMoodSystemTest` exercises concurrent mood writes over 50 rounds, classification of gentle and fearful runs, streak/dominant mood accumulation, counter saturation at `Int.MAX_VALUE`, and streak reset on mood changes while historical counts persist. `FrameInputAdmissionTest` covers accepted limits, invalid/nonpositive frame input rejection, bounded delta/speed preservation and caps, and exceptions on misuse of bounded functions. `FramePerformanceMonitorTest` verifies empty and populated snapshots, means/percentiles/max/slow ratio, bounded ring window with cumulative totals, atomic rejection of negative measurements, concurrent snapshot/report invariants, and constructor guards. `FramePerformanceReportTest` checks deterministic escaped JSON and finite values; rejects blank scenario, contradictory frame metrics, workload current-over-peak, and inconsistent ghost I/O completion/failure/duration maxima. These tests remain unexecuted.

`FramePerformanceResetTest` verifies reset clears warmup and cumulative samples while retaining monitor configuration and accepting new post-reset measurements. `GameAccessibilityActionRouterTest` checks only currently published node/action pairs reach the handler; stale/unsupported, disabled/unaffordable Garden actions and malformed semantics fail closed; an affordable purchase is admitted once; false/throwing handlers never report success. `GameAccessibilityGeometryTest` checks every semantic node gets finite positive bounds inside the viewport, Journal node bounds exactly match its visible chip, Garden plant node geometry follows canonical order, settings nodes align by row, and zero viewport fails closed. Source-reviewed; not executed.

`GameAccessibilityNodeProviderTest` exercises the virtual Android accessibility tree: root child publication, status description and clickability, virtual node action delegation constrained by current semantics, exclusive/clearable accessibility focus, and checkable settings states. `GameAccessibilitySemanticsTest` covers all surfaces' stable unique IDs/focus order, menu Journal action, live run status plus bounded jump/long-jump/duck availability, stance-dependent disablement, settings booleans/dismiss action, exactly one affordable next Garden plant, wardrobe style visibility/actionability, unaffordable descriptions, REST quote/summary and fallbacks, disabled continue while recovery is pending, and malformed-snapshot exceptions. `GameConstantsInvariantTest` asserts finite positive and ordered scroll/score/Bloom/mercy/spawn/biome/wind configuration. `GameStateEncounterSelectionIntegrationTest` connects the opening guide's five authorized families to bag fairness, validates live-pool exhaustion/isolation, and confirms reset restores the opening contract. Tests not run.

`GameStateLongHorizonPropertyTest` performs 8,192 deterministic mixed operations and checks monotonic finite time/distance/score, high-score floor, seed balance agreement with durable storage, Bloom timer/meter bounds, nonnegative run counters, debuff bounds and complete reset invariants; it separately applies 2,048 conversions during one Bloom window and confirms no timer refresh/retrigger. `GameStateOpeningInputBoundaryTest` ensures malformed/negative hold durations cannot complete onboarding while the authored hold threshold does. `GameThreadFailureTest` checks update failure stops before render, render failure occurs after update, captured stage/cause are reported once, and failure-handler exceptions are suppressed onto the original failure. `GameThreadPerformanceTest` checks frame timing capture, stop during update skips render but records frame, and synthetic non-Canvas render callbacks do not close pending input-latency actions. `GameThreadShutdownTest` checks interruptible long sleep shutdown, restoration of caller interrupt, bounded wait for uncooperative update followed by no stale render, and safe stop-before-start. Source reviewed only.

`GameStateManagerTest` focuses on the state owner and persistence races: stale run previews/saves reconcile high-score ownership without false tie/new-record labels; nonpersistent summaries stay isolated; score/distance records cannot regress; Seeds/Bloom activation, bulk saturation and in-Bloom rewards obey one timer; world scroll speed and distance agree per frame including debuff start/expiry; invalid deltas and numeric multipliers fail safely; transient/milestone/onboarding state resets; pacifist rewards dequeue FIFO; mercy counters and Bloom conversion/debug/fraction APIs behave; and generated run summary reflects current counters, killer, mood, and route tier. The suite uses Robolectric preferences but was not executed.

`GameViewFrameInputTest` verifies rejected deltas do not increment the frame counter, normal deltas increment once, and huge finite deltas are admitted as one bounded frame. `GardenPurchaseInteractionCoordinatorTest` checks a successful authoritative purchase emits exactly one growth cue and all nonpurchase statuses suppress feedback. `GardenPurchaseManagerTest` tests durable atomic progress-plus-currency transactions, exact full catalogue budget and one-seed-short boundary, duplicate taps, insufficient funds, canonical persisted values over caller cache, compatibility namespace routing, legacy overload validation, and complete/invalid request outcomes. `GardenSanctuaryAtmosphereIntegrationTest` verifies the planner returns the exact baseline for a persisted reckless mood, preventing unintended atmospheric modifiers. Not run.

`GardenSanctuaryPlannerTest` integrates saved encounter, relationship, collision, route, mood, biome, memory, and wardrobe progression into Garden presentation. Its cases cover gentle/fearful baselines; repeated harm, strained bonds and repeat killer; milestone keepsake and costume rewards; named route/world signs and world opinion; kindness, clean flora reads, peaceful biome friendship, familiar returns and Peace Kept lighting; explicit homecoming consequences for bond/history/mood/memory/dress; and carried-home copy/trace/badge projections. The suite asserts exact labels or bounded presentation changes for concrete histories; it does not mutate through rendering or check UI layout. Not executed.

`GhostFileFormatTest` pins the v2 ghost file magic/version/count/frame field ordering and stable `PlayerState` wire code, confirms legacy count/ordinal files load without rewrite, preserves unknown future-version bytes while rejecting them, and rejects unknown stable state codes. `GhostIoTelemetryTest` checks write start/completion/failure/frame/latency maxima, concurrent snapshot publication invariants across 10,000 operations, and reset isolation. `HapticManagerTest` checks service acquisition/release ownership, cancel retains service for re-enable, patterns no-op while disabled or released, and idempotent teardown. `InputHandlerTest` covers tap/hold/swipe arbitration, 80px strict swipe threshold around 75ms decision boundary, hold cap, malformed coordinate/tick handling, cancellation/reset semantics, no duplicate classification after hold starts, and 1,024 seeded predecision gesture traces each resolving to exactly one jump or duck family. Tests remain unrun.

`InputLatencyReportTest` checks deterministic complete JSON identifies app-touch-to-posted-frame measurement (explicitly not touch-to-photon) and validates injected/sample counts, percentile ordering and device refresh metadata. `InputLatencyTelemetryConcurrencyTest` coordinates UI writer, render completer and snapshot reader across 2,048 actions, validating coherent bounded samples and percentile ordering under concurrent access. `InputLatencyTelemetryTest` verifies all four latency segments, bounded-window percentiles, replacement/drop semantics for unfinished touch, out-of-order rejection, render-before-response handling, cancel behavior, negative-clock recovery and reset. `LatestRequestGateTest` confirms newer tokens invalidate old ones, cancel invalidates current ownership, and 10,000 changes cannot revive stale tokens. These tests were read but not run.

`LeitmotifManagerTest` exercises pure playback-profile composition: Bloom is loudest and conversion-sensitive; later run stages increase fullness/tempo; extreme scroll clamps; Bloom swell follows remaining time and conversions; menu/rest/run motifs differ; invalid scroll/time/conversion inputs fall back safely; and conversion influence saturates at five. It verifies all numeric profile/motif dimensions remain finite and bounded. Not executed.

`LeitmotifProfileCacheTest` verifies immutable MENU/REST profile instances are reused across speed changes and run profiles remain speed-derived. `LightingIdentityProfileTest` checks night/Bloom color changes, caller-owned identity mutation/reuse, calm fallback for nonfinite values, endpoint clamps, and cached scene-specific sanctuary profiles. `LiveCollisionEffectsTest` verifies the live adapter delegates every effect port while leaving ordering to coordinators, including shared sound mapping and distinct haptic pulses. `LiveGameAccessibilityActionsTest` checks stable semantic IDs map to typed runtime commands and mismatched/informational/unknown actions fail closed. `LiveGameAccessibilityGardenFeedbackTest` verifies successful virtual plant purchase invokes purchase once and emits one growth cue; rejection or wrong semantic action emits none. Tests were read, not run.
