API 36.1 emulator connected validation (2026-10-07): an initial run of the 20 ordinary instrumentation tests found one failure in the test helper `mutateStopped`: its single call to `GameView.pause()` returned false within the production thread's one-second bounded stop window. `pause()` intentionally fails closed and logs rather than mutating live state while the render owner remains active. The helper now retries pause from the UI thread for at most 10 seconds, sleeping outside the UI thread, and performs the mutation only after a successful stop. The isolated biome-cycle regression passed, then the complete ordinary connected suite passed 20/20 with zero failures/skips on `Medium_Phone_API_36.1` (API 36.1, SwiftShader). This covers an emulator only; `@LargeTest` physical performance/input profiling and physical-device behavior remain unverified. The AVD was shut down cleanly. This audit remains active; the repository is not certified exhaustive and store, human, physical, and release acceptance gates remain open.

Lint warning triage (same 2026-10-07 reports): the single `ApplySharedPref` call is in `FeedbackSettings` and uses synchronous `commit()` as a persistence result boundary; changing to asynchronous `apply()` would discard that success/failure signal and needs a contract-level change. The min-API `appCategory` attribute is ignored on API 24–25 and is consumed from API 26 onward. The two fixed-orientation warnings reflect an explicit game layout policy and have a manifest instrumentation assertion; Android 16 large-screen adaptation still needs product/device acceptance. `RuntimeAssetValidator.getIdentifier()` is intentional runtime enumeration and covered by the new instrumentation class, but dynamic lookup is also why resource shrinker warnings need source-aware review. The remaining dependency/plugin version warnings are upgrade opportunities, not proven defects; changes require compatibility/build validation. `ObsoleteSdkInt` and `ViewConstructor` merit source cleanup review. The 47 `UseKtx` warnings are refactoring suggestions; the 22 `UnusedResources` warnings may be false positives under dynamic loading, so neither category was mass-edited without establishing usage semantics.

Strict JSON preflight symlink bypass (2026-10-07): `verify_strict_json_evidence.expand_paths()` resolved each supplied file and each recursive match before calling `strict_json.load_file()`. This erased leaf or parent symlink identity, bypassing `stable_file_reader`'s no-follow boundary. The resolver now keeps absolute lexical paths and passes them unchanged to the descriptor walker. Regression tests cover a direct leaf link, a linked JSON discovered by directory expansion, and a JSON path beneath a linked parent; the focused strict JSON suite passes 15 tests. The complete script suite passes 1,136 tests, `py_compile` succeeds, and `git diff --check` passes. Candidate verification tests were also expanded for missing roots, invalid Git SHAs, mismatch between HEAD and `refs/heads/main`, and CLI output/error behavior; 17 focused tests pass, and measured `verify_main_candidate.py` statement coverage increased from 73.5% to 97.1%. Remaining Python warning: Pillow `Image.getdata()` emits a deprecation warning in visual comparison, screenshot curation, and asset import; no replacement was made in this pass. Audit remains active.

Strict JSON preflight expansion bound (2026-10-07): the 2,048-file limit was checked only after `Path.rglob()` results had all been materialized by `list.extend`, so a very large directory could consume unbounded traversal time and memory before the advertised guard ran. Recursive expansion now appends incrementally and raises as soon as the 2,049th JSON file is found. A deterministic test patches the limit to two and proves traversal consumes exactly three matching paths. The focused strict JSON suite passes 16 tests and the full Python suite passes 1,137 tests after both preflight fixes; `py_compile` and `git diff --check` pass.

Screenshot evidence publication parent-swap race (2026-10-07): a deterministic live probe renamed an already admitted raw-capture output directory and replaced its pathname with a symlink immediately before temp-file creation. The previous `NamedTemporaryFile(dir=path.parent)` plus pathname `os.replace()` followed the replacement and wrote `capture-session.json` into the external target. Added `stable_file_reader.atomic_write_bytes()`: it creates a random exclusive temp leaf relative to a held no-follow directory descriptor, fsyncs file contents, checks that the parent path still names the held directory, renames with source/destination dirfds, fsyncs the directory, and rechecks the binding. Both capture-session finalization and screenshot-sidecar writing now use it. Fault-injection regressions prove swaps cannot write into the external target or publish into a renamed directory. The focused finalizer suite passes 9 tests and writer suite 6 tests; the complete Python suite passes 1,139 tests. `py_compile scripts/*.py` and `git diff --check` pass. The audit remains active.

Curated screenshot parent-swap read bypass (2026-10-07): the curated verifier checked directory components once, then read PNG/sidecar files with full-path `lstat()` plus leaf-only `O_NOFOLLOW`. A deterministic race renamed `final/` and replaced it with a symlink to the same directory; because each leaf inode and metadata remained identical, the complete `verify_curated_set()` call accepted the symlink traversal. `_read_regular_bytes()` now delegates to the shared descriptor walker, which rejects the symlink component while opening the leaf relative to a held parent descriptor. This also hardens raw-set and capture-session reads that reuse this helper. The screenshot finalizer now passes the already bounded, stable-parsed sidecar object to `parse_capture_evidence()` instead of reopening it by pathname. The race regression failed against the old reader and passes after the fix; curated verifier tests pass 24/24, finalizer 9/9, and the full Python suite passes 1,140 tests. `py_compile scripts/*.py` and `git diff --check` pass; repo-wide audit remains active.

Release-summary publisher parent-swap race (2026-10-07): the release-readiness and physical device-acceptance summary writers used `NamedTemporaryFile(dir=path.parent)` followed by pathname `os.replace()`, while creating missing parent directories through `Path.mkdir()`. This left their reports vulnerable to the same concurrent parent replacement that affected screenshot evidence. They now create missing parents via held no-follow directory descriptors and publish bytes through `atomic_write_bytes()`, which anchors the exclusive temp, rename, and directory fsync to one checked parent inode. Fault-injection tests replace each output parent with a symlink immediately before temp creation and confirm neither external nor renamed directories receive a report. The source-contract test was updated to accept the expanded shared-reader import while still requiring the no-follow reader and atomic publisher. Focused readiness/device suites pass 19 and 17 tests; the full Python suite passes 1,142 tests. `py_compile scripts/*.py` and `git diff --check` pass. Audit continues.

Store-metadata path-swap and stale-manifest audit (2026-10-07): metadata file reads used pathname-based opens, missing metadata directories were created with `Path.mkdir()`, and `finalize_metadata()` unlinked an existing manifest before writing its replacement. These operations left parent-symlink races and could destroy the prior manifest before the replacement was safely published. Reads now use the shared no-follow descriptor reader; missing directories are created component-by-component relative to held directory descriptors; manifest publication uses `atomic_write_bytes()`. The redundant pre-write unlink is removed, so an admitted regular manifest remains in place until atomic replacement. Regression coverage confirms a leaf symlink injected at open is rejected and a parent swap cannot redirect publication or delete the previous manifest. The metadata verifier suite passes 16 tests; the full Python suite passes 1,143 tests. `py_compile scripts/*.py` and `git diff --check` pass. Audit remains in progress.

Acceptance summary publisher parent-swap audit (2026-10-07): release governance, Play delivery, installed candidate identity, installed identity matrix, and human acceptance summaries all used pathname-based temporary creation and replacement, with `Path.mkdir()` for missing parents. Added shared `atomic_write_json()` over descriptor-relative directory creation and `atomic_write_bytes()`, and migrated all five summary writers. A table-driven race test swaps each parent to a symlink at temp creation and confirms each writer fails closed without publishing into either the external target or moved original directory. Release-governance and human-acceptance suites pass 22 and 15 tests; the new four-writer regression passes. The complete Python suite passes 1,145 tests; `py_compile scripts/*.py` and `git diff --check` pass. Other output writers remain to be audited; repository-wide completion is not established.

Release evidence index publisher parent-swap audit (2026-10-07): `build_release_evidence_index.py` validated output parents but then created a pathname-based temporary and used pathname `os.replace()`, leaving a swap window after its final validation. Publication now creates missing parent components through `ensure_directory_exists()` and delegates canonical bytes to `atomic_write_bytes()`, anchoring temp creation, replace, and fsync to one no-follow parent descriptor. A deterministic injection renames the admitted directory and installs an external symlink immediately before temp creation; the publisher fails closed and leaves neither destination with an index or temp file. The release-index focused suites pass 44 tests; the full Python suite passes 1,146 tests. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Remaining output publishers and Android/runtime/release gates have not been exhausted; audit remains active.

Single-file report publisher parent-swap continuation (2026-10-07): `build_resolved_dependency_sbom.py`, `visual_baseline_provenance.py`, and `verify_android_page_size_package.py` had pathname temp-file plus replace sequences after parent creation/checks. They now use the shared descriptor-anchored atomic byte writer; the shared helper can also reject a symlink leaf observed immediately before replacement, preserving the visual-provenance writer's explicit symlink-swap contract. A table-driven fault injection swaps each writer's parent directory for an external symlink at temporary creation and verifies fail-closed behavior and cleanup. Focused writer suites pass 9, 27, and 23 tests; the new publisher race test passes. Full Python suite passes 1,147 tests; `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. The Pillow 11.3 pin remains relevant to outstanding `Image.getdata()` deprecation warnings because `get_flattened_data()` is unavailable at that declared dependency floor. Remaining publishers and all Android/device/runtime/release gates remain unaudited or incomplete.

Acceptance aggregate output alias and publication audit (2026-10-07): `_write_json_atomic()` resolved the requested output path before writing. If that path was a symlink to an unprotected external file, the publisher resolved the link and atomically replaced the external target while leaving the symlink behind. It now retains the lexical absolute destination, creates parents through no-follow directory descriptors, rechecks protected-source aliasing, and rejects a symlink leaf immediately before descriptor-relative replacement. A regression verifies an external target's bytes and symlink remain intact; another injects a parent swap and verifies no redirected or moved-directory output. The same descriptor-anchored writer now serves creative-asset inventory, declared-dependency inventory, and visual-regression reports, with a shared three-writer parent-swap injection test. Focused suites pass; integrated Python suite passes 1,149 tests, compileall and diff checks pass. Android debug/release unit variants each pass 1,289 tests on JDK 21; initial JDK 17 run could not initialize Robolectric SDK 36, and rerunning on the locally installed JDK 21 resolved it. Host CI Android tasks (Kotlin compilation, lint, debug and Android-test APK assembly, release AAB bundle) succeed; lint reports 84 warnings and zero errors per variant. R8 `mapping.txt` and `usage.txt` are both present and nonempty. Debug APK and release AAB package inspection both pass as no-native-code packages. Local API 36.1 connected instrumentation passes 30/30 with no skips, but emulator performance-profile output is not physical-device performance evidence and does not satisfy the workflow's API 35 connected gate. Fresh release-source-asset verification passes for 30 assets and 15 raw audio resources; current no-trainable-surface classification is complete with zero findings and no execution claim. Device, physical hardware, human acceptance, and release publication remain unverified; repository audit continues.

Staged acceptance aggregate and release-summary publication continuation (2026-10-07): the final device-acceptance aggregate publisher validated the staged report and all source snapshots, then used pathname `os.replace()` without binding the output directory or rechecking the staged inode at the final rename. Added `atomic_replace_file()` to the shared stable-file module; it opens the source and destination parent without following links, verifies the staged file's device/inode/size/mtime/ctime against the validated snapshot, checks the parent binding, renames relative to the held descriptor, fsyncs the directory, and rolls back if the parent changes across the rename. Existing regular outputs are hard-linked to temporary recovery names before replacement so a detected parent race restores prior bytes; failed rollback preserves and reports the backup path. Regressions inject swaps both before staged lookup and during the actual rename, verify staged bytes and any previous output are restored, and check no external publication occurs. The no-trainable-surface report and Play release text-summary publisher now also use descriptor-anchored byte publication, with symlink-leaf rejection and injected parent-swap regressions. The full Python suite passes 1,156 tests; `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. More transactional compilers, directory publishers, collection output paths, runtime and physical-device gates remain under review.

Multi-output acceptance compiler publication continuation (2026-10-07): five manifest-plus-summary compilers (device acceptance, human acceptance, release governance, Play delivery, and installed identity matrix) repeated pathname-based staging, backup moves, and rollback. They now serialize their existing JSON formats and delegate to `atomic_publish_files()`, which stages/fsyncs all payloads under one held no-follow directory descriptor, preserves prior regular-file contents as recovery snapshots, publishes through descriptor-relative renames, checks parent binding after each rename, and restores prior outputs on failure. A cleanup failure after commit now reports that outputs were published and identifies the retained recovery directory; a regression verifies committed bytes and backup retention. Legacy compiler tests now assert domain-error translation and preservation of both prior outputs, while shared helper tests cover second-output failure, parent swap, and cleanup failure. The focused six-module suite passes 95 tests; the complete Python suite passes 1,159 tests. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Other publishers, Android/runtime/device gates, and full repo audit remain incomplete.

Rollback snapshot immutability audit (2026-10-07): shared single-file and multi-file publication helpers used hard links for previous-output recovery. An in-place write to the replaced output could therefore mutate the rollback link and lose the exact old bytes during a later failure. Recovery files are now copied from a no-follow descriptor, with source identity/metadata checked before and after copying, destination identity rechecked immediately before replacement, and mode/timestamps retained. Added injected concurrent-mutation regression to the multi-output publisher; existing parent-swap regressions exercise rollback through the single-file helper. The focused publication/compiler suite passes 96 tests; full Python suite passes 1,160 tests. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Remaining directory publishers, other output paths, runtime/device checks, and external release gates remain unaudited or incomplete.

Raw screenshot directory publisher race audit (2026-10-07): `publish_screenshot_capture_set.py` validated directory paths, then copied unmanaged files and moved the output/staging directories through path-based operations. A parent swap could redirect copy or publication operations, and backup cleanup failures were silently ignored. Unmanaged entries are now traversed/copied relative to held no-follow directory descriptors with source identity checks; directory renames are relative to one held parent descriptor, parent binding is checked around publication, and failed publication moves the new set back to staging before restoring the old set. Old-set cleanup now uses descriptor-relative recursive deletion and reports post-commit failures with the retained recovery path. Added regressions for parent-symlink swap during rename and backup-cleanup failure; updated the source contract to require descriptor-relative renames. Screenshot publisher, capture contract, session finalizer, and sidecar suites pass 37 tests; full Python suite passes 1,162 tests. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Other directory publishers and runtime/device/release gates remain open.

Atomic JSON writer integration and rollback snapshot continuation (2026-10-07): store-graphics and installed-candidate-identity manifests still had independent temporary-file plus pathname-replace writers. They now serialize their existing sorted JSON bytes and call the shared descriptor-anchored `atomic_write_bytes()` helper, with symlink-leaf rejection and collector-specific error translation. This review found the shared byte writer still hard-linked prior outputs; it now uses a stable copied snapshot and checks the output identity immediately before replacement, matching the single- and multi-file helpers. Added a store-manifest parent-swap regression; collector NaN and failed-publish cleanup tests pass with the new error mapping. Focused writer/collector/index/aggregate suite passes 59 tests; full Python suite passes 1,163 tests. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Directory publication in store graphics and installed-identity collection, remaining output paths, Android/runtime/device checks, and external release gates remain unaudited or open.

Store-graphics directory generation and cleanup audit (2026-10-07): graphics output creation followed parent symlinks via `.resolve()`, staging used path-based removal/creation and Pillow paths, and final directory swaps relied on `Path.replace()`. Generation now keeps a no-follow parent descriptor, creates/cleans staging relative to it, writes images through a held staging descriptor path, and writes the private manifest through that descriptor. Final promotion uses shared `atomic_publish_directory()` with identity checks, recovery of interrupted backups, descriptor-relative renames/recursive deletion, parent-binding checks, and explicit post-commit cleanup errors. The raw screenshot publisher now shares the iterative descriptor-relative tree-removal helper. Added regressions for parent swaps during render and rename, old-set restoration, backup-cleanup failures, and cleanup of deeply nested directories without Python recursion. The focused graphics/helper set passes 22 tests and the combined graphics/screenshot/helper set passes 35; full Python suite passes 1,167 tests. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Installed-identity directory publication, remaining output paths, Android/device behavior, and external release gates remain open.

Installed-identity collection output transaction audit (2026-10-07): the collector wrote device pulls and temporary manifests directly beneath the requested output parent, resolved parent symlinks, and removed an existing empty destination before its final rename. It now collects in a private system temporary directory, rechecks the output parent identity after collection, copies only stable regular files and directories into sibling staging through held descriptors, and promotes with `atomic_publish_directory(destination_must_be_empty=True)`. A previously empty destination is preserved through backup/restore on publication errors; concurrent nonempty output changes fail closed. Added parent-symlink swap and failed-promotion restoration regressions. Focused collector/helper suites pass 35 tests; full Python suite passes 1,169 tests. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Remaining publishers/output paths and Android, device, human-acceptance, and external release gates remain open.

Curated screenshot directory publication continuation (2026-10-07): screenshot curation copied validated bytes into a path-created staging tree, swapped final/backup with `Path.replace()`, recursively removed paths by pathname, and wrote its summary directly. Staging is now created under a held no-follow parent descriptor; validated image and sidecar bytes are written relative to the held staging descriptor, then `atomic_publish_directory()` handles recovery, parent identity checks, rollback, and descriptor-relative cleanup. Summary publication now uses the shared atomic byte writer with symlink-leaf rejection. Updated staging-mutation and interrupted-publication injections for descriptor-relative operations, and added a parent-symlink swap regression proving the previous set remains intact and no external files are created. Curator/capture-contract/stable-helper focused suites pass 39 tests; full Python suite passes 1,171 tests. A follow-up removed Pillow `getdata()` deprecation use from sprite processing and visual comparison; both focused suites pass with `DeprecationWarning` treated as errors. Sprite strip output now creates parents through the no-follow directory helper and publishes PNG bytes atomically with symlink-leaf rejection. The importer/comparator focused suite passes 15 tests under the same warning policy; full Python suite still passes 1,171 tests. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Remaining direct output paths, Android/runtime/device behavior, and human/release gates remain open.

Repository audit continuation (2026-10-07): removed remaining Pillow `getdata()` deprecation use in `import_final_assets.py` and `compare_visual_regression.py`, and changed sprite strip publishing to create parent directories through the no-follow helper and publish PNG bytes with `atomic_write_bytes()` plus symlink-leaf rejection. The importer/comparator focused set passes 15 tests with `DeprecationWarning` treated as an error; the full Python discovery suite passes 1,171 tests under that warning policy. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. `./gradlew test --no-daemon --console=plain` compiled debug and release Kotlin, then 139 of 627 Robolectric tests failed before test bodies because SDK 36 requires Java 21 and this host provides Java 17. `adb` is unavailable, so instrumented physical/emulator lanes could not run. Current Python write-site scan identifies private temporary snapshot/scratch outputs and atomic publishers; the source/runtime audit, Android behavior and device gates are still open. This is not exhaustive completion.

Android host-validation continuation (2026-10-07): acquired no system changes by downloading and extracting the Ubuntu OpenJDK 21 JDK under `/tmp`; with that runtime, `./gradlew test --no-daemon --console=plain` passes 1,289 debug unit tests and 1,289 release unit tests (241 suites each; zero failures/errors/skips). `assembleDebug assembleDebugAndroidTest bundleRelease lintRelease` also succeeds and emits debug APK, Android-test APK, unsigned release APK and AAB. Lint reports 84 warnings per variant, all Warning severity: ApplySharedPref (synchronous persistence of malformed preference repair), minimum-SDK/manifest/API advice, dependency-version suggestions, 22 unused resources and 47 UseKtx style suggestions; none is an error, and these were not mass-edited without semantic review. The earlier Java 17 run's 139 failures were runtime-startup failures before test bodies; the Java 21 rerun supersedes that result. Device execution is still unavailable because `adb` is not installed and no device endpoint is exposed. This evidence covers host unit/build/lint lanes only; it does not prove exhaustive code correctness or device/release acceptance.

Coverage and package-inspection continuation (2026-10-07): an audit-only AGP coverage run (temporary `enableUnitTestCoverage` setting, restored after task completion) generated JaCoCo XML for debug tests. It reports 5,016/24,452 lines (20.51%) and 2,473/15,851 branches (15.60%), but cannot be treated as valid whole-app coverage: `CatTest` passes both Cat behavior tests while the report has no Cat execution data; a targeted retry with Robolectric `instrumentedPackages` still produced zero Cat hits. JaCoCo documents that runtime/report class-file mismatch can yield 0% for executed classes, so coverage instrumentation/class identity must be resolved before coverage-guided claims. The release page-size inspector CLI passes on the locally built unsigned APK and AAB with `--require-no-native-code`; it reports 0 native libraries (APK 384 entries, AAB 390). The candidate SHA passed to this diagnostic was HEAD only and does not bind these artifacts to the dirty worktree. `test_verify_android_page_size_package.py` passes 23 tests, and focused GameThread tests pass under Java 21. Device execution and trustworthy whole-app coverage remain open.

Connected Android validation continuation (2026-10-08): located the installed Android SDK and booted `Medium_Phone_API_36.1` with 4 GiB RAM; its API 36.1 extension initially caused transient Gradle `Unknown API Level` property-fetch failures, then the warm device was recognized. A focused Journal repair instrumentation test passed. The first direct all-tests invocation incorrectly included `@LargeTest` physical-performance profiles; after 14/30 tests the SwiftShader Play Store AVD disappeared during `profileAllEntityFamiliesOnHardware` (60-second testcase, empty failure body, no app-process fatal exception in its log). Repository comments and `scripts/run_connected_validation.sh` explicitly exclude `@LargeTest` because SwiftShader performance evidence is invalid and starves its sustained-sample window. Reran with the exact CI annotation filter `notAnnotation=androidx.test.filters.LargeTest`: all 20 eligible instrumentation tests pass, zero failures/errors/skips. Physical profiling remains unverified and must run on authorized physical devices; the AVD-only interrupted run is not product acceptance.

Screenshot publisher recursive-copy stability continuation (2026-10-08): coverage-guided review of `publish_screenshot_capture_set.py` found recursive unmanaged-directory copies verified only the opened directory inode before traversal. Concurrent additions/removals during traversal could therefore publish an inconsistent operator-owned tree. The copier now compares directory device, inode, mode, mtime, and ctime before opening, immediately after opening, and after recursive copy; detected mutation aborts before output promotion. Added cases for nested file mode/timestamp and symlink preservation, staged-entry precedence, exclusion of managed screenshots/session sidecars, FIFO rejection, and a deterministic directory-mutation injection. Focused publisher suite passes 16 tests; full Python suite passes 1,175 tests. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. This closes the reviewed publisher paths only; repo-wide source/runtime/device and human/release gates remain open.

Release PNG palette bounds review (2026-10-08): `_validate_png()` accepted indexed-color `PLTE` chunks with more entries than the image bit depth can represent. The PNG specification limits palette entry count to the range representable by the indexed pixel depth. Added that bound and a bit-depth-1, three-entry malformed PNG regression. The focused source-asset suite passes 14 tests, including all checked-in runtime assets; full Python discovery passes 1,176 tests. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Audio/container semantic validation, remaining uncovered source-asset branches, Android runtime behavior, and release gates remain under audit.

Release Ogg page-state validation continuation (2026-10-08): Ogg review found reserved page flags were accepted, and page continuation flags were not checked against prior-page lacing. The parser now rejects reserved flags, continuation on a stream's first page, repeated BOS, mismatched continuation state, and EOS with an unfinished packet. Added flag regressions with recomputed valid checksums. The 15-test focused source-asset suite passes; direct validation of checked-in assets reports 30 runtime assets (29 PNG, 1 font) and 15 required/checked audio files. Full Python discovery passes 1,177 tests under Coverage.py; current total is 81.6% statements, while `verify_release_source_assets.py` is 72.0% and remains under branch review. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. This validates structural container checks, not actual audio decoding or full runtime playback.

Release evidence candidate identity exactness review (2026-10-08): builder and verifier both stripped whitespace from candidate SHA strings inside JSON evidence before checking the lowercase 40-hex contract; the builder also stripped the caller's expected candidate SHA. A padded identity could therefore be promoted as candidate-bound and independently verified as the canonical candidate. Both paths now preserve raw spellings and reject any noncanonical candidate input. Added builder and verifier regressions using a padded nested `commit_sha`, plus a padded expected SHA case. Focused builder/verifier suites pass 13 and 17 tests; full Python discovery passes 1,179 tests under Coverage.py. Fresh branch coverage is 81.6% overall and 72.9% for the index verifier; `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. The verifier's remaining branches, other evidence validators, Android/runtime/device behavior, and release gates remain open.

Release font table-directory validation continuation (2026-10-08): `_validate_font()` checked table tags and bounds but did not enforce the OpenType directory's ascending tag order or four-byte table alignment. Added both structural checks, and corrected the synthetic valid-font fixture to emit sorted, aligned tables and valid search parameters. Regressions exercise unsorted tags and misaligned offsets. The 16-test source-asset suite passes, including the checked-in font; full Python discovery passes 1,180 tests under a fresh Coverage.py run. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Font table contents beyond required headers, remaining source-asset branches, runtime asset loading, Android/device behavior, and release gates remain open.

Release font metrics validation continuation (2026-10-08): the source-asset font check previously required `hhea` and `hmtx` table names but did not parse `hhea.numberOfHMetrics` or verify that `hmtx` holds enough metrics for `maxp.numGlyphs`. The validator now requires the 36-byte hhea header, constrains horizontal metric count to 1..glyphCount, and checks the derived minimum hmtx length. Tests cover zero metrics and a truncated metrics array. The 17-test source-asset suite passes, and direct validation accepts the checked-in font and assets. Full Python discovery passes 1,181 tests under Coverage.py; total statement coverage is 81.6%, and the source-asset module is 72.8%. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Other font-table semantics, remaining source-asset branches, Android runtime behavior, physical-device behavior, and release gates remain open.

Human acceptance hash spelling validation continuation (2026-10-08): the general text helper strips surrounding whitespace, so candidate SHA-1/SHA-256 identities and device/evidence SHA-256 values were normalized before lowercase-hex checks. A padded identity could therefore pass as candidate-bound. Added `_canonical_hash()` and applied it to all six human-acceptance digest fields without trimming. Regression tests exercise whitespace on each field. The focused human-acceptance suite passes 16 tests; full Python discovery passes 1,182 tests under fresh Coverage.py. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Full human acceptance workflow execution, Android/runtime, physical-device, and release gates remain open.

Play-delivery exact hash spelling and test-fixture repair (2026-10-08): Play-delivery validation used trimmed display strings for candidate commit/artifact/certificate identities, the installed-matrix digest, and external evidence digests. Padded hash spellings could therefore match canonical identities. Added exact lowercase-hex checks for each field and six whitespace regressions. The uppercase-hash regression itself had its assertion outside the temporary fixture scope, so it was passing/failing based on a deleted matrix; corrected its indentation so validation occurs while the fixture exists. Focused Play-delivery suite passes 17 tests; full Python discovery passes 1,183 tests under fresh Coverage.py. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Play-delivery evidence remains schema validation only; actual Play Console/operator evidence, device runtime behavior, and release gates are not proven by these tests.

Installed identity digest canonicalization continuation (2026-10-08): installed-candidate validation trimmed whitespace before checking candidate commit/certificate, device serial, installed-package certificate, APK and pulled-evidence digests. Installed-matrix validation did the same for its device-manifest and per-record digests. Padded values could pass canonical lowercase-hex checks, and the serial digest was not explicitly checked in the matrix layer. Added exact hash validation across both validators and regressions for each candidate/APK/evidence field plus both matrix references. Focused suites pass 17 candidate-identity and 23 matrix tests; full Python discovery passes 1,185 tests under fresh Coverage.py. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Current coverage and all live external evidence remain bounded evidence; Android/device/release acceptance and broad repository review remain open.

Screenshot capture identity exactness continuation (2026-10-08): the capture-session finalizer stripped candidate, origin, and APK hashes, while its sidecar parser stripped candidate/APK/image hashes and filename/scenario identities before validation. Padded values could be accepted as canonical. Hash fields now validate exact lowercase hex; sidecar text identities reject surrounding whitespace; requested hashes are not normalized. `expected_count` also now rejects bool, float, string, and nonpositive inputs with the declared finalizer error instead of allowing coercion or leaking a comparison `TypeError`. Added regressions across parser and finalizer. Both focused suites pass 10 tests; full Python discovery passes 1,187 tests under fresh Coverage.py. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Capture validation remains structural and does not establish physical-device screenshot authenticity or full app behavior.

Screenshot capture writer input validation (2026-10-08): the sidecar writer stripped text before validating it, allowing padded candidate/APK hashes and scenario names to be normalized and emitted as canonical evidence. It also leaked attribute/type errors for non-string metadata, accepted booleans as numeric settle times, and could leak an attribute error for a non-datetime capture timestamp. Writer inputs now reject surrounding whitespace, wrong value types, boolean settle times, and non-datetime timestamps with `CaptureEvidenceWriteError`; regressions cover these cases. The focused writer/reader/finalizer suites pass 26 tests; full Python discovery passes 1,187 tests. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. This validates writer contracts and fixture behavior, not the authenticity of device capture or exhaustive app behavior.

Declared dependency inventory identity and safe-read continuation (2026-10-08): the builder checked source parents with `lstat()` before reopening through a pathname, leaving a parent replacement window, and accepted unbounded source sizes. Reads now use the shared no-follow descriptor walker with an 8 MiB limit; deterministic parent-swap, leaf-symlink, and oversized-source regressions cover those boundaries. Review also found it required only two distinct plugin declarations without checking their identities; it now requires the Android application and Kotlin Android plugin IDs. Focused inventory tests pass 12 cases; full Python discovery passes 1,190 tests. Repository-wide review and Android/runtime/device/release gates remain open.

Landscape menu runtime visual audit and Android verification (2026-10-08): API 36.1 emulator screenshots showed title prose intersecting the arrival badge, homecoming consequence chips/text covering the willow and character, ambient bird crossing copy, and the COMFORT heading overlapping the Forest Journal chip. Menu spacing and bird placement were corrected, the journal chip was moved up to reserve a clear heading row, and layout/text-recording regressions were added. The landscape rerender is visually clear at 1280x720. Focused UI tests pass; full debug and release JVM suites each pass 1,291 tests with zero failures/errors/skips; ordinary API 36.1 connected instrumentation passes 20 tests with zero failures/errors/skips; debug/release lint each reports 84 warnings and no Error/Fatal findings; debug/release APK and release AAB assembly succeed. Full Python discovery passes 1,190 tests, `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. A fresh package inspection reports 0 native libraries for the 5.54 MB release APK and 6.36 MB AAB; this diagnostic was tagged with HEAD while the worktree was dirty, so it does not bind those artifacts to that commit, and it does not replace a 16 KB-page runtime device check. The first connected attempt timed out during APK installation on a high-load six-core emulator; a cold two-core/2 GiB retry passed. One full JVM run hit a JBR C2 compiler SIGSEGV in Robolectric `ShadowSharedPreferencesEditorImpl.apply`; the forced rerun with `-XX:TieredStopAtLevel=1` completed all requested Android tasks. Physical-device profiling, human visual/accessibility acceptance, signing/store delivery, and broad repository review remain open.

Evidence compiler Python API input hardening (2026-10-08): coverage-guided review found that all five `compile_bundle()` public APIs could leak Python `TypeError`/`ValueError` for non-object input and non-JSON values, although their CLI file inputs use strict JSON. The human acceptance, Play delivery, installed identity matrix, release governance, and physical device acceptance compilers now reject non-mapping drafts and wrap copy/serialization failures in their declared compiler exception; device acceptance serialization now also forbids NaN/Infinity. Regression tests cover every compiler. The combined focused compiler suites pass 94 tests. Full Python discovery at each audit step is recorded below in chronological entries; exhaustive repository review and all physical-device, human-acceptance, and release gates remain open.

Five-compiler regression integration (2026-10-08): after hardening all five compiler APIs, full Python discovery passes 1,210 tests; `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass.

Android package snapshot race check (2026-10-08): page-size inspection froze artifact bytes while hashing but only compared the byte count to its initial size. Same-size in-place mutation could therefore yield a digest for bytes read during a changing file. It now opens the lexical path with `O_NOFOLLOW`, checks descriptor and path identity plus mode/size/timestamps before and after snapshotting, and rejects mutation. A deterministic same-size rewrite regression passes with the focused package-inspector suite (24 tests); full Python discovery passes 1,211 tests, `compileall`, and `git diff --check`.

Capture session finalizer identity inputs (2026-10-08): `finalize_capture_session()` called `.strip()` on serial/package/activity inputs before checking types, so malformed direct API calls leaked `AttributeError`; it also normalized padded identity values. It now requires exact nonblank strings and rejects surrounding whitespace. New regressions cover null, integer, empty, and padded values for each field; focused finalizer/contract suites pass 26 tests; full Python discovery passes 1,194 tests, `compileall`, and `git diff --check`. Screenshot evidence remains structural and does not prove capture authenticity.

Release source-asset container validation (2026-10-08): the PNG verifier checked decompressed byte length only for non-interlaced images and accepted illegal scanline filters and lowercase reserved bits in chunk names. It now validates filter bytes and exact row geometry for all seven Adam7 passes, including valid interlaced fixtures, and enforces the chunk-type reserved bit. M4A validation now rejects duplicate/incomplete `ftyp` and empty `mdat`; WAV validation checks PCM rate/block alignment and extensible format bounds; MP3 validation requires a complete frame and rejects reserved emphasis values; raw resource filenames must satisfy Android's lowercase naming form. Malformed and valid fixtures cover each finding. The focused source-asset suite passes 22 tests, including the checked-in asset catalogue; full Python discovery passes 1,199 tests, `compileall`, and `git diff --check`. The live verifier reports 30 assets and 15 audio resources; Pillow fully decodes all 29 runtime PNGs, ffprobe accepts all 15 checked-in Ogg files, and fc-scan identifies the bundled font. The custom checks remain structural for M4A/WAV/MP3 because those formats are not present in the checked-in resource set.

Release evidence index candidate-binding input validation (2026-10-08): builder and verifier recursively collected candidate identity fields only when their values were strings, silently treating an explicitly present null/number/list identity as absent. Both now reject malformed identity field types. Their required-kind APIs also now validate non-string/non-iterable inputs through their declared errors and interpret a string argument as one kind rather than its characters. Regression tests cover malformed explicit identities and required-kind values. The focused builder/verifier/contract suites pass 36 tests; full repository Python discovery passes 1,202 tests, `compileall`, and `git diff --check`.

Supply-chain provenance validation hardening (2026-10-08): the validator used host `Path` semantics for declared source-file and resolved report paths. On Linux, Windows drive paths and backslash traversal strings could pass; these fields now require canonical POSIX repository-relative paths. Review also found strict JSON accepts escaped unpaired surrogates that could leak `UnicodeEncodeError` through UTF-8 canonicalization, and a non-string expected commit could leak regex `TypeError`. Invalid Unicode and malformed API values now raise `SupplyChainEvidenceError`. Focused validator, purl-binding, and workflow contract suites pass 22 tests; full Python discovery passes 1,206 tests, `compileall`, and `git diff --check`.

Release evidence index path symmetry (2026-10-08): stable wrapper and index builder accepted drive-prefixed paths and normalized repeated separators while the verifier required canonical POSIX spelling. Wrapper, builder, and verifier now reject colon/drive syntax and noncanonical spellings consistently, preventing platform-dependent path interpretation and builder/verifier disagreement. Focused builder, stable-builder, verifier, and output-safety suites pass 48 tests; full Python discovery passes 1,208 tests, `compileall`, and `git diff --check`.

Exact-head verification continuation (2026-10-08): reviewed the GameView frame drawing boundary; `draw`, `update`, input, accessibility routing, lifecycle geometry, and run-state decisions serialize on `runtimeStateLock`, so the hypothesized input/draw race is not present in this path. Forced a fresh execution of `testDebugUnitTest testReleaseUnitTest lintDebug lintRelease --rerun-tasks` with Android Studio JBR 21: BUILD SUCCESSFUL, 1,291 JVM tests per variant with 0 failures/errors/skips, and 84 lint warnings per variant (0 errors). Re-ran Python discovery: 1,211 tests pass; `git diff --check` passes. Connected-device instrumentation remains unavailable because `adb` is not installed in this environment. Physical-device, human acceptance, store/signing, and broad behavioral coverage gates remain open; this is not an exhaustive-completion claim.

Asset-format edge validation continuation (2026-10-08): adversarial WAV fixtures reproduced acceptance of PCM payloads whose length was not divisible by the sample-frame block alignment, and extensible WAV headers with arbitrary subformat GUIDs or out-of-range valid-bit counts. The verifier now requires complete PCM/float frames and validates extensible GUIDs against PCM/IEEE float, valid-bit bounds, format alignment and float precision. Ogg verification previously accepted only a Vorbis/Opus prefix; it now validates complete Vorbis identification header fields and OpusHead version/channel/mapping structure. New malformed and valid codec fixtures first failed against the old behavior and pass after the fixes. The focused source-asset suite passes 25 tests; full Python discovery passes 1,214 tests, compileall and `git diff --check` pass. Fresh branch coverage is 89% overall and 76% for `verify_release_source_assets.py`; all 15 checked-in audio files pass ffprobe, and the live source-asset verifier reports 30 assets and 15 audio resources. Lower-covered evidence validators and Android/runtime/device/release acceptance remain open.

Connected runtime verification continuation (2026-10-08): the Android SDK `adb` was present outside PATH, and the API 36.1 `Medium_Phone_API_36.1` emulator was booted with a 3 GiB guest limit and two vCPUs. `JAVA_HOME=/opt/android-studio/jbr PATH=/opt/android-studio/jbr/bin:$PATH ./gradlew connectedDebugAndroidTest -Pandroid.testInstrumentationRunnerArguments.notAnnotation=androidx.test.filters.LargeTest` completed successfully: 20 tests, zero failures and skips. This validates the filtered emulator suite only; `@LargeTest` physical-performance profiles and real-device acceptance remain open.

Evidence path canonicalization audit (2026-10-08): cross-layer probes found the release-evidence builder/verifier accepted control characters and malformed Unicode; the stable-index builder did not reject those spellings before filesystem access. Human acceptance, Play delivery, installed identity/matrix, governance and device acceptance silently trimmed padded paths, while device acceptance also accepted drive and repeated-separator spellings. Supply-chain source paths admitted control characters. Release-summary facts trimmed paths, normalized redundant POSIX segments and allowed malformed Unicode to reach `lstat`; its existing test explicitly requires safe Windows-style relative separators to remain accepted, so that compatibility is preserved while absolute/drive/traversal/noncanonical paths are rejected. Each finding has regression coverage, including unpaired surrogate exception behavior. The focused path and affected-validator suites pass (83 cases across the first integrated group; 23 release-summary cases); full Python discovery passes 1,218 tests, compileall and `git diff --check` pass. Fresh branch coverage is 89% overall. Audit remains open; lower-covered validators, broader Kotlin/runtime behavior, physical-device and human/release acceptance remain outstanding.

Screenshot-session finalizer boundary continuation (2026-10-08): direct API probes showed a non-datetime `captured_at_utc` (string, integer, or `date`) escaped as `AttributeError` while reading `.tzinfo`. The finalizer now rejects these values with `CaptureSessionFinalizeError` before timestamp comparison. The focused finalizer suite passes 12 tests; full Python discovery passes 1,219 tests, compileall and `git diff --check` pass. Coverage remains 89% overall; finalizer coverage is 74%, so its uncovered filesystem/error branches remain for follow-up. API 36.1 ordinary connected suite remains at 20/20 from the exact-head run above; no emulator is currently attached.

Runtime-asset ownership audit continuation (2026-10-08): the runtime reference auditor followed a symlink used as the assets-root directory because recursive discovery did not inspect the root itself. It also used `Path.open()` after separate discovery, so a regular asset replaced by a symlink between discovery and hashing was followed. It now rejects a symlink root and hashes through bounded no-follow stable-file consumption using the runtime asset size limit. Deterministic tests reproduced both old accepts and now pass; live audit reports 30/30 owned runtime assets and the same five documented byte-identical alias groups. Screenshot-session finalizer review also found non-datetime direct API timestamps leaked `AttributeError`; invalid values now raise `CaptureSessionFinalizeError`. The finalizer suite passes 12 tests. Full Python discovery passes 1,221 tests, compileall and `git diff --check` pass; branch coverage remains 89% overall, with the runtime reference auditor at 77%. Lower-covered logic and the full repository/runtime/physical/human/release audit remain open.

Governance digest canonicalization continuation (2026-10-08): `_string()` strips surrounding whitespace, so governance candidate hashes and evidence-reference digests could be accepted after silent normalization when callers invoked the validator directly. Candidate commit/artifact/certificate digests and governance, device-acceptance and human-acceptance reference digests now require their original strings to be canonical lowercase hex without surrounding whitespace. Regression cases cover each candidate digest and all three reference paths. Governance validator tests pass (25), full Python discovery passes 1,223 tests, and `git diff --check` passes. Fresh branch coverage is 89% overall and 77% for `validate_release_governance.py`; this is a bounded pass, with its uncovered validation branches and broader Kotlin, runtime, device and release gates still open.

Android package member-integrity continuation (2026-10-08): page-size inspection previously trusted ZIP central-directory metadata without reading member data, allowing a package with a corrupted stored member and invalid CRC to be reported as package-inspection compatible. Inspection now streams every member under the existing per-entry and aggregate uncompressed size bounds, so decompression errors and CRC mismatches fail closed; observed uncompressed member sizes are checked against directory metadata. A byte-flipped member regression passes after the change. The focused page-size suite passes 26 tests; full Python discovery passes 1,225 tests, `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Branch coverage is 89% overall and 75% for the page-size inspector. This package check still does not establish 16 KiB device runtime compatibility; connected and physical-device gates remain separate and open.

Ogg continuation-page parsing continuation (2026-10-08): the asset verifier previously extracted an identification packet only from the page where its terminating lacing value appeared. A standards-shaped 276-byte OpusHead split across a 255-byte first page and a 21-byte continuation page reproduced rejection of a valid stream. The verifier now assembles the first packet across pages before checking the codec header. The source-asset suite passes 26 tests; full Python discovery passes 1,226 tests, `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Fresh branch coverage is 89% overall and 77% for the source-asset verifier; broader coverage and Android runtime/device/release gates remain open.

Ogg multiplexing continuation (2026-10-08): after packet reassembly was added, a second adversarial fixture interleaved a second logical stream between the split OpusHead pages. The parser incorrectly appended that other stream's packet bytes to the identification packet and rejected the valid first stream. Reassembly is now bound to the first BOS stream's serial number. Both split-packet and interleaving regressions pass; the source-asset suite passes 27 tests, full Python discovery passes 1,227 tests, `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Branch coverage remains 89% overall and 77% for the source-asset verifier; complete parser and end-to-end coverage remains outstanding.

OpenType table-structure audit continuation (2026-10-08): the font parser discarded table-directory checksums, accepted a corrupted `head.checkSumAdjustment`, and only checked that cmap/name record counts were nonzero. Regressions reproduced acceptance of changed table payload bytes and incomplete cmap/name record arrays. It now validates each table checksum with OpenType `head` handling, verifies the whole-font adjustment, bounds cmap encoding/subtable records, and bounds name/language-tag records and strings. The synthetic font fixture was corrected to contain valid checksums and minimally well-formed cmap/name structures. The source-asset suite passes 30 tests; the live source-asset verifier accepts the shipped font and reports 30 assets. Full Python discovery passes 1,230 tests, `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Fresh branch coverage is 89% overall and 75% for the source-asset verifier; remaining parser branches and app/device/release verification are open.

Exact-checkout Android connected continuation (2026-10-08): booted the API 36.1 `Medium_Phone_API_36.1` emulator from the current checkout and reran `connectedDebugAndroidTest` with `@LargeTest` excluded. All 20 instrumentation tests passed with zero skips/failures; the emulator was then shut down. This confirms the filtered emulator suite on this checkout only; physical-device performance and human/release acceptance remain open.

Supply-chain referenced-file verification continuation (2026-10-08): validation previously checked only the syntax of the declared source-file and resolved-report hashes; it did not recompute those hashes from repository files. The live evidence pair exposed a stale `releaseRuntimeClasspath.txt` digest that had been accepted. The end-to-end validator now consumes each referenced repository-relative file through the bounded no-follow stable reader and compares its actual digest. Regressions cover modified declared sources, modified reports, and symlinked source files. The live declared inventory and resolved SBOM were rebuilt from the current checkout/reports (12 direct dependency entries and 60 resolved components) and now validate against exact HEAD `eeccc677107f69039dbf71053c18a9038d4b5ff6`. Supply-chain validator tests pass (18) and purl binding tests pass (3); full Python discovery passes 1,232 tests, `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Branch coverage is 89% overall and 76% for the supply-chain validator; the broader repository audit remains open.

Declared dependency source completeness continuation (2026-10-08): end-to-end validation also accepted a nonempty but incomplete `sourceFiles` list, so an inventory could omit the Gradle wrapper or pinned Python requirements while retaining valid remaining file hashes. It now requires exactly the four source paths that define the declared plugin/module, wrapper, and Python CI requirement inputs. Regression cases cover omission and substitution with an unrelated valid repository file. The supply-chain validator suite passes 19 tests; the current live evidence pair validates; full Python discovery passes 1,233 tests, `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Overall branch coverage is 89%, supply-chain validator coverage is 76%, and the repository-wide audit remains open.

Parsed-object/source-byte binding and strict Unicode error continuation (2026-10-08): six dual-input validators accepted a parsed mapping independently from caller-provided bytes, then used those bytes for the resulting manifest digest. A caller could therefore validate one object while claiming another byte identity. `strict_json.loads_matching_object()` now strictly parses and type-sensitively compares bytes with the validated mapping; Play delivery, release governance, installed identity matrix/candidate, human acceptance, and device acceptance all enforce this binding. Regressions exercise each caller. The shared JSON reader also allowed an unpaired surrogate in its Python-string input to escape as raw `UnicodeEncodeError`; it now reports `StrictJsonError`, with a regression. Focused suites pass (17 strict JSON, 19 Play, 26 governance, 25 installed matrix, 18 installed candidate, 18 human, 18 device, 10 device hardening); full Python discovery passes 1,240 tests. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass; aggregate branch coverage is 89%. Governance and supply-chain validators remain at 77% and 76% coverage, and the full codebase/runtime/physical/human/release audit remains open.

Evidence-root symlink boundary continuation (2026-10-08): five candidate-bound evidence validators resolved `evidence_base` before checking child components, which silently converted a symlinked evidence root into an accepted real path. They now validate every root component with a no-follow directory descriptor and preserve the lexical path for subsequent stable file reads; their resolvers also reject parent traversal directly. Added shared and five-validator regression coverage plus a governance end-to-end case. Focused governance, human, Play, installed-matrix, installed-candidate, stable-reader, and cross-validator suites pass (27, 18, 19, 25, 18, 12, and 1 tests); full Python discovery passes 1,243 tests at 89% aggregate branch coverage. Java 21 debug and release unit reports each show 1,291 tests, zero failures/errors/skips; debug/release lint each show zero errors and 84 warnings. API 36.1 connected instrumentation passes 20/20 with no skips/failures after a first-boot package-manager delay; physical-device and release/human gates remain open. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. The full repository audit remains active.

Descriptor-relative directory cleanup path validation continuation (2026-10-08): `remove_directory_at(parent_fd, name)` accepted `".."`, absolute paths, and names containing separators. Since `dir_fd` operations interpret those as paths rather than one child, a direct cleanup call could traverse and unlink outside the intended directory before failing to remove the final path. It now rejects non-string, empty, dot, dot-dot, absolute, separator-containing, and NUL names before any filesystem operation; directory publication also rejects non-normal sibling basenames. Regressions verify external and parent sentinels survive rejected names and that a dot-dot publication path fails before changing either directory. Stable-file, screenshot-publisher, and store-graphics focused suites pass (14, 16, and 12 tests); full Python discovery passes 1,245 tests. Shared stable-file-reader branch coverage remains 77%, so additional fault/race branches are still under review. Android debug/release unit, lint, connected instrumentation, and build checks from this checkout remain as recorded above; exhaustive repository, physical-device, human-acceptance, and release verification are still incomplete.

Directory rollback diagnostics and fsync fault continuation (2026-10-08): injected recursive cleanup failure after one old file had already been deleted showed that atomic directory promotion reported a partial backup as if the complete previous directory remained. Its post-commit cleanup error now says the backup may be partial; screenshot-set publication uses the same accurate wording. Separate fsync injections reproduced a `TypeError` in both rollback routes when the backup rename succeeded, cleared `backup_name`, and the following parent fsync failed. Those handlers now report that the previous directory was restored at the destination and that directory sync failed. Regressions verify restored contents, staging presence, and partial-backup state. Stable-file, store-graphics, and screenshot-publisher suites pass (16, 12, and 16 tests); full Python discovery passes 1,247 tests at 89% aggregate branch coverage. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Remaining stable-file failure branches and repository-wide runtime, physical-device, human, and release gates are still open.

Post-cleanup durability diagnostic and evidence-root callsite continuation (2026-10-08): a parent `fsync` failure after recursive backup removal was reported as backup-cleanup failure even though no backup path remained. Directory and screenshot publishers now say the output was published, the backup was removed, and directory sync failed; injections verify the committed tree and absent backup. A broader callsite search also found device and human bundle validators resolving the evidence root before their nested no-follow path checks, and device acceptance compilation doing the same. Those pre-resolutions are removed; root checks now run in the resolver across all six candidate-bound validators. Added end-to-end compiler and top-level device/human symlink-root regressions. Focused changed suites pass; full Python discovery passes 1,252 tests at 89% aggregate branch coverage and 78% for `stable_file_reader.py`. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. App unit/lint/emulator results are from the prior app-source pass; remaining repository-wide components, physical-device, human-acceptance, and release gates remain open.

Release-readiness evidence-root continuation (2026-10-08): a final root-normalization search found `validate_release_readiness` resolving its evidence root before `_safe_relative_path` checked components, allowing a caller-supplied symlink root when evidence paths were supplied through the resolved target. The helper now validates the root through a no-follow directory descriptor, checks each component with `lstat`, and returns the lexical path for stable bounded reads; the top-level gate no longer resolves the root first. Helper and complete-gate symlink-root regressions pass. Full Python discovery passes 1,254 tests at 89% aggregate branch coverage; release-readiness tests pass (21). The audit of all repository and release tooling remains incomplete, as do physical-device, human-acceptance, and release publication gates.

Release-summary and asset-provenance root continuation (2026-10-08): the release-summary verifier resolved both its source root and release-summary directory before no-follow checks, masking symlink aliases; asset-provenance scanning similarly resolved its repository root before walking source assets. Both now validate directory components using the shared no-follow descriptor helper and preserve lexical paths. New regressions reject source/release root aliases and asset-scan root aliases. Focused release-summary and asset-provenance suites pass (22 and 11 tests); full Python discovery passes 1,256 tests. This pass covers root aliases but not all races in recursive asset enumeration, remaining Python branch gaps, complete Kotlin state-space, physical-device behavior, human acceptance or final release gates. The full repository audit remains open.

Release-evidence index root continuation (2026-10-08): the builder, stable snapshot builder, independent verifier, and publisher normalized the caller's evidence root before checking evidence paths, so symlinked root aliases could evade the no-follow component boundary. These entry points now validate and retain the lexical root through the shared directory-descriptor helper. A CLI wrapper also resolved `--root` before reaching the fixed library function; it now preserves the input path, with a regression proving no output is published for a linked root. Coverage includes builder/verifier/publisher/CLI aliases and FIFO rejection while permitting the required safe directory-descriptor opens. Focused builder/verifier/publication suites pass (15, 22, and 5 tests), and the stable builder suite passes (9). The CLI regression was included in the subsequent complete discovery, which now passes 1,276 tests at 89% overall branch-aware coverage. Broader path races, uncovered validator branches, Kotlin/runtime state space, physical-device, human, and release gates remain open.

Source and dependency root-boundary continuation (2026-10-08): the release source-asset parser, store-graphics verifier, declared dependency inventory builder, and supply-chain evidence validator also resolved caller-provided repository roots before walking/hash-binding source files. They now reject symlinked roots through the shared no-follow directory checker. Added source-root alias regressions for each entry point. Focused suites pass: source assets 31, store graphics 15, dependency inventory 13, and supply-chain validation 20 tests. Full Python discovery passes 1,263 tests; `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Broader race coverage, Kotlin/runtime state space, physical-device, human-acceptance, and release gates remain open.

Scenario-source root and file continuation (2026-10-08): scenario-trace validation still resolved the root inside `validate_trace()`, defeating the earlier CLI/source-loader root check. The source contract reader also resolved each Kotlin source path and performed a path-based read after separate metadata checks, which followed symlinks and left a replacement race. Validation now passes the lexical root through to the no-follow directory check, and Kotlin source text is consumed through the bounded stable-file descriptor reader. Added regressions for direct trace root aliases and symlinked source files. Focused source-contract and trace suites pass (10 each); full Python discovery passes 1,266 tests. Remaining implementation paths, races, Android state-space, physical-device, human-acceptance, and release gates are still open.

Evidence compiler draft/output path continuation (2026-10-08): test-first reproduction showed `compile_device_acceptance.compile_file()` resolved a symlinked draft path before its stable reader, accepted the target, and published output. The same pattern existed in all five human/device/governance/Play/installed-matrix compilers and also canonicalized output/summary parents before the transactional publisher's no-follow checks. Added a shared `absolute_lexical_path()` helper and changed all five compilers to retain lexical paths through containment checks, bounded no-follow draft reads, and transactional publication. Regression tests reject symlinked drafts across all five and symlinked output/summary parents; focused suites pass (device 15, governance 28, Play 20, human 20, installed matrix 26). Current Android verification refresh (2026-10-08): from this exact worktree, Gradle completed `testDebugUnitTest`, `testReleaseUnitTest`, `lintDebug`, `lintRelease`, `assembleDebug`, and `bundleRelease`. Debug and release XML reports each record 1,291 tests with zero failures, errors, or skips; both lint reports show zero errors and 84 warnings. `connectedDebugAndroidTest` most recently passed all 26 non-`@LargeTest` tests on a clean boot of `Medium_Phone_API_36.1`, with zero failures/errors/skips, confirmed from XML. A prior resource-pressured attempt aborted after 15 tests; after isolating the failures and rebooting, the full suite passed. Stable-file rollback FIFO race continuation (2026-10-08): branch review of the 78%-covered stable-file publisher layer found that `atomic_write_bytes`, `atomic_replace_file`, and `atomic_publish_files` statted an existing destination and then opened it for rollback copying without `O_NONBLOCK`. A concurrent regular-file-to-FIFO replacement between those operations could block a publisher indefinitely. A deterministic injected swap reproduced the unsafe open flags in all three paths without hanging the test process; each rollback snapshot open now includes `O_NONBLOCK`, and a shared regression asserts this while confirming the replacement FIFO is rejected before snapshotting. The focused stable-file suite passes 18 tests. The independent evidence-index verifier also gained a race regression that swaps an already inspected regular file to a FIFO immediately before its leaf open and confirms rejection without blocking; its focused suite passes 23 tests. Full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,274 tests at 89% total coverage, with `stable_file_reader.py` at 79% and `verify_release_evidence_index.py` at 75%. Remaining uncovered branches and repository/runtime/physical/human/release coverage remain open.

Stable-index failure cleanup parent-swap continuation (2026-10-08): a deterministic verifier failure swapped the evidence-root directory to a symlink before `build_stable_index()` cleanup. The old `Path.unlink()` followed that parent alias and deleted an external sentinel sharing the index basename. Cleanup now retains a no-follow descriptor for the publication directory and removes the published index only when its inode metadata still matches the captured publication identity. The adversarial parent-swap test preserves the external sentinel; the focused stable-index suite passes 12 tests. Full Python discovery passes 1,276 tests at 89% overall branch-aware coverage; other post-publication races and remaining code coverage stay under audit.


Stable-index verification binding continuation (2026-10-08): the builder now compares the output inode, size, and timestamps through its held no-follow parent descriptor after the independent verifier returns. A deterministic verifier wrapper that replaces the output after reading it confirms the builder rejects the stale summary. The focused stable-index suite passes 12 tests; full Python discovery passes 1,276 tests at 89% overall coverage.


Entity collision and full JVM verification refresh (2026-10-08): reviewed Player locomotion, hitbox transitions, Entity collision geometry, EntityManager outcome arbitration, and deferred Eagle/Owl escape resolution against their integration/property tests. This source pass found no additional confirmed defect. A cross-reference inventory found 190 production Kotlin files; every file stem appears in unit or instrumentation test source, though identifier presence alone does not establish behavioral coverage. With Java 21, fresh `testDebugUnitTest` and `testReleaseUnitTest` runs each report 1,291 tests, zero failures/errors/skips; fresh `lintDebug`/`lintRelease` report zero errors and 84 warnings each, while `assembleDebug` and `bundleRelease` succeed with Gradle reporting their package tasks up-to-date; a focused run of collision arbitration/outcomes, Eagle/Owl escape, multipart sweep, and Player boundary/physics tests reports 47 tests with zero failures/errors/skips.
