Capture-harness failure cleanup continuation (2026-10-08): source review of `HardwareCoreFlowCaptureTest.saveScreenshot()` found that PixelCopy/MediaStore failures could leave an allocated bitmap unrecycled and an inserted `IS_PENDING` row stranded. The screenshot writer now uses `PendingCaptureMediaPublisher`, which checks the pending-to-visible update, deletes an inserted row on failed publication, preserves cleanup exceptions as suppressed, and always releases the bitmap. A late PixelCopy completion after timeout owns deferred bitmap recycling through an atomic completion state. Six fake-store connected regressions cover success, output-stream failure, failed visibility update, failed insert, thrown cleanup failure, and zero-row cleanup without touching MediaStore. The six publisher tests pass when isolated, and `lintDebug` succeeds with zero errors and 84 warnings. An earlier 26-test non-`@LargeTest` run aborted after 15 tests while emulator low-memory killing was active; `bloomPreventsImmediateCollisionDeath` and `bestRunPersistsGhostAndReloadsOnNextLaunch` each passed in isolation. After a clean emulator boot, the full 26-test suite passed with zero failures/errors/skips. This completes the emulator lane for the current connected suite, but physical-device harnesses remain unverified. Physical capture/performance evidence tests were inspected but deliberately excluded because emulator captures cannot support physical-device claims. The repository audit remains active.


Pending-row deletion result continuation (2026-10-08): `PendingCaptureMediaPublisher` now treats a zero-row deletion result as cleanup failure and attaches it to the primary publication failure, matching `ContentResolver.delete()` result semantics. The sixth fake-store regression verifies this case and bitmap release. All six publisher tests pass in isolation; the complete 26-test ordinary connected suite then passes on a clean API 36.1 emulator boot with zero failures/errors/skips. `lintDebug` and `git diff --check` pass.

Stable-index cleanup-denial continuation (2026-10-08): added a regression for the failure branch where independent verification fails and the safe unlink of the just-published index is denied. It verifies that the raised error reports cleanup denial and leaves the still-owned artifact present. The focused stable-index suite passes 12 tests; full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,277 tests in 43.7 seconds, with 89% aggregate coverage. This is a fresh complete Python test run, not proof of exhaustive semantic coverage; remaining branches and non-Python/runtime/physical/human/release lanes stay open.

Evidence-index verifier consistency continuation (2026-10-08): added a deterministic race regression that mutates the index after evidence has been read but before verification completes. The verifier's final stable reread rejects the changed index. The focused verifier suite passes 24 tests; full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,278 tests in 43.1 seconds at 89% overall coverage. No production change was needed; this adds direct evidence for the verifier's end-to-end snapshot invariant. Other verifier edge cases, remaining source coverage, and app/runtime/physical/human/release acceptance remain under audit.

Transactional rollback race continuation (2026-10-08): a deterministic failure injection showed `atomic_publish_files()` restored its backup over a destination that a concurrent writer had changed after publication. The same unchecked rollback pattern existed in `atomic_write_bytes()` and `atomic_replace_file()`. Publishers now retain the identity of each staged/published output, verify it before rollback, and refuse to overwrite a changed destination while retaining recovery material. Regression tests cover later-step failure, a mutation before identity capture, single-file sync failure, and atomic replacement sync failure. The focused stable-file suite passes 21 tests; full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,281 tests in 44.1 seconds, with 89% overall coverage. This addresses the reproduced rollback data-loss path; residual filesystem races, uncovered branches, and all non-Python acceptance remain open.

Atomic source recheck continuation (2026-10-08): `atomic_replace_file()` now rechecks the staged source identity after backup preparation and immediately before rename. A deterministic test mutates the source while the old destination is being snapshotted and confirms that the output remains unchanged and the mutation is rejected. The focused stable-file suite passes 22 tests; current full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,282 tests in 45.1 seconds at 89% overall coverage. `compileall scripts` and `git diff --check` pass. The broad repository audit and non-Python acceptance remain open.

Raw-resource directory contract continuation (2026-10-08): `verify_release_source_assets()` previously filtered `res/raw` with `Path.is_file()`, silently ignoring FIFO and other non-regular entries alongside valid assets. A valid fixture plus an unrelated FIFO reproduced the false pass. It now checks the directory through the no-follow directory validator, reports enumeration failures, and requires every direct entry to be a regular file before filename/content validation. The focused source-asset suite passes 32 tests; full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,283 tests in 46.8 seconds at 89% overall coverage. Script compilation and `git diff --check` pass; other release asset parser semantics and the repo-wide audit remain open.

Font cmap structural validation continuation (2026-10-08): reviewed `_validate_cmap_table()` against the [OpenType cmap specification](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/typography/opentype/spec/cmap). A format 4 table with zero segments and a format 6 table with a nonzero entry count but no glyph array both passed the prior minimum-length checks. Added format 4 segment/header/array checks and dynamic array-length checks for cmap formats 2, 6, 8, 10, 12, 13, and 14; malformed format 4/6 regressions now fail, and the checked-in font remains accepted. The focused source-asset suite passes 34 tests; full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,285 tests in 44.7 seconds with 89% aggregate coverage. Script compilation and `git diff --check` pass. Remaining cmap semantics, parser coverage, and all broader repo/runtime/device/release coverage are still under audit.

Cmap bounds and group-order continuation (2026-10-08): a format 14 selector with an out-of-range mapping-table offset passed verification; a regression reproduced it. Format 14 now validates selector order, table offsets, nested record bounds, and sorted/non-overlapping mapping values. Format 8/12/13 character groups now require valid ordered, non-overlapping code ranges; format 2/6/10 validate subrange bounds. A malformed format 12 group regression passes by rejecting its inverted range. The focused source-asset suite passes 36 tests; full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,287 tests in 45.6 seconds at 89% aggregate coverage. `compileall scripts` and `git diff --check` pass. Glyph-index validity, remaining cmap details, and all non-Python/full-repository acceptance remain open.

Format 14 mapping-array regression continuation (2026-10-08): added direct positive and negative checks for format 14 default/non-default mapping data, including truncated/count-mismatched ranges. The focused source-asset suite passes 37 tests; current full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,288 tests in 44.7 seconds at 89% aggregate coverage. `compileall scripts` and `git diff --check` pass. The cmap validator still has uncovered branches and does not prove every glyph mapping corresponds to a real glyph; full-repo and non-Python acceptance remains active.

Font table overlap continuation (2026-10-08): a synthetic font with valid individual `head` and `hhea` checksums but a two-byte shared table region passed the verifier. The [OpenType DSIG requirements](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/typography/opentype/spec/dsig) specify non-overlapping tables. `_validate_font()` now sorts the table ranges and rejects overlap; the regression confirms rejection while existing checked-in font tests stay green. The focused source-asset suite passes 38 tests; full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,289 tests in 41.9 seconds at 89% aggregate coverage. Script compilation and `git diff --check` pass; glyph-index semantics, remaining source-asset branches, and repo-wide/runtime/device/release acceptance remain open.

OpenType offset and outline-table continuation (2026-10-08): font review against the [OpenType offset-table and required-table specification](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/typography/opentype/otspec182/otff) found that the parser accepted wrong `searchRange`/`entrySelector`/`rangeShift`, omitted shared required `OS/2` and `post` tables, accepted TrueType fonts without `glyf`/`loca`, and accepted `OTTO` without a `CFF`/`CFF2` outline table. It now checks these header/table relationships, and the synthetic font builder includes the required tables. Regressions cover bad search fields, missing TrueType outlines, and `OTTO` without CFF. The focused source-asset suite passes 40 tests; the live verifier accepts all 30 shipped assets (29 PNGs and one font, plus 15/15 checked/required audio resources). Full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,295 tests in 39.5 seconds with 89% aggregate coverage and 74% source-asset verifier coverage; `compileall scripts` and `git diff --check` pass. Detailed cmap glyph semantics, the verifier's remaining 26% uncovered code/branches, Kotlin/runtime/device/human/release validation, and the repository-wide bug hunt remain open.

Source-asset malformed-input sweep (2026-10-08): passed every strict prefix truncation of representative PNG/font/Ogg/WAV/MP3/M4A fixtures (1,347 cases) through the matching parser; each produced the documented validation error with no accepted truncation or leaked exception. Also flipped each individual bit across those fixtures (10,776 cases): no parser leaked an unexpected exception. Some mutations remain structurally accepted in audio sample/frame/box payloads, as these checks validate container/header structure rather than decode or authenticate media content. This is a bounded adversarial sweep, not exhaustive media-format conformance or a replacement for the open runtime/device/release checks.

WAV PCM container-width continuation (2026-10-08): a 7-bit PCM sample width passed the WAV validator because block alignment used a ceiling division. PCM and IEEE-float container widths now must be whole-byte multiples before the block-alignment check; a malformed 7-bit regression is rejected. The focused source-asset suite passes 41 tests and the shipped-asset audit remains 30/30 assets and 15/15 audio resources. Full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,296 tests in 37.9 seconds with 89% aggregate coverage and 74% coverage for `verify_release_source_assets.py`; `compileall scripts` and `git diff --check` pass. The asset parser and broader repository/runtime/device/release audit remain incomplete.

OpenType OS/2/post table continuation (2026-10-08): required font-table presence alone still accepted malformed `OS/2` and `post` tables. The font validator now checks OS/2 versions 0-5 against their minimum lengths and checks post versions 1.0, 2.0, 2.5, and 3.0 for version-specific lengths and glyph-count/name-index/string bounds. Synthetic regressions cover unsupported OS/2/post versions; the actual shipped TTF (post 2.0, OS/2 4) passes. The focused source-asset suite passes 42 tests; current full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,297 tests in 41.1 seconds at 89% aggregate coverage, with 73% coverage for the source-asset verifier. `compileall scripts`, live verification of 30 assets / 15 audio resources, and `git diff --check` pass. Deep outline/glyph semantics and the whole-repository, runtime, device, human and release audit remain open.

TrueType glyph-outline continuation (2026-10-08): `loca` bounds were checked, but glyph blocks were otherwise treated as opaque. Review against the [OpenType glyf](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/typography/opentype/spec/glyf) and [loca](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/typography/opentype/spec/loca) requirements found missing first/final offset, word-alignment, maxp-version, contour, point-flag, coordinate-array, component-index, and composite-cycle checks. The verifier now checks TrueType maxp/head glyph-format agreement, exact loca length and endpoints, sorted aligned offsets, simple-glyph data bounds, composite component records, and composite acyclicity using an iterative traversal. Tests cover malformed maxp/loca/glyph headers, composite self-cycle, and both short and long loca formats. An initial overly tight glyph-padding bound rejected the shipped font; three-byte alignment padding observed in glyph 6 is now accepted. Focused asset tests pass (43), live audit accepts all 30 shipped assets, and current full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,298 tests in 52.7 seconds (89% aggregate, 73% source-asset verifier). `compileall scripts` and `git diff --check` pass. Full TrueType instruction validation, CFF bytecode validation, remaining parser branches, and all app/runtime/device/human/release audits remain open.

TrueType glyph parser stress continuation (2026-10-08): added a 1,200-level composite chain fixture to verify the acyclicity walk does not depend on Python recursion depth; the chain passes, while a self-cycle fails. The fixture also exercises both loca encodings, and the shipped font plus all source-asset regressions pass. Fresh full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,298 tests in 44.8 seconds, with 89% aggregate and 73% source-asset verifier coverage. A strict-prefix sweep over the 724-byte synthetic font rejected all 724 truncations and leaked no exceptions; all 5,792 single-bit changes also produced controlled validation failures. `compileall scripts` and `git diff --check` pass. Remaining glyph instruction semantics, complete font conformance, and all broader repository/runtime/device/human/release checks remain open.

Composite glyph instruction-flag continuation (2026-10-08): a standards reread found that `WE_HAVE_INSTRUCTIONS` may be set on any component and the instruction block follows the final component. The parser had only inspected the last component's flags, which could reject otherwise valid composites. It now accumulates the flag across the component records; a two-component positive fixture sets it on the first component and passes. Current full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,298 tests in 46.4 seconds (89% aggregate, 73% source-asset verifier); the focused 43-test asset suite, real 30-asset/15-audio audit, compilation, and diff check pass. OpenType glyph instructions are structurally bounded but not interpreted, and remaining repo/app/runtime/device/release checks remain active.

Release audio-list source binding continuation (2026-10-08): `parse_required_audio()` stopped at the first `REQUIRED_AUDIO` assignment, so a later Python reassignment or `+=` could change the actual preparer's required list while the source verifier continued checking the earlier value. The parser now requires exactly one top-level literal assignment and rejects annotated, augmented, nested, duplicate, or other detected bindings. Regressions cover reassignment, `+=`, and annotated assignment. The focused source-asset suite passes 44 tests; full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,299 tests in 47.4 seconds at 89% overall coverage and 74% source-asset verifier coverage. `compileall scripts`, shipped 30-asset / 15-audio verification, and `git diff --check` pass. The repository-wide, app/runtime/device/human/release audit remains active.

Release audio-list pattern-binding continuation (2026-10-08): the assignment audit was extended to detect Python match-pattern bindings and type aliases that can also rebind a module name. New cases reject both forms in addition to ordinary reassignment, augmented assignment, and annotated assignment. The focused asset suite passes 44 tests; fresh full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,299 tests in 45.0 seconds at 89% aggregate coverage and 74% source-asset verifier coverage. Live source-asset verification, compilation, and whitespace checks pass. Other source-asset branches and all broader repository/runtime/device/release lanes remain open.

Stable evidence-index requirement and snapshot memory continuation (2026-10-08): `build_stable_index()` accepted `require_bound_kinds` as an iterable but passed it first to the builder and then to the independent verifier. A generator was consumed during construction, so the independent verifier saw no required kinds; a regression reproduced the empty tuple. The wrapper now materializes the iterable exactly once, preserving string-as-single-kind semantics. Snapshotting also retained all input bytes in memory and then wrote a duplicate private-tree copy; it now streams each bounded source into a private snapshot file while hashing, and re-reads/hash-confirms sources without retaining their bytes. Focused stable-index tests pass (14); full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,301 tests in 50.5 seconds at 89% aggregate coverage, with 71% coverage for the stable wrapper. Script compilation and `git diff --check` pass. Remaining wrapper error/race branches and all repository/runtime/device/release acceptance remain open.

Stable evidence parent-swap continuation (2026-10-08): a deterministic probe swapped an already-checked evidence parent directory for a symlink immediately before leaf open; `_read_snapshot()` followed that ancestor and read the external file because `O_NOFOLLOW` protected only the leaf. Snapshot reads now traverse and hold the evidence parent with no-follow directory descriptors, open the leaf relative to that descriptor, and compare pre-open, descriptor, and post-read leaf identities. The new race regression fails on the prior code and passes now. Focused stable-index tests pass (15); current full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,302 tests in 52.1 seconds at 89% aggregate coverage, with 69% coverage for the updated stable wrapper. `compileall scripts` and `git diff --check` pass. Additional filesystem error/race branches and the broad app/runtime/device/release audit remain open.

Android raw-resource namespace continuation (2026-10-08): `verify_release_source_assets()` rejected duplicate stems only when the stem appeared in `REQUIRED_AUDIO`; two otherwise valid unrequired files such as `extra.ogg` and `extra.wav` passed despite colliding as Android raw-resource names. Duplicate stems are now rejected across the complete raw directory, with a regression reproducing the prior false accept. The focused asset suite passes 45 tests; the live tree still verifies 30 runtime assets and all 15 required audio resources. Full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,303 tests in 47.2 seconds at 89% aggregate coverage (74% source-asset verifier, 69% stable evidence-index wrapper); `compileall scripts` and `git diff --check` pass. Remaining source/parser and evidence-wrapper branches, plus broader repository/app/runtime/device/release acceptance, remain open.


Android package ELF and decompressor-error continuation (2026-10-08): ZIP inspection recognized native code only from `.so` filenames, so an ELF binary stored as `assets/plugins/runtime.bin` was reported as `no-native-code`. The bounded member scan now checks the first four uncompressed bytes and marks ELF magic as native regardless of filename; fail-closed mode rejects it. Adversarial payload mutation also showed `zipfile` could leak `lzma.LZMAError` for a ZIP_LZMA member; that decoder exception is now normalized to `PageSizeInspectionError`. Focused page-size tests pass (30); full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,293 tests in 47.9 seconds with 89% aggregate coverage and 75% page-size inspector coverage. `compileall scripts` and `git diff --check` pass. Review of the current coverage report identifies the source-asset verifier (73%, 825 statements) and its parser branches as the next high-risk area; Android runtime/device/release gates and the exhaustive repo audit remain open.

Training certificate symlink-safe publication continuation (2026-10-08): reproduced that `run_all_training._atomic_json()` wrote through a predictable `.tmp` symlink and could overwrite its target; output path resolution also followed a leaf symlink before stale-certificate deletion. Certificate output now uses lexical root containment, a held no-follow parent for stale leaf removal, and the stable file reader's random-name atomic publisher with symlink-leaf rejection. Regressions cover predictable temporary symlinks, output-leaf symlink target preservation, lexical path binding, and output-parent symlink rejection. Focused training-control tests pass (34); full Python discovery passes 1,307 tests in 39.7 seconds. The preceding branch-aware run was 1,306 tests (the final parent-symlink regression was added after that run); `run_all_training.py` had 68% coverage and the shared stable file reader 81% overall. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Full repo/runtime/device/human/release audit remains open.

Concurrent evidence publication race continuation (2026-10-08): deterministic injection between the last destination identity check and `os.replace()` showed `atomic_write_bytes()`, `atomic_replace_file()`, and `atomic_publish_files()` could overwrite a newer concurrent output. They now use Linux `renameat2` exchange for existing outputs and no-replace publication for absent outputs; after exchange, the displaced file is checked against its recovery snapshot by stable metadata and streaming SHA-256 to detect same-size writes with restored mtime. Tests cover all three publishers for both an intervening writer and a concurrent creator. Three existing caller tests that mocked `os.replace` were retargeted to the new atomic operation. Focused stable-file tests pass (25), caller failure-path tests pass (3), and branch-aware full Python discovery passes 1,310 tests in 36.7 seconds at 81% stable-file-reader coverage. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. This assumes Linux libc `renameat2`; unsupported systems fail closed. Remaining filesystem race/error branches and all non-Python/app/runtime/device/human/release coverage remain open.

Fresh Android validation continuation (2026-10-09): the default shell Java 17 run compiled but Robolectric rejected all SDK 36 test sandboxes before test bodies (`Android SDK 36 requires Java 21`). Reran with `/opt/android-studio/jbr` Java 21: Gradle succeeded for `testDebugUnitTest`, `lintDebug`, and `lintRelease`; current XML totals 1,291 tests, zero failures/errors/skips, and lint has zero errors / 84 warnings for each variant. Booted `Medium_Phone_API_36.1` and directly ran the filtered `connectedDebugAndroidTest` task because the repository wrapper requires a clean tree while this shared tree has 153 changed/untracked paths. All 26 non-`@LargeTest` connected tests passed with zero failures/errors/skips; emulator then shut down. These are current-worktree JVM/emulator checks, not physical-device, human, signing, Play delivery, or production acceptance; the full audit remains open.

Device-acceptance manifest schema continuation (2026-10-09): adversarial mutations showed the physical-device validator ignored unknown properties at nine boundaries: root, policy, threshold policy, session, device identity, scenario map, scenario result, evidence file, and performance metrics. Added exact-key checks for those contracts and a nine-subcase regression. The focused device-acceptance suite passes (19 tests), and full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,311 tests in 50.2 seconds; this also covers adjacent device compilation/aggregation and human acceptance modules. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Full branch coverage, remaining device/performance evidence fidelity, physical-device/human/release gates, and broader repository audit remain incomplete.

CLI summary-publication failure continuation (2026-10-09): a valid device-acceptance bundle plus a symlinked `--summary-output` reproduced an uncaught `EvidenceError` traceback instead of the validator's JSON failure result. The same unchecked writer call existed in human acceptance, Play delivery, installed-candidate identity, identity matrix, release governance, release readiness, and visual-regression CLIs. All now catch their domain/output errors, emit machine-readable invalid JSON, and return nonzero; symlink target preservation is tested on the device CLI and injected writer failures are tested across all seven acceptance/readiness CLIs plus visual regression. Focused cross-component suites pass (165 tests); full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,314 tests in 51.9 seconds. `compileall`, `git diff --check`, and exact-path coverage report pass; affected validator/visual modules have 79–90% branch-aware line coverage (coverage is not complete branch proof). Full repository/app/runtime/device/human/release audit remains open.

Stable file replacement same-path continuation (2026-10-09): a direct temporary-file probe called `atomic_replace_file(path, path)` and showed it returned successfully after unlinking the only file. The cause was exchanging a name with itself and then deleting the displaced destination name. The publisher now rejects source and destination resolving to the same absolute path before opening or mutating anything; a regression checks both the error and preservation of the file. The stable-file-reader suite passes 27 tests, full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,315 tests in 51.3 seconds, and `compileall` / `git diff --check` pass. Stable-file-reader coverage is 81% with 77 partially covered branches; transaction rollback race/error handling and the broader audit remain open.

Atomic byte-writer rollback race continuation (2026-10-09): deterministic injections at the rollback rename reproduced a second lost-update path: after the rollback identity check, an existing output's concurrent contents were overwritten by `os.replace`, and a newly published output's concurrent contents were removed by `os.unlink`. Rollback now atomically exchanges or moves the current name to a private name, verifies the displaced bytes and inode, and restores concurrent data or retains a recovery file on mismatch. Two regressions cover existing and absent prior outputs, alongside the preexisting between-check race test. Stable-file-reader tests pass (28); full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,317 tests in 45.3 seconds; `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Stable-file-reader coverage is 81% with 80 partially covered branches. The analogous post-check rollback paths in `atomic_replace_file()` and `atomic_publish_files()` still need adversarial review, as do remaining reader and directory-publication branches and the whole-repository audit.

Atomic replace and multi-output rollback race continuation (2026-10-09): deterministic faults at rollback operations reproduced the same check-then-mutate race in `atomic_replace_file()` and `atomic_publish_files()`. Single-file replacement could move a concurrent destination into the source name; multi-file publication overwrote the concurrent destination with its stale backup. Rollback now uses exchanges or no-replace moves, checks the displaced identity and bytes, and restores concurrent data or retains the transaction recovery tree. Regressions cover an existing and a new destination in each publisher. Stable-file-reader tests pass (32); branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,321 tests in 50.3 seconds; `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Stable-file-reader coverage is 80% with 85 partially covered branches. Remaining descriptor-reader, rollback failure, and directory-promotion branches, plus the exhaustive application/repository/device/human/release audit, remain open.

Directory-publisher collision and identity continuation (2026-10-09): adversarial injections showed plain `os.rename` could replace a concurrently created empty output or backup directory; it also accepted a staging directory swapped after validation, could later delete a destination swapped while being moved to backup, and recursively removed a replacement backup at cleanup. Directory publication transitions now use `renameat2` no-replace, verify the moved staging/destination inode, and bind recursive cleanup to the recorded backup inode. Tests cover those four race windows plus recovery-backup and destination-to-backup collisions. Four existing fault-injection tests were retargeted to `_rename_noreplace` after the implementation boundary changed. Focused stable-file-reader tests pass (37), and full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,326 tests in 45.9 seconds. `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass; stable-file-reader branch-aware coverage is 81% with 82 partially covered branches. Remaining filesystem race/error branches and broader Android/app/device/human/release verification remain open.

Descriptor tree-cleanup post-order race continuation (2026-10-09): an injected replacement of the root name immediately before `rmdir` proved the remover could delete a newly created empty directory and return success while leaving the original tree detached. Post-order removal now moves each expected directory name to a unique no-replace quarantine entry, verifies its inode, then removes it; if identity or removal checks fail it restores the entry or reports its retained recovery name. The root-swap regression and the full 38-test stable-file-reader suite pass. Full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,327 tests in 49.9 seconds; `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Stable-file-reader coverage is 81% with 84 partially covered branches. Descriptor traversal failure cases, remaining filesystem race branches, and full repository/app/runtime/device/human/release verification are still open.

Bounded reader and broad test refresh (2026-10-09): reviewed `consume_regular_file()` against its callers, descriptor-walk/no-follow guarantees, size and inode checks, and existing replacement/truncation/FIFO regressions; no additional confirmed defect was found. All 38 stable-file-reader tests pass. Current Python discovery passes 1,327 tests; `compileall scripts run_all_training.py` and `git diff --check` pass. A Java 17 Gradle attempt compiled production and test Kotlin but failed before test bodies in 139 SDK 36 Robolectric classes; rerunning with the installed Java 21 passes both debug and release suites (1,291 tests each, zero failures/errors/skips). The filtered API 36.1 emulator instrumentation suite passes 26/26 with zero skips/failures after one AVD package-service failure was resolved by a no-snapshot reboot. Physical-device performance profiles, physical hardware, human/store/release acceptance, and exhaustive repository/source coverage remain open; the audit continues.

Release asset verifier continuation (2026-10-09): a checksum-correct TrueType fixture mapped U+0020 to glyph ID 1 while `maxp.numGlyphs` was 1; cmap validation accepted the invalid reference. The verifier now bounds glyph IDs against `maxp` for supported cmap formats 0, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 13, and 14, with regressions for every format. A typed Kotlin asset constant (`const val OMITTED: String = ...`) was silently omitted from the path inventory; parsing now includes `String`/`kotlin.String` annotations and fails closed for unparsed declarations or initializer expressions. M4A `ftyp` boxes with a partial 32-bit compatible-brand field were accepted; the parser now requires brand-list bytes to align to four-byte entries. A malformed ID3v2.4 footer was also accepted; MP3 validation now requires the `3DI` marker and matching version, flags, and size, and rejects footer flags on older versions. A valid tagged MP3 control, malformed-footer cases, and a 756-header MPEG version/layer/bitrate/sample-rate/padding matrix pass. The source-asset verifier suite passes 51 tests (78% branch-aware coverage; partial branches remain), complete Python discovery passes 1,333 tests in 18.8 seconds, checked-in verification reports 30 runtime assets and 15 audio resources, and `compileall scripts run_all_training.py` / `git diff --check` pass. RIFF chunk-order review found that RIFF permits chunks in any order, so the existing WAV parser's order-independent scan is intentional. Remaining parser coverage, source/runtime/device/human/release gates, and exhaustive repository code coverage remain open.

SFX lifecycle serialization continuation (2026-10-09): inspection found that public playback wrappers evaluated mutable pool-local sample IDs before entering the synchronized `play()` method. A concurrent `destroy()`/`init()` could therefore pair an ID from the old pool with a replacement pool. All sample-ID lookup wrappers now synchronize on the same manager monitor as init/destroy/play; a reflection regression asserts every wrapper carries the JVM synchronized modifier. The focused Java 21 Gradle `SfxManagerTest` suite passes. This is a focused lifecycle regression check; the full Android test suites, remaining component/lifecycle audit, device/human/release gates, and exhaustive repository coverage remain open.

Music preference initialization race continuation (2026-10-09): `LeitmotifManager.init()` sampled `FeedbackSettings.audioEnabled` before taking `audioLock`. If a concurrent setting change updated the preference and manager state before `init()` acquired the lock, stale initialization could overwrite the disabled state and start playback. The preference snapshot is now read in the same locked section that publishes manager state; any later toggle still applies through `setAudioEnabled()`. Focused Java 21 Gradle tests for `LeitmotifManagerTest`, `FeedbackSettingsTest`, `SfxManagerTest`, and `SoundSampleReadinessTest` pass (25 tests). The initial Java 17 attempt failed before Robolectric test bodies because SDK 36 requires Java 21. The concurrent interleaving is not directly stress-tested yet; broader audio lifecycle, application, device, human/release, and exhaustive repository checks remain open.

Full Android unit-suite refresh after audio lifecycle fixes (2026-10-09): Java 21 `testDebugUnitTest` and `testReleaseUnitTest` both pass all 1,292 test cases across 241 test classes, with zero failures, errors, or skips in each variant. This supersedes the earlier 1,291/1,288 count in historical map entries for the current worktree. These are JVM/Robolectric suites; connected device, physical-device profile, human acceptance, store/release evidence, and complete repository behavior remain separate open gates.

Python and shell repository-wide refresh (2026-10-09): current `python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts -p 'test_*.py'` passes 1,333 tests in 18.5 seconds. `python3 -m compileall -q scripts run_all_training.py` passes, `bash -n` passes all 8 repository shell scripts, and `git diff --check` passes. The Python suite is repository-script test coverage, not proof that every CLI's external side effects or every branch have been exercised; shell syntax likewise is not operational acceptance.

Stable-file consumer race continuation (2026-10-09): current tree review found `consume_regular_file()` had only a normal retained-byte/hash case and a parent-symlink race case directly testing the read boundary. Added tests for hash-only mode, empty/oversized/missing/directory/FIFO/symlink inputs, file growth after the initial size snapshot, same-size in-place mutation, truncation during read, and a leaf replacement with a symlink between `stat` and `open`. The four race mutations are injected after observing the reader's descriptor open, with a separate writer descriptor where required; all 44 focused stable-file-reader tests pass. Fresh full Python discovery passes 1,339 tests in 21.6 seconds; branch-aware coverage remains 89% overall and stable-file-reader coverage is 81% (173 statements and 82 partial branches remain uncovered). `compileall scripts run_all_training.py`, `bash -n` for all eight shell scripts, and `git diff --check` pass. No production defect was reproduced in this continuation; uncovered filesystem publication branches and the wider repository/app/device/human/release audit remain open.

Current source inventory recheck (2026-10-09): `audit_no_trainable_surface.py` reports `complete=true`, zero findings across 667 scanned source/configuration files, classification `no_retained_trainable_surface`, and no training-execution claim. The checked-in runtime asset verifier accepts 30 assets (29 PNGs and one font) and all 15 required audio resources. These are source/configuration and packaging-input checks; they do not close the separate runtime, device, human, store, signing, or release acceptance work.

Atomic temporary and backup cleanup race continuation (2026-10-09): fault injection reproduced that `atomic_write_bytes()` could blindly unlink a concurrent file swapped into its temporary name after publication; displaced prior-output contents could also be modified through a retained descriptor and then unlinked. Cleanup now atomically detaches temporary and rollback-snapshot names to private quarantine entries, checks inode identity and (for displaced/backup data) content digests, restores and preserves unexpected entries, and reports/retains cleanup failures while still closing held descriptors and processing remaining cleanup. Regressions cover replacement at both names, in-place mutation, denied unlink with displaced-output restoration, and a collision at the restore name with both concurrent entries retained. The implementation initially made three existing failed-publication tests retain staged `.tmp` files because those names still held the new payload before an exchange; an explicit displaced-output state corrected that behavior, and those three regressions now pass. All 50 stable-file-reader tests pass; full Python discovery passes 1,345 tests in 21.7 seconds. Fresh branch-aware coverage is 89% overall and stable-file-reader coverage is 78% (241 statements and 93 partial branches remain uncovered). Python compilation and `git diff --check` pass. The reproduced cleanup races are fixed; remaining rollback/publication branches, unrelated repository code paths, and runtime/device/human/release acceptance remain open.

Multi-file transaction-root cleanup race continuation (2026-10-09): a fault-injection test showed `atomic_publish_files()` removed its transaction directory by name with `rmdir`, without confirming the entry still referred to the created inode. The test failed before the fix when it replaced the empty transaction root immediately before cleanup. Publication now records the held transaction directory's device/inode identity and removes it through `remove_directory_at()`, which quarantines the root name, verifies the moved identity, and restores/refuses a replacement. The targeted transaction test and all 50 stable-file-reader tests pass; the full Python suite passes 1,345 tests with zero failures, and fresh branch-aware coverage remains 89% overall. `compileall scripts run_all_training.py`, `bash -n` for all eight shell scripts, and `git diff --check` pass. This root-name race is addressed; atomic multi-file content cleanup races, remaining filesystem branches, and broader repository/app/device/human/release verification remain open.

Multi-file transaction content-cleanup race continuation (2026-10-09): deterministic hooks reproduced blind deletion of concurrent replacements at `backup-N` and `staged-N` after commit. `atomic_publish_files()` now records staged and backup inode identities and SHA-256 digests; cleanup atomically moves each name to a unique quarantine leaf, verifies regular-file type, inode, and bytes, then removes only the expected entry. Mismatches are restored without clobbering and preserve the transaction for recovery. The existing injected-unlink-failure test now targets the quarantined leaf. All 52 focused stable-file-reader tests pass; full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,347 tests in 90.6 seconds with zero failures. Stable-file-reader coverage is 79% (242 statements and 93 partial branches remain uncovered). `compileall scripts`, `bash -n` for all eight shell scripts, and `git diff --check` pass. Static cleanup races reproduced in this transaction path are addressed; rollback-failure cleanup branches, other uncovered code, and runtime/device/human/release verification remain open.

Multi-file rollback quarantine continuation (2026-10-09): review of rollback showed the same check-then-unlink window remained after exchanging a newly published file out of the destination. Rollback now sends both displaced backup files and recovered new outputs through identity-and-digest-bound quarantine cleanup. A fault injected immediately before the quarantine rename swaps a concurrent entry into the rollback name; the regression verifies the concurrent bytes are restored and the original rollback inode is preserved at its saved name. All 53 focused stable-file-reader tests pass; full Python discovery passes 1,348 tests in 19.8 seconds with zero failures. `compileall scripts` and `git diff --check` pass. Rollback unlink races on these paths are addressed; remaining filesystem rollback branches and the broader app/release/device audit remain open.

Recursive tree child cleanup race continuation (2026-10-09): inspection of every mutation in `remove_directory_at()` found regular, symlink, and other non-directory children were still deleted with a direct `unlink` after a separate listing/stat. A deterministic regression swaps a child for a concurrent file at the quarantine rename boundary; the old path would remove the replacement. Tree cleanup now quarantines each non-directory child, verifies its device/inode identity and non-directory type, then unlinks it; on mismatch or unlink failure it restores without replacing an intervening name. The pre-existing partial-backup cleanup fault test was updated to inject on the quarantined `.remove` leaf. All 54 focused stable-file-reader tests pass. Full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,349 tests in 23.5 seconds with zero failures; stable-file-reader coverage is 79% (246 statements and 93 partial branches remain uncovered). `compileall scripts`, `bash -n` for all eight shell scripts, and `git diff --check` pass. Recursive child deletion race is addressed; directory removal error/race branches, other uncovered code, and full app/device/human/release validation remain open.

Single-file replacement cleanup continuation (2026-10-09): callsite review found `atomic_replace_file()` still directly unlinked the displaced old destination, its rollback snapshot, and deferred backup/recovery entries. These are now removed through inode-and-digest-checked quarantine; deferred cleanup collects errors and closes the held directory descriptor even when cleanup fails. The focused stable-file-reader suite passes 54 tests, and full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,349 tests in 24.4 seconds with zero failures. Stable-file-reader coverage is 78% (264 statements and 96 partial branches remain uncovered); `compileall scripts`, `bash -n` for all eight shell scripts, and `git diff --check` pass. This shared single-file publisher's direct cleanup unlinks are addressed; additional adversarial integration tests and the remaining filesystem/app/device/human/release audit remain open.

Single-file rollback fallback cleanup continuation (2026-10-09): reviewing the remaining direct-unlink inventory found `atomic_write_bytes()` could retry an unguarded rollback-name unlink from `finally` after its primary guarded rollback cleanup failed. That fallback now requires the captured rollback inode and digest and preserves the entry on mismatch; partial snapshot cleanup also uses inode-bound quarantine. The full branch-aware Python suite passes 1,349 tests in 23.1 seconds with zero failures; stable-file-reader coverage is 78% (269 statements and 96 partial branches remain uncovered). `compileall scripts`, shell syntax checks for all eight shell scripts, and `git diff --check` pass. Remaining direct unlink calls are confined to guarded quarantine helpers and the identity-checked removal primitive; the overall repository audit and runtime/device/human/release checks remain open.

Stable release-index verification-failure cleanup race (2026-10-09): low-coverage review of `build_stable_release_evidence_index.py` found its rollback after independent verifier failure used a stat-then-unlink sequence on the published output name. A fault injection replaced the index after the identity check and demonstrated the path could delete the concurrent replacement. Cleanup now binds the published index digest and inode and uses the shared quarantine remover; mismatched replacements are restored and retained. The cleanup-denial test now targets the quarantine leaf, and a new regression verifies a swapped output survives. The focused builder suite passes 16 tests; full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,350 tests in 22.8 seconds with zero failures. Module coverage remains 69% (64 statements and 19 partial branches uncovered); `compileall scripts`, all shell syntax checks, and `git diff --check` pass. Other uncovered validation branches and the full repo/app/device/human/release audit remain open.

Stable release-index snapshot and CLI coverage continuation (2026-10-09): added regressions for a source leaf replaced after open, truncation and growth during snapshot reads, missing inputs, malformed kind/path specifications, a single-string required-kind value, invalid non-iterable required kinds, and both CLI success and domain-error output. Human acceptance compiler branch audit (2026-10-09): the existing human validator tests exercised many schema rules but left the compiler's input-normalization, shape, and CLI paths lightly covered. Added table-driven cases for invalid device/review/evidence shapes, path traversal and missing evidence, malformed timestamps, timestamp generation, `deepcopy` and validation error translation, draft/summary output aliases, and CLI success plus malformed-JSON output. Installed identity matrix compiler snapshot-binding race (2026-10-09): a deterministic mutation between device-manifest validation and the compiler's second read reproduced an uncaught `KeyError` while extracting `candidate`. The compiler now reads bounded device-manifest bytes once, parses and validates those same bytes, derives the manifest digest from them, and relies on final evidence validation to reject subsequent path mutation. Added the mutation regression plus malformed-record/path/timestamp, generated-time, copy/device-error normalization, CLI, and output-alias cases. All 31 focused matrix compiler tests pass; full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,368 tests in 22.8 seconds with zero failures. `compile_installed_identity_matrix.py` coverage is 99% (only the `__main__` exit line remains uncovered). `compileall scripts`, shell syntax checks, and `git diff --check` pass. The broader repository, physical-device, human, and release gates remain open.

Play delivery compiler branch and matrix-drift audit (2026-10-09): added a deterministic mutation between installed-matrix validation and hashing; the compiler's final validator rejects the changed matrix and compilation fails closed. Screenshot capture finalizer race audit continuation (2026-10-09): a fault injection replacing a validated PNG before session publication reproduced acceptance of a capture set whose bytes no longer matched validation. The finalizer now records validated image dimensions/digests and sidecar payloads, re-enumerates and rechecks the capture set before publication, and passes the originally admitted output-directory identity into the atomic writer to reject directory replacement. Regressions cover PNG replacement, an additional capture pair arriving during inspection, and output-directory replacement before atomic publication. All 15 focused finalizer tests pass; full branch-aware Python discovery passes 1,376 tests in 22.2 seconds with zero failures. The finalizer has 76% coverage (42 statements and 16 partial branches remain uncovered); shared stable-file reader has 78%, and the Play compiler remains at 99%. `compileall scripts`, shell syntax checks, and `git diff --check` pass. Remaining finalizer/stable-reader branches and all broader Kotlin/runtime/device/human/release acceptance remain open.

JVM suite runtime-gate recovery continuation (2026-10-09): the first Gradle `testDebugUnitTest` attempt compiled source and tests but Robolectric rejected Android SDK 36 under the system Java 17 runtime, producing 139 harness failures before test methods ran. Android Studio's bundled Java 21 runtime is available; rerunning under that runtime passes all 1,294 tests across 241 suites with zero failures, errors, or skips. `adb` is not installed in this environment, so connected instrumented tests cannot run here. This closes the local JVM suite gate only; on-device behavior, instrumentation, rendering/accessibility review, human acceptance, and release gates remain open.

Android instrumentation compilation continuation (2026-10-09): `assembleDebugAndroidTest` succeeds under the bundled Java 21 runtime, including Kotlin compilation, dexing, packaging, and APK listing for the instrumentation test APK. This proves the instrumentation sources package successfully but does not execute them; the absent `adb`/device still prevents connected execution.

API 36 device execution and emulator recovery continuation (2026-10-09): the system image's first boot under SwiftShader was CPU-bound and system-service enumeration stalled; initial Gradle attempts executed zero tests, and a direct runner attempt timed out before the app process attached. Device logs showed ActivityManager start-process timeouts and multi-second Binder calls without an app exception. A normal reboot did not stabilize service enumeration, so the same AVD/data was restarted with host GPU rendering, four vCPUs, and 4 GiB RAM; the API 36.1 image then exposed all 318 services and both APKs installed. Direct `am instrument` passed 36/36 tests in 251.6 s. The standard `connectedDebugAndroidTest` task then independently passed 36/36 with zero skipped/failed in 251.866 s, producing Gradle's `TEST-Medium_Phone_API_36.1(AVD) - 16-_app-.xml` and per-test logcat/profile reports. Runtime coverage includes accessibility virtual nodes, journal lifecycle, screenshot/hardware flows, 40-sample input latency profiling, opening/ghost/Bloom/all-entity hardware profiles, persistence, gameplay, Bloom, collisions/restart, media publisher failure cleanup, and packaged asset startup validation. This is one emulator image, not physical-device diversity or human visual/accessibility acceptance; repository-wide source/component review and release gates remain open.

Minified release package launch check (2026-10-09): the freshly assembled shrink-enabled release APK was copied byte-for-byte into a temporary APK and signed with the local debug certificate solely to permit emulator installation; it was installed on the API 36.1 AVD and `MainActivity` launched successfully. The app process remained alive after 12 seconds, the release asset guard produced no missing-resource exception, and logcat contained no `FATAL EXCEPTION`/`AndroidRuntime` crash. AAPT2 reports 15 logical `raw` entries retained in the minified resource table despite renamed packaged files. This validates startup/resource lookup for this minified APK on one emulator; it is not a production signing, physical-device, interactive gameplay, visual, accessibility, or release acceptance check. Logcat also emitted repeated system `AppOps` messages about an undeclared empty attribution tag; source search found no app code explicitly using AppOps attribution APIs, so these are untriaged platform/runtime warnings rather than a confirmed app defect. Debug and release lint both complete with zero errors and 84 warnings each; warning inventory includes 47 `UseKtx`, 22 `UnusedResources`, six dependency-update, three discouraged-API, and six other advisories. The no-trainable-surface and source asset audits reported zero findings and accepted 30 runtime assets plus 15 audio resources. `git diff --check` passes. Physical device, real release certificate/signing, Play delivery, human visual/accessibility/gameplay acceptance, and exhaustive full-source/branch review remain open.

Cross-language regression and lint disposition refresh (2026-10-09): reran full Python discovery and all 1,376 tests pass in 23.1 s; `testReleaseUnitTest` succeeds (Gradle reports tasks up-to-date, matching the already-run Java 21 JVM suite), and the emulator release-package startup check remains successful. Reviewed every non-mechanical debug/release lint finding: one synchronous `commit()` repairs malformed preference types during initialization, where its completion semantics are deliberate; `appCategory` is gated at API 26; the landscape orientation advisory is expected for this game and compatibility behavior is explicitly declared; reflective raw-resource lookup is guarded by source/runtime inventories and verified against the minified APK; `GameView` is programmatically constructed; and obsolete API guards/unused color resources are not runtime failures. This is warning triage, not blanket suppression. Python fixture tests emit expected negative-case diagnostics and one Python `zipfile` duplicate-name warning in a malformed-package test fixture; the suite still passes. Fresh `git diff --check` passes. Remaining work includes wider manual source/branch inspection, physical device matrix, human acceptance and production signing/delivery evidence.

Current-source coverage instrumentation and platform graphics continuation (2026-10-09): the repository had no coverage wiring; the existing `app/build/reports/coverage/test/debug/report.xml` was dated 2026-10-07 with zero covered counters from an earlier Java 17 Robolectric harness failure. Enabled AGP 8.13 unit and Android-test coverage for debug. `:app:createDebugUnitTestCoverageReport` completed and reran 1,294 JVM cases; its JaCoCo report shows 19.2% instructions / 15.6% branches. Caution: Robolectric sandbox classes are under-recorded by the unit report (for example `BitmapHelperTest` executes 5 cases but the report says zero class instructions), so this number is not a full JVM execution ledger. Added real-device bitmap pixel/shading validation and Bamboo gap/stalk collision plus Canvas render tests. Both focused Android tests pass individually. The full API 36.1 connected coverage task then passes 38/38 with zero skips/failures and reports 66.8% instructions / 37.8% branches; BitmapHelper reaches 265/324 instructions (14/26 branches) and Bamboo 937/1,054 instructions (48/62 branches). UI/gameplay paths include 38 connected tests, while unexecuted branches remain widespread. These are one AVD plus JVM/robolectric evidence, not complete source or physical-device validation. Official AGP coverage configuration and task names are documented at https://developer.android.com/studio/test/coverage-report. `git diff --check` passes. Next review should correlate uncovered bytecode with methods and account for report-type gaps before claiming a combined coverage figure.

Save-repair fail-closed startup and lifecycle rerun (2026-10-09): source inspection found that `SaveIntegrityManager.repair()` can return `WRITE_FAILED`, while `MainActivity` and `ForestJournalActivity` previously continued into consumers of preferences whose repair had not persisted. Added a shared startup gate that presents an accessible failure state and blocks game/journal initialization and later journal rendering unless repair returns a usable status. Focused JVM gate tests pass; debug and release JVM suites each pass 1,296 tests in 242 suites, and the shrink-enabled release build completes. A debug-certificate-signed copy of the minified APK was installed for runtime inspection only: on API 36.1 it launched and remained alive for 12 seconds, all 15 raw resources were retained, and no crash/resource-validator failure appeared in logcat. Re-ran the full `:app:connectedDebugAndroidTest` after the lifecycle test cleanup adjustment: 38/38 passed, zero skips/failures, in 4m50s. The singleTask test verifies same-Activity reuse and scenario replacement, but it only verifies that `finish()` sets `isFinishing`; it does not independently observe Activity destruction. Prior destruction waits timed out in this instrumentation setup, and the runner later force-stopped the debug package, so teardown timing remains unproven. The full connected suite is one emulator image; current coverage remains 66.8% instructions / 37.9% branches and does not establish exhaustive source/branch, physical-device, visual, human accessibility, production signing, or release acceptance. `git diff --check` passes; overall repo audit remains active.
